  - Screen six tests them all: after the third ofrenda (153), a 45° ramp (156 and 157) leads up to a bridge of five crumbling tiles (158 to 162, top y = 192) over five spikes, and a 45° ramp leads down (163 and 164); then the last lift (166 to 168), like the first, carries Poncho up the last cliff (columns 169 to 179, top y = 128), where a Tlacuache walks from the cliff's edge to the level's right edge, past the exit at 174.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) lies on a 2-tile ledge over the shuttle's path (columns 107 and 108, row 9, top y = 144), 48 px above the shuttle's top. A full jump to the right from the shuttle lands on the ledge and takes the veladora, and riding the shuttle under the ledge (his head passes flush under it) does not touch it (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). The spikes are below, so it is a risk. It is in plain view (Open Question 15).
- **Ofrendas** at columns 45, 98 and 153, about 25%, 55% and 85% of the width (25.3%, 54.7% and 85.3%). No ofrenda lies between a key and its door: a lost life puts every key back and shuts every door (GDD-WLD-05), so Poncho, back at an ofrenda, never has to go back for a key (checked by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). Every enemy starts at least 3 tiles from the start and from each ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01, checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04), and neither the Zopilote nor a Tlacuache spots Poncho standing at the start or at an ofrenda (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Doors, keys and fragile walls (GDD-WLD-05):** each door and each fragile wall is four tiles tall and stands on flat floor. Without a key no jump from the floor right before a door gets Poncho past it, and the corridor's ceiling, from which a jump lands on the top of the door at 140, is out of reach (above; measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). A claw swipe from the floor right before a fragile wall breaks its lower two tiles, a hole two tiles high that he walks through (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). Each key lies before its door.
- **Moving platforms and blocks (GDD-WLD-05):** a jump from under a lift at its low end lands Poncho on it as it rises into his falling feet (a rising platform catches him, GDD-WLD-05), and holding right then puts him on its cliff's top as it arrives there; from the step Poncho walks onto the shuttle at its first end, and riding it to its far end and holding right puts him on the far wall (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). A crusher crushes Poncho standing under it as it comes down (97 steps after they start for the ones at columns 64 and 120, 49 steps after they leave their top for the corridor's) and never one standing beside it, on the floor or, at column 120, on the Alacrán pen's block; between the corridor's two crushers, at the key, he is safe (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). No platform's or crusher's path crosses a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09; measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02):** one cactus buddy (column 22), three Tlacuaches (on the dune from 72, behind the third fragile wall from 145, on the last cliff from 177), one Zopilote (cruising at y = 112 from 108) and one Alacrán (from 117, behind the second fragile wall). The Zopilote spots Poncho waiting on the step for the shuttle, where his hurtbox centre is 64 px below its cruise line (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). While Poncho is away each walker keeps to its stretch: the dune's Tlacuache from x = 1096 to 1272, over the dune and the floor round it, the Alacrán from 1863 to 1897, in its pen, the Tlacuache behind the third fragile wall from 2312 to 2344, and the one on the last cliff from 2712 to 2872 (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **The enemies' own walks keep clear of the moving blocks** (the main session's choice, 2026-11-02, Proposed: an Alacrán walking through a solid stone block looks wrong; narrowed to the enemies' walks as placed by the user's "Accept both", 2026-11-02): enemies pass through moving blocks (GDD-WLD-05), so 1-3 places them where none walks or flies through a crusher's column. Each of its six enemies, while Poncho is away (every door shut and every fragile wall whole), keeps clear of all four crushers' columns, and so does the Alacrán once its fragile wall is broken (it then walks out as far as the far wall and back to its pen's block, from x = 1831 to 1897; measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). The Tlacuache let out of the third pen is the exception (below).
- **Known and accepted (user, 2026-11-02: "Accept both"; kept as known quirks, their details Proposed):**
  - *A second way into the Alacrán's pen, past a fragile-wall lesson.* The second fragile wall stands one floor column past the shuttle's far wall, whose top is two tiles below the fragile wall's, so from the far wall's right end a full jump clears the fragile wall and lands in the pen (at x = 1884.6, 39 steps after the jump), the wall still whole: a second way in beside the claw from the floor. The Alacrán stays penned that way, since the wall stays (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
  - *The Tlacuache let out of the third pen.* If Poncho opens the door at column 140, breaks the third fragile wall (column 143) and moves on leaving its Tlacuache alive, the Tlacuache walks out left through the open door, through the corridor and its two crushers and into the column of the crusher at 120, as far as the block of the Alacrán's pen (x = 1928): the one enemy whose walk then crosses moving blocks. It takes a player who skips it, and Poncho is past it by then (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Piñata (GDD-PWR-05):** in Base a jump from under it opens it, the taco drops to the floor, and holding right once it has dropped catches it. In Taco or Fuego a chile comes out instead and stays on its top, where a claw from the right end of the 2-tile wall opens it and a hop from there takes the chile, as in 1-1 (measured in Base and in Taco by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the floor at the start, over the hill, either side of the first key, over the cactus buddy and the first gap, either side of the first fragile wall, on the 2-tile wall, past the piñata, up the first lift and on the first cliff, under the first crusher, over the pen's block and on the dune, past the second door and over the second gap, before the second ofrenda, on the step, along the shuttle's path, one on the veladora ledge, on the far wall and before the second fragile wall, in the Alacrán's pen and past its crusher, before the corridor, along it and either side of its key, past the third door, in the third pen and over its wall, up the ramp and over the crumbling bridge, up the last lift and on the last cliff. Each is in an empty tile within a jump of the ground (a level tile or a sand slope) in its column or within two columns of it, and none is touched by Poncho standing at the start or at an ofrenda (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Hilos in play:** holding right from the start, with no jump, collects the first three hilos ahead of him, one on the floor and two on the hill, by the 60th step (1 s) (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Played through:** from a fresh start with seven lives, holding right, jumping at each wall, gap, spike, cactus buddy and closed piñata, clawing each fragile wall and each Alacrán, Tlacuache or Zopilote just ahead, waiting for each lift and the shuttle and riding them, and waiting before each crusher until it rises clear, reaches the exit losing no life on the way, in under 2400 steps (40 s), having used all three keys and collected 45 of the 50 hilos; after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start it still reaches the exit losing none (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- **Plants (GDD-ART-06):** 16, of six kinds (no palm yucca), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one, and none in a crusher's column (the plant rule does not look at moving blocks, so the layout keeps them apart; checked by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`).
- Drawn with World 1's backdrop, ground and slope tiles (GDD-ART-06, GDD-MOV-09); its other things keep their placeholder art until theirs is drawn. The look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-03: "Approved"; `docs/screenshots/level1-3/`).

---

### GDD-WLD-10 Toldos (bouncy awnings)

Status: Decided (the hold ladder, user, 2026-11-04: "Hold ladder": hold jump and each bounce climbs higher, let go and it settles). The numbers and the details are Proposed. Built (`src/sim/awning.ts`, tested in `src/sim/awning.test.ts` and `e2e/awning.spec.ts`, and in every World 2 level's tests); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`).

World 2's first signature element (GDD-WLD-02): the *lonas* stretched flat over the stalls of a tianguis, flat so the shape on screen is the shape that collides. An awning is a market tarp that gives back Poncho's fall: **hold jump and each bounce climbs higher; let go and it settles under his feet.** World 1's sand slopes enrich running (GDD-MOV-09); the awnings enrich jumping, with no new button. The spring (GDD-WLD-05) stays the timing toy: a timed press, a fixed launch, and no standing on it. The awning is a rhythm toy: a hold, a ladder of heights, and a floor once he lets go.

- **Shape:** a flat, horizontal one-way surface, a whole number of tiles wide (2 to 6), its top at a multiple of 8 px, set in empty tiles over a stall, a pit or the floor. It is solid only from above, exactly like the top of a moving platform (GDD-WLD-05) that does not move: Poncho lands on it when his feet cross its top during a step while he falls and his body box overlaps its width by any amount, at its very edge too (a cat lands on its feet); he passes through it from below and from the sides, so he walks under it and jumps up through it. Its poles and frame are scenery: nothing touches them. The level validator counts its cloth 8 px thick (`AWNING.height`, GDD-TEC-04).
- **Plain rebound:** a landing with jump neither held nor pressed in the jump buffer throws him back up at half his landing speed (`AWNING.plainFactor` 0.5). If that is under 150 px/s (`AWNING.settleSpeed`) he does not rebound: he stands on it as on any floor, on the ground, with coyote time, the jump buffer and the normal 410 px/s jump (GDD-MOV-02, GDD-MOV-03). Every fall is capped at 360 px/s (GDD-MOV-04), so a plain rebound is at most 180 px/s. Measured (`src/sim/awning.test.ts`): a fall of 1 tile lands too slowly to rebound and he stands on it at once; a fall of 2 tiles or more lands at 360 px/s and rebounds once at 180 px/s, a hop of about 11 px, and then he stands: at most one small hop. Standing on it, he jumps the ordinary jump, about 56 px.
- **Held rebound, the ladder:** a landing with jump held, or pressed in the 0.1 s jump buffer (GDD-MOV-03; the same "held or buffered" test as the stomp's high bounce, GDD-CMB-01), throws him up at the next tier: 480, 540 and 600 px/s (`AWNING.tiers`; the first is the spring's plain launch and the last its timed one, GDD-WLD-05), so his feet rise 77.1, 97.3 and 120.2 px above the awning (measured on 2-1's first awning by `src/levels/level2-1.test.ts`, and within a few px by `src/sim/awning.test.ts`). The tier is the count of held rebounds in a row, capped at 3. "Held" is jump held from a press the simulation saw, as the glide reads it (GDD-MOV-07): a jump held through a respawn (GDD-LIFE-02), or pressed while paused and held through the resume (GDD-UI-02), does not count until it is pressed again; a press in the buffer counts even if let go before the landing (the main session's reading, 2026-11-04, Proposed).
- **The chain:** the count carries on from one awning to the next. It goes back to 0 on any landing that is not a held rebound: a plain rebound, settling, a landing on a level tile, a sand slope, a moving platform, a cordel (GDD-WLD-11) or a spring, and on a lost life. A stomp in mid-air (GDD-CMB-01) is not a landing and keeps it, and so is a bump on La Estrella (GDD-BOS-03). A landing on a cordel and on an awning in the same step is a landing on the cordel. A jump from a settled awning is an ordinary 410 px/s jump, and landing back on it holding jump is tier 1: from standing, four beats of 56.4, 77.1, 97.3 and 120.2 px (measured by `src/levels/level2-1.test.ts`), "hop, boing, boing, BOING".
- **A late press:** a jump pressed in the 0.1 s (6 steps, `AWNING.lateSteps`, as `FORGIVENESS.bufferSteps`) after the landing step of a plain rebound or of settling counts as held at that landing: he leaves from that step at the tier that landing would have given held, from the awning's top if he stands on it, else from where he is in the air, and the rebound's sound plays; the 7th step is too late, and from standing that press is the ordinary jump. One late press per landing; a landing on anything else ends it. A rebound leaves no coyote time, so a jump pressed on the way up never replaces it. Where the wall cling is on (GDD-MOV-08), a late press beside a wall is a wall kick instead. A child who presses a hair late still gets the bounce. (The details are the main session's, 2026-11-04, Proposed; measured by `src/sim/awning.test.ts`.)
- **Momentum:** a rebound never changes his speed across, and he steers in the air as in any jump (GDD-MOV-01). Running along a row of awnings holding jump is a fast, flowing hop across the stalls.
- **Letting go cuts it:** releasing jump while he rises from a rebound cuts the rise like a jump's (×0.4, GDD-MOV-02), unlike a spring's fixed launch. So "let go to come down" works the same way everywhere: he falls back, rebounds plainly and settles.
- **Head bumps:** a rebound into a ceiling or a piñata bumps it like any jump, and his rise stops there: a piñata over an awning opens (GDD-PWR-05), and a bump is how he hits La Estrella (GDD-BOS-03).
- **Torn awning (toldo roto):** a faded, patched tarp that bounces him once. It works as a whole awning for one landing: a held landing gives the next tier as usual and the chain goes on, and a plain landing always rebounds, at 180 px/s or more, never letting him stand. Any landing tears it: from the next step it is torn and gone (its two halves hang from the frame, scenery) until a life is lost, when every torn awning is whole again, like the crumbling tiles (GDD-WLD-05, GDD-LIFE-02). A Chapulín's bounce tears it too (GDD-ENM-03). Its plain rebound is at least `AWNING.tornMinSpeed` (180 px/s), even from a 1-tile fall. It is the twist of 2-3 (GDD-WLD-14).
- **Edge cases:** a landing on an awning and on a level tile in the same step (an awning flush against a ledge at its height): the tile wins, no rebound; a level keeps an awning's end at least 1 tile from a level tile whose top is at its height, so the edge always reads. A stomp and an awning landing in the same step: the stomp wins (its bounce, no rebound, the chain kept, and a torn awning under the stomped enemy is not torn by that step, so it can still rebound him on the way down; checked by `src/sim/awning.test.ts`). No spring, sand slope, crumbling tile, door or piñata sits in an awning's tiles, and moving platforms and moving blocks keep their paths clear of awnings (World 2's levels have no spring, slope, crumbling tile, door, platform or block, and the level validator fails an awning that overlaps a level tile, GDD-TEC-04). The camera does not move up (GDD-UI-04), so an awning a tier 3 rebound can be taken from has its top no higher than y = 160 (`AWNING.minTopY`), which keeps his head on the screen (the level validator checks it, GDD-TEC-04).
- **Others on an awning:** the walking enemies (the Alacrán, the Tlacuache, the Paloma and the Trompo) and the flyers ignore awnings, and a level never lets a walk cross one (each World 2 level's tests check it); the Chapulín is the one enemy that bounces on them (GDD-ENM-03), and La Estrella is thrown back up by one in her phases 1 and 2 and tears through them in phase 3 (GDD-BOS-03). A walking taco or a chile passes through an awning, and a level keeps a piñata whose item would fall onto one away from it (in 2-1 the taco of the piñata over the first awning falls past the awning's end, `src/levels/level2-1.test.ts`). Fireballs pass through awnings (they bounce on level tiles and slopes only), and so does the claw (a flutter of the cloth is not drawn yet).
- **The glide (GDD-MOV-07):** off in World 2's levels (GDD-PRG-01). Where it is on, a glide ends on landing as now, so a held fall onto an awning is a held rebound. The awning reads held jump, which the jump buffer, coyote time, the variable jump, the stomp's high bounce and the glide read too; each has a test with awnings in it (`src/sim/awning.test.ts`, L-024).
- **Look (built, GDD-ART-06; approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`; details Proposed):** drawn from `awning.png`: a flat canvas of rosa mexicano and white stripes 4 px wide over a scalloped valance, 8 px deep in all, outlined in near-black like everything Poncho can touch (GDD-ART-05). At rest it is flat, and it does not dip while he stands on it; after a rebound its middle sags, the ends fixed, 2 px for a plain rebound and 2, 4 or 6 px for tiers 1, 2 and 3 (`AWNING.sagPx`), for 4 steps (`sagSteps`), then springs 1 px above rest for 4 steps (`liftPx`, `liftSteps`; `src/ui/marketLayout.test.ts`). The torn awning is faded to the plaster's pink with two square patches, teal and yellow, and a valance with gaps, and once gone it is drawn as its two halves hanging at its ends. Stripes with scallops mean "bounce" and nothing else in World 2 is striped canvas, so shape and motion tell it, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). **Sound (GDD-AUD-02, Proposed):** a held rebound plays the jump sound raised 0, 2 and 4 semitones for tiers 1, 2 and 3 (`AWNING.reboundSemitones`), a late press's rebound too, and a plain rebound plays the land sound (`e2e/awning.spec.ts`). A column of hilos above an awning at the tier heights is the wordless lesson "this goes higher" (GDD-WLD-04; 2-1's first awning, GDD-WLD-12).
- `AWNING` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `tiers` [480, 540, 600], `plainFactor` 0.5, `settleSpeed` 150, `tornMinSpeed` 180, `lateSteps` 6, `height` 8, `minTopY` 160, `sagPx` [2, 2, 4, 6] (a plain rebound, then tiers 1 to 3), `sagSteps` 4, `liftPx` 1, `liftSteps` 4, `reboundSemitones` [0, 2, 4]. `src/sim/awning.ts`, the drawing in `src/ui/marketLayout.ts` and `src/ui/MarketThings.ts`.

### GDD-WLD-11 Papel picado: cordeles and rollos

Status: Proposed (what papel picado does is Open Question 23). Built (`src/sim/papel.ts`, tested in `src/sim/papel.test.ts`, `src/sim/world2run.test.ts` and `e2e/awning.spec.ts`, and in every World 2 level's tests); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`). The claw batting a bundle under Poncho's own body (below) is the main session's choice during the build (2026-11-04), for the user to confirm.

World 2's second signature element (GDD-WLD-02): papel picado strung across the market as **cordeles** that Poncho stands on and jumps up through; some are still **rolled up on their post**, and a swipe of the claw unrolls the bundle into a bridge: a cat batting a ball of string, building the fiesta as he goes. 1-3 taught that the claw changes papel picado (fragile walls, GDD-WLD-05), so a bundle invites a swipe without a word.

- **Cordel:** a straight horizontal line between two posts (a pole, a rooftop's edge, a wall), a whole number of tiles long, its top at a multiple of 8 px, with a row of paper flags hanging under it. Only the line collides; the flags are paper and nothing touches them.
- **A one-way floor:** a cordel is the top of a moving platform (GDD-WLD-05) that does not move: Poncho lands on it from above, stands, walks and jumps from it as on the ground, jumps up through it from below and walks off its ends. It never bounces (that is the awning's job), even with jump held, never crumbles and never moves. A landing on it ends an awning chain, and wins over a landing on an awning in the same step (GDD-WLD-10). Only its 2 px line counts for the level validator (`ROLLO.linePx`, GDD-TEC-04).
- **Stacking:** cordeles may be stacked at least 3 tiles (48 px) apart, so a full jump (about 56 px, GDD-MOV-02) goes up through one and lands on the next: a papel picado staircase in an aisle. A cordel over an awning has at least 2 tiles of clear air between them, so a rebound visibly passes through it, and no cordel runs over a sand slope or a spring.
- **Rollo:** a cordel that starts rolled up, a round bundle 12 x 12 px (`ROLLO.size`) tied at the post at one of its ends, a short loose end hanging. A level gives it as its line (its left end, top and length) and its post, `left` or `right`; the bundle's box hangs from the line's top at the post's end, so the line is at least 8 px above the ground Poncho claws the bundle from, and he hops up onto it: "claw, then hop" (Open Question 30). Rolled up there is nothing to stand on. A claw swipe whose box overlaps the bundle (GDD-CMB-02), or a fireball that touches it (GDD-PWR-03, used up), unrolls it, on the swipe's first step. So does a swipe while Poncho's own body box overlaps the bundle, whichever way he faces, so a bundle tied flush against a wall he stands against is batted too (the main session's choice, 2026-11-04, Proposed; his body alone, with no swipe, does nothing; `src/sim/world2run.test.ts`). The cordel grows from that post from the next step at 160 px/s (`ROLLO.unrollSpeed`, 8/3 px a step) to its full length and stays strung, and while it grows only the strung part is there. A claw or a fireball on a rollo already unrolling or strung changes nothing. 160 px/s is faster than his 120 px/s run (GDD-MOV-01), so a Poncho who claws it from beside the post and runs on, hopping onto it, is always behind its front. A cordel 6 tiles long is strung in 36 steps, 0.6 s (measured by `src/sim/papel.test.ts`). A jump pressed during the claw's 2-step wind-up lifts the swing over a bundle at his feet, so the claw and the hop are two beats (Open Question 31).
- **Reset:** a strung rollo stays strung until a life is lost; then every rollo is rolled up again, like the fragile walls, doors and piñatas (GDD-LIFE-02). A level puts a rollo between its respawn ofrenda and the pit it bridges (as GDD-WLD-09 keeps keys before their doors), so Poncho never respawns on the wrong side of a rolled-up bridge (checked for 2-2 and 2-3 by their level tests).
- **The far post:** a rollo can sit at either end of its line. On the far post it unrolls towards Poncho: the twist of 2-3, where the far bundles over its alley and its last pit are reached by a claw on the rise of an awning rebound, and the one over its Fuego pit only by a fireball (GDD-WLD-14).
- **Edge cases:** the claw's box is level with his body, so a swipe while he stands on a cordel never touches the line under his feet; the claw passes through cordeles (a flutter of the flags is not drawn yet), and only bundles react. One swipe that overlaps a bundle and an enemy hits both, as it does piñatas and fragile walls. The Paloma walks on cordeles and on the strung part of rollos (GDD-ENM-03); no other enemy does, and a Chapulín's hop passes through them. Items and fireballs pass through cordeles and strung rollos.
- **Look (built, GDD-ART-06; approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`; details Proposed):** drawn from `cordel.png` and `rollo.png`: a dark line 2 px thick, iron twisted with wood, and under it two paper flags a tile, 6 x 8 px, one every 8 px, in the pink, gold, teal and blue of World 2's mood line (GDD-ART-06), cut out so the sky shows through, with a zigzag bottom edge and the near-black outline; the line and the posts have none, so the line reads as a line. The flags sway 1 px right and left on a 2 s sine, each tile out of step with the next. A rollo is a fat roll of the same paper, outlined, that sways 1 px for half of every second as an idle hint and flaps open at the front of the line while it unrolls (`src/ui/marketLayout.test.ts`). Not built yet: a paper "fwip" as it unrolls (GDD-AUD-02, Proposed).
- **Set aside (Proposed):** hanging and swinging from strings (World 4's vines own that), the claw cutting a cordel (a swipe at an enemy would cut the next bridge), cordeles that roll back on a timer (World 6's timed platforms) and slanted cordeles to slide down.
- `ROLLO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `size` 12, `unrollSpeed` 160, `linePx` 2. A cordel lands Poncho as a moving platform does (`PLATFORM`). `src/sim/papel.ts`, the drawing in `src/ui/marketLayout.ts` and `src/ui/MarketThings.ts`.

### GDD-WLD-12 Level 2-1

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: World 2's levels in "One World 2 PR"; 8 screens, "8 screens", for a perfect run of about 45 s). The layout is Proposed, and so is the pit awning's width, the main session's choice during the build (2026-11-04: 3 tiles over an 8-tile pit, below; the beat had planned 2 tiles with 3 open on each side), and the new Shō beat of the 8-screen layout (the drop and the rung, below), the main session's choice (2026-11-04).

- **Place in the game:** World 2's first level (GDD-WLD-01), "El Tianguis", a street market under pink and white tarps (the name Proposed, Open Question 26). Picking "Next level" on the boss arena 1-4's results menu starts it with seven lives, a new world (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02), and once 1-4 is cleared "Start" plays World 2's run, which starts here, always with seven (user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world"). "Retry" on its results menu starts it again with the lives at its exit, its "Next level" starts 2-2 with them (GDD-WLD-13), and `?level=2-1` opens it with seven lives. Out of lives in any World 2 level, the restart is here, with seven (GDD-LIFE-05). (Checked by `src/levels/levels.test.ts` and `e2e/world2.spec.ts`.) World 2's rules: gaps of at most 4 tiles, with the covered-pit rule (GDD-WLD-03). It has no tips (GDD-UI-05) and no placeholder lines. The glide and the wall cling are off (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level2-1.ts`.
- **What it teaches:** the awning (GDD-WLD-10) and the Paloma (GDD-ENM-03), ending with the first cordeles (GDD-WLD-11). It is meant to be easier than 1-3, so the step into World 2 is a new toy, not a wall (playtesting will tell). It has no 4-tile open gap and no Tlacuache.
- **Size:** 240 x 17 tiles (8 screens, 3840 x 272 px), the longest GDD-WLD-03 allows in World 2. The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224), and Poncho starts on it at tile column 3. The exit stands at column 234 on the exit's rooftop (columns 229 to 239, top y = 80): with the camera at the right edge, 3360 px in, it is on screen from 384 to 400 px, left of the phone's Jump button, as in 1-1 and 1-3.
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03; every "measured" by `src/levels/level2-1.test.ts`).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 46: review, then the awning, with no way to fail.* An Alacrán (starting at column 14) penned by two crates 1 tile high (10 and 18), a 3-tile gap (22 to 24), then the first awning, 4 tiles wide (31 to 34), alone over flat floor, its top at y = 192, 32 px up, so a jump from the floor lands on it. A jump onto it, let go, lands with one small hop (a plain rebound) and settles 43 steps after the takeoff; held, the jump climbs the hold ladder in place: his feet 56.4 px over the awning for the jump, then 77.1, 97.3 and 120.2 for tiers 1, 2 and 3 (measured). Over its column 32 a column of four hilos (rows 6, 5, 4 and 3) gives one hilo per beat and never the next one: the jump reaches the lowest, each tier one more ("this goes higher"). A piñata (column 34, row 5) over its right end opens to a first-tier rebound, 47 steps after the press, and not to a jump from the awning; its taco walks off the piñata's right side and falls past the awning's end to the floor, never over the cloth (measured; GDD-WLD-10, GDD-PWR-05).
  - *Shō, columns 47 to 153: the awning develops; the Paloma arrives, then the two meet.* In five phrases with rests between them.
    - *The Paloma alone.* The first Paloma (from column 52) alone on flat floor, penned by two crates 2 tiles high (48 and 57): standing on a crate Poncho is 32 px above it, out of its sight (24 px), so he can look down into the pen before he commits. It can be clawed in its telegraph, stomped, or walked under while it hovers: from inside the pen, waiting for its hover, walking under it and hopping the far crate costs nothing (measured). The first ofrenda (60) follows on the floor, the rest before the stalls.
    - *The chain.* The stall row, three awnings 3 tiles wide at y = 192 (64 to 66, 69 to 71, 74 to 76), 2 tiles apart: holding jump and right from the floor rebounds off them at tiers 1, 2 and 3 in turn, a fast, flowing hop across the stalls. The ladder awning (79 to 81, y = 192) stands against the wall of the first rooftop (82 to 91, top y = 112, 80 px above it, a wall 7 tiles high): from anywhere on it, holding right, a first-tier rebound peaks 2.9 px under the rooftop's top and falls back, and a second tier lands on it (measured; Open Question 28), so the chain is needed, and holding right against the wall is enough. The stall row's tier 3 flows into the ladder awning against the wall, a fourth rebound, and onto the rooftop 203 steps after the first jump from the floor a column before the first stall (measured). On the rooftop a chimney 2 tiles high (86) pens the second Paloma (from 89) on the roof's right part, out of sight of a Poncho who has just landed. Off the rooftop's right end, an alley 6 tiles wide (92 to 97) with a floor: walking off the rooftop lands on the alley awning (93 to 97, y = 176), set against the wall of the second rooftop (98 to 110, top y = 112); a first-tier rebound from it lands on that rooftop, a plain jump from it does not, and from the alley floor a jump lands back on the awning, so the alley never traps him (measured).
    - *The drop: a Paloma met from above.* The second rooftop is a long roof, a rest, whose right end looks down on the third Paloma (from 115), penned on the floor below between the rooftop's wall and a crate 2 tiles high (119; it keeps to x = 1783 to 1897). Standing anywhere on the roof's right part he is 112 px above it and it never sees him (measured), so he can watch it walk before he steps off. Walking off the edge (from x 1770, right held, jump up) he lands 55 px out from the wall (x 1831), 35 steps later. Measured over 72 waits at the edge, one every 1/6 s over 11.8 s (more than its round of the pen, 11.4 s): stepping off while it walks back towards the wall from x 1850 to 1830, or out from it from 1809 to 1829, he comes down on it, a stomp, 34 steps after stepping off (14 of 72); stepping off as it is at x 1853 walking back, he lands against its side, a hit, as any stomp just missed (1 of 72); otherwise he lands beside it, where it is telegraphing or too far to see him (57 of 72). Landing behind it as it walks away, 28 px from it, it telegraphs and a claw in its telegraph defeats it, and he walks on and hops its crate with no hit (measured). It is the first Paloma met from high above, and the first stomp offered as the easy answer; it sets up the stomp from the crate at the pit (the Ten).
    - *Rest.* The street past the crate, with the second ofrenda (126) on the floor, which every route crosses.
    - *The rung: the awning and the Paloma together.* The take-off awning (129 to 131, y = 192) stands alone on the street before the fourth Paloma's pen, a crate 2 tiles high (133) and a crate 1 tile high (139) with the Paloma (from 136) between them on the floor (it keeps to x = 2151 to 2217). The landing awning (139 to 141, y = 192) is over the 1-tile crate, 16 px of air between them, and against the wall of the third rooftop (142 to 153, top y = 112), 80 px above it like the ladder awning: from anywhere on it, holding right, tier 1 peaks 2.9 px short and tier 2 lands on the rooftop (measured). The Paloma's walk ends at the 1-tile crate, its box's side exactly at the landing awning's end, never under it. Standing anywhere on the take-off awning Poncho is out of its sight (measured). From the ofrenda, running and pressing jump past x 2040, held, he lands on the take-off awning and its tier 1 rebound throws him over the crate into the pen. If the Paloma is under him there (after a 2 s wait at the ofrenda, measured), he stomps it, 84 steps after he sets off, the stomp keeps the chain (GDD-WLD-10, GDD-CMB-01), and held through it the stomp's high bounce carries him onto the landing awning, whose rebound is then tier 2, onto the rooftop: the Paloma is a rung of the ladder. Let go from the stomp on, its low bounce comes down in the pen, the chain lost; and with the Paloma gone the take-off rebound lands in the pen too. From the pen a jump beside the 1-tile crate goes up through the landing awning and lands on it held: tier 1, then tier 2 onto the rooftop, the ladder awning's lesson again, so the rung is a reward and never a lock (measured). Measured over a whole round of the Paloma (28 take-offs, after waits of 0 to 6.75 s in steps of 0.25 s; its round of the pen is 6.6 s): 10 land on it and climb the rung to the rooftop; 16 land beside it with no hit and climb to the rooftop (a claw takes it when it is ahead of him, otherwise it is behind him); 2 land against its side, a hit, as any stomp just missed. The third rooftop is the rest, and from its right end he sees the pit ahead.
  - *Ten, columns 154 to 173: the twist, a bounce he must take.* Off the third rooftop, a crate 2 tiles high (161) pens the fifth Paloma (from 164) on the floor at the edge of an 8-tile pit (166 to 173). The pit awning, 3 tiles (169 to 171, y = 208, 16 px above the floor), leaves 3 open tiles before it and 2 after. No jump crosses the pit without it (measured with it taken away). The running jumps that reach it (pressed from x = 2636 on) land on its first 20 px; held, the rebound is tier 1 and every one of them carries him over the 2 tiles past it. Not held, with right held throughout, the plain rebound's small hop (16 steps) carries him on across the cloth and he comes down on it, his body 9 px clear of its far end (x = 2705, then 2737); only right held after that, standing, walks him off, as off any ledge. Let go and settled, a jump from it reaches the far floor. With the far floor a column further (a 9-tile pit, 3 open tiles each side) the tier 1 rebound from the awning's first 20 px falls short for every jump pressed before x = 2648, all but those in the last 8 px before the edge: so the 8-tile pit (measured). The Paloma at the edge is the twist: sprinting at it is a hit (it telegraphs at once, and running on into it is a hit on the 14th step); waiting for its hover, walking under it to the edge and jumping past it lands on the pit awning; and from the crate's top a jump onto it stomps it, and with jump held through the stomp (the high bounce) it is a stepping stone onto the pit awning (measured; Open Question 28). The drop and the rung have shown the stomp from above and the stomp held as a bounce; here the bounce is over a pit.
  - *Ketsu, columns 174 to 239: the conclusion.* Rest on the floor past the pit, then a cordel staircase over safe floor: the lower cordel (184 to 187, y = 176, 48 px over the floor) and the upper (187 to 190, y = 128, 48 px higher), sharing column 187. A full jump from the floor lands on the lower, from it on the upper, and from the upper on the tall rooftop (192 to 201, top y = 80, a wall 9 tiles high): no jump from the floor or from the lower cordel reaches it (measured). Off its right end, the floor to the third ofrenda (205), then the last stall row (216 to 218, 221 to 223, 226 to 228, y = 192), the last against the wall of the exit's rooftop (112 px over the stall): from anywhere on the last stall, holding right, a second tier peaks 14.7 px under the rooftop's top and a third lands on it (measured). Holding jump across the three stalls climbs it in one flowing chain, tier 1, 2, 3.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) hangs high over the pit awning's middle (column 170, row 3), in plain view (Open Question 15). Three held rebounds in place on the pit awning reach it at the top of the third and not before, and a column of four hilos under it (rows 7 to 4) gives one per beat, as over the first awning (measured). It is the risky route: three bounces on a 48 px cloth with the pit below.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 60, 126 and 205, about 25%, 53% and 86% of the width (25.2%, 52.7% and 85.6%), all on the floor: the first past the first Paloma's pen, before the stall row; the second on the street between the drop and the rung, which every route crosses; the third at the foot of the tall rooftop.
- **Gaps (GDD-WLD-03):** a 3-tile gap (22 to 24) and the 8-tile pit (166 to 173), which the pit awning leaves open 3 tiles before it and 2 after; the validator with World 2's rules passes, and with the pit awning taken away it reports the pit as an 8-tile gap (measured). No open gap is wider than World 1's 3: the step into World 2 is the awning, not a longer jump.
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02, GDD-ENM-03):** one Alacrán and five Palomas, none starting on an awning. Each walks its pen and never under or across an awning: while Poncho waits at the start, the Alacrán keeps to x = 183 to 281 and the Palomas to 791 to 905, 1399 to 1465 on the first rooftop, 1783 to 1897 under the second rooftop, 2151 to 2217 in the rung pen (its side reaching the landing awning's end, never under it) and 2599 to 2649 at the pit's edge (measured over 4000 steps). None starts within 3 tiles of a respawn (the level validator), and none is startled by Poncho standing at the start or at an ofrenda (measured).
- **Awnings (GDD-WLD-10):** twelve, 2 to 6 tiles wide, tops at multiples of 8 and no higher than y = 160, each at least 1 tile from a level tile whose top is at its height; the ladder awning, the alley awning, the rung's landing awning and the last stall stand against a rooftop's wall, and the landing awning is over a crate 1 tile high (measured). No cordel has an awning under it or within 2 tiles beside it. There are no sand slopes, springs, crumbling tiles, doors, keys, fragile walls, moving platforms or blocks.
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the floor at the start, over the Alacrán's crates and the gap, the column over the first awning, in and over the first Paloma's pen, rising arcs over the stall row, a column against the first rooftop's wall over the ladder awning (the jump, tier 1, tier 2), over the chimney and in the rooftop pen, over the alley, on the second rooftop and on the drop from its right end, over the drop Paloma's crate, by the second ofrenda, over the take-off awning's arc, at the top of the rung's tier 2 by the third rooftop's edge, on the third rooftop and the drop from it, over the pit Paloma's crate and in its pen, the arc and the column over the pit awning, past the pit, by the cordel staircase, on the tall rooftop, and rising arcs over the last stall row onto the exit's rooftop. None is in the HUD's top rows (all at row 3 or lower), none is touched by Poncho standing at the start or an ofrenda (measured). The playthrough below collects 44; holding jump in place on the first awning and on the pit awning collects the other 6 (measured).
- **Played through:** a bot that holds right, jumps at walls, the gap, the Alacrán, awnings and cordeles ahead, holds jump over awnings, waits out of a penned Paloma's sight until it is far from the wall, then jumps in and claws it, and at a rooftop's edge over a Paloma waits until the Paloma is at least 64 px from the drop, reaches the exit in 2791 steps (46.5 s) with all seven lives, every ofrenda and the five Palomas clawed, and clears it too after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start (measured). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- Drawn with World 2's art (GDD-ART-06), with 20 decor items of all seven kinds (measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`).
- **The frame of every World 2 level, 2-1 to 2-3 (Proposed):** up to 240 x 17 tiles (8 screens, GDD-WLD-03), three ofrendas at about 25, 55 and 85% of the width, 50 hilos (GDD-WLD-04), one veladora on the risky high route, in plain view but high, reached by a tier 3 rebound (Open Question 15), and the exit on a rooftop (`src/levels/world2.test.ts` and each level's tests). Each new thing comes first in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test (GDD-WLD-03).

### GDD-WLD-13 Level 2-2

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: World 2's levels in "One World 2 PR"; eight screens, "8 screens", "World 2 on only", GDD-WLD-03). The layout is Proposed, and so is the twist that replaces the Zanate's beat (the chile and its fireball, below; the Zanate comes later, GDD-ENM-03; Open Question 29), and the shō's third beat, the bank and the fruit counter (the main session's layout, 2026-11-04, for the user to confirm from screenshots).

- **Place in the game:** World 2's second level, "Mercado Municipal", the covered market hall: fruit, chiles secos, flower and talavera stalls under rows of papel picado (the name Proposed, Open Question 26). "Next level" on 2-1's results menu starts it with the lives Poncho has at 2-1's exit, "Retry" on its own starts it again with the lives at its exit, and its "Next level" starts 2-3 with them (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02; `e2e/world2.spec.ts`); `?level=2-2` opens it with seven lives, and out of lives in it the press starts 2-1 with seven (`e2e/world2.spec.ts`). The frame and rules of 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12): gaps of at most 4 tiles, with the covered-pit rule (GDD-WLD-03), no tips and no placeholder lines, the glide and the wall cling off (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level2-2.ts`.
- **What it teaches:** the rollo (GDD-WLD-11) and the Chapulín (GDD-ENM-03), with 2-1's Paloma on its cordeles and 2-1's awnings (GDD-WLD-10) in its last act. It is meant to sit between 2-1 and 2-3 in difficulty (playtesting will tell).
- **Size:** 240 x 17 tiles (8 screens, 3840 x 272 px), one screen tall. The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224), and Poncho starts on it at tile column 3. The exit stands at column 234 on the rooftop of the last stalls (columns 224 to 239, top y = 112), as 1-3's stands on its last cliff (GDD-WLD-09): with the camera at the right edge it is on screen from 384 to 400 px, left of the phone's Jump button (measured).
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03; every "measured" by `src/levels/level2-2.test.ts`).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 48: the rollo where failing is safe.* Three hilos on the floor, a crate (column 9) and a mezzanine (columns 10 to 19, top y = 160). At its edge a rollo hangs on its post over a **sunken aisle** six tiles wide (columns 20 to 25, its floor the level's floor, 64 px down), whose far side, a second mezzanine (26 to 28), is 4 tiles up from it: the bundle (y = 152), the papel picado look of 1-3's fragile walls, and hilos hanging in the air where its line will run. Walking off the edge drops Poncho into the aisle with no life lost, and a 2-tile step at column 20 lets him climb back to the bundle; no jump from the mezzanine reaches the far side (every one lands on the mezzanine, the step or the aisle's floor) (measured; Open Question 29). A claw swipe from the edge unrolls the line at 160 px/s ahead of him and he hops onto it, its front always ahead of him (measured): the line is 8 px above the mezzanine (every rollo here is a hop up, Open Question 30). Then a Chapulín in a pen between two 2-tile walls (33 and 41; it starts at column 37 and keeps to x = 550 to 650): met alone, jumped while it crouches or clawed.
  - *Shō, columns 49 to 133: developing it, in three beats.*
    - *The floor's pits.* A real pit six tiles wide (49 to 54) under a rollo 8 px over the floor, then a rest where the first ofrenda stands (58); then a pit (61 to 66), a pillar two tiles wide (67, 68) and a pit (69 to 74), each pit under its own rollo: claw, hop, cross, step down onto the pillar, claw the next. A Paloma walks the pillar (x = 1079 to 1097), clawed from the first bridge, whose line is 8 px over the pillar's top. No jump crosses a floor pit with its rollo rolled up: each ends short of the pit or in it (measured).
    - *The Chapulín on an awning.* A 2-tile wall (77) pens a second Chapulín between it and the flower stall's wall (90 to 92, 6 tiles, top y = 128), with an awning five tiles wide 16 px over the pen's floor (85 to 89, y = 208). Its round is regular: tall 480 px/s rebounds across the awning, the stall wall turning it, then hops on the floor to the pen wall and back (x = 1264 to 1434, measured). Poncho meets it crouching from the top of the pen wall, and climbs the stall wall the awning's way: from under the awning, a jump held through two rebounds (his feet peak at y = 151.6, 130.9 and 110.7: tier 1 falls 2.9 px short of the wall's top, tier 2 clears it; measured). From the wall a hop over one open tile onto a cordel (94 to 98, y = 128) where a Paloma walks (x = 1511 to 1577; the open tile keeps it off the wall he lands on), and down to the floor, a rest seven tiles long (99 to 105).
    - *The bank and the fruit counter (new).* The rollo and the Chapulín together, and the picture the twist will turn. A fourth floor pit (106 to 111) under its rollo, 8 px over the floor, lands on a **bank** six tiles wide (112 to 117) where a third Chapulín hops between the pit's edge and the **fruit counter** (118 to 122, 3 tiles high, top y = 176), which turns it (it keeps to x = 1807 to 1882, measured). The strung bridge is a safe seat to read its round: its pit's edge turns it, so it never touches Poncho standing anywhere on the line (measured over 600 steps); standing on the bank in its way is a hit, on its first hop (measured). Waiting at the bridge's end until it has just crouched in the near half of its round, walking up and clawing it costs nothing (measured). A full jump climbs the counter. From the counter the next bundle hangs below and beyond: a rollo over a pit (127 to 132) whose bundle hangs 24 px over the floor at the pit's edge, past four tiles of plain floor (123 to 126). From anywhere on the counter no claw touches it, and without the bridge every jump from the counter comes down on the floor below it or in the pit (measured): he steps down, claws it from the floor and hops onto its line (measured). The counter is the chile walkway's twin, as high, as wide, as far from its pit, its bundle as low (measured), with plain floor where the walkway has spikes. The second ofrenda (134) follows.
  - *Ten, columns 134 to 167: the first chile, and a bundle the claw cannot reach.* Past the second ofrenda (134) a taco lies on the floor (136), so a Poncho walking on is in Taco by the piñata (138, 48 px over the floor), which then gives World 2's first chile on its top; a hop left from the end of a walkway (141 to 145, top y = 176) takes it, and he is in Fuego (measured). Past the walkway a bed of four spikes (146 to 149) and a pit (150 to 155), and over the pit a rollo whose bundle hangs 24 px over the floor at the pit's edge, below and beyond the walkway: the fruit counter's picture again, with spikes where Poncho stepped down. From anywhere on the walkway no claw touches it, standing or jumping. A fireball does: spat facing right from anywhere on the walkway, it falls onto the spike bed, bounces along it and unrolls the bundle 21 to 44 steps later, and a running jump from the walkway's edge lands on the line over the spikes. Without the bridge every jump from the walkway comes down on the spikes or in the pit (measured). It is never a dead end: in Taco or Fuego a drop onto the spikes costs the power, not a life, and a claw from the floor by the pit unrolls the bundle (measured); in Base the drop costs a life and Poncho is back at the second ofrenda with the taco and the piñata. A rest of twelve floor tiles (156 to 167) follows the bridge.
  - *Ketsu, columns 168 to 239: rollos with awnings.* A Tlacuache penned by a block (168) against the aisle's edge (x = 2712 to 2776), where a Fuego Poncho can spit at it from behind the block. The aisle: a pit fourteen tiles wide (174 to 187) with a rollo at its edge (16 px over the floor, 174 to 177), two 2-tile awnings in it (182, 183 and 186, 187, y = 208) and above them two cordeles a tile apart (178 to 183 and 185 to 189, y = 144). No jump from the bridge lands on a cordel; a running jump from its end onto the first awning, holding jump, rebounds him up through the cordel and he lands on it (measured). A Paloma walks the second cordel only (x = 2967 to 3033), clear of that rise. Without the bridge every jump ends short of the pit or in it (measured). Then the floor past the aisle with the third ofrenda (203), a 2-tile wall (209, 210) and from it a jump onto the last awning (212 to 215, y = 160, the highest an awning may be, GDD-WLD-10), over a pen where a fourth Chapulín hops (x = 3391 to 3441). The last pit (216 to 223) has a rollo high over it (y = 112), level with the rooftop: from the floor no jump and claw touches its bundle, from the awning's right end a jump with a claw on the way up does, and he comes down on the line as it unrolls and walks on to the exit. Without the bridge every jump from the floor or the awning ends in the pit (measured).
- **Rollos (GDD-WLD-11):** nine, each tied at its left post, none with an ofrenda between its post and its far end, so Poncho never respawns past a rolled-up bridge (measured). Every bundle the claw is meant to reach is where the claw of a Poncho standing at the edge reaches it, the seventh (the fireball's) and the last (the jump's) excepted; the sixth, the counter's, from the floor below the counter only (measured); a jump pressed in the claw's 2-step wind-up lifts the swing over a bundle at the edge, so the claw and the hop are two beats (Open Question 31). On every bridge the bot claws, his centre stays behind the strung front, by more than 2 px, and by more than 5 px on the floor's pits (measured).
- **Gaps (GDD-WLD-03):** pits of 6, 6, 6, 6, 6, 6, 6, 14 and 8 tiles; the uncovered runs in them are at most 4 tiles (columns 93; 96 to 98; 178 to 181; 184, 185; 216 to 219). World 1's rules would refuse the level, for its length and for its two 4-tile runs (measured).
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) floats in the top row (column 215, row 0) over the last awning's right end, atop a column of three hilos at the heights of its rebounds (rows 6, 4 and 2), with nothing to stand on beside it (Open Question 29): bouncing in place holding jump, his head peaks at y = 71.6, 50.9, 30.7 and 7.8, so only the tier 3 rebound's rise touches it; a full jump from the high bridge's start peaks at 23.6 (measured). The expert route bounces three times, claws the high bundle on the tier 3 rise, takes the veladora and comes down onto the bridge, and still reaches the exit (measured). In plain view (Open Question 15).
- **Ofrendas** at columns 58, 134 and 203, about 25%, 55% and 85% of the width (24.4%, 56.0% and 84.8%), on the floor: the first in the rest past the first floor pit, the second past the counter's bridge, the third past the aisle.
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02, GDD-ENM-03):** four Chapulines (in the first pen, on the stall's awning, on the bank, under the last awning), three Palomas (on the pillar, on the stall's cordel, on the aisle's second cordel) and one Tlacuache (from 171). Each starts at least 3 tiles from every respawn (the level validator), keeps to its stretch while Poncho waits at the start (the x ranges above, over 1800 steps), and no walker crosses an awning (measured).
- **Piñata (GDD-PWR-05, L-047):** 48 px over the floor, two columns before the walkway, the taco on the floor before it. In Taco or Fuego the chile stays on its top, which the hop from the walkway's end reaches; in Base it gives a taco, and a Poncho standing under it catches it as it comes back (measured).
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: three on the floor at the start (collected by the 35th step holding right with no jump), over the mezzanine and in the air over the sunken aisle, over the first pen, over each floor bridge, in the hop off the pillar, over the stall awning and on the stall wall and cordel, over the bank's bridge, by the counter's wall, on the counter and a trail down from its end to the bundle below, over the counter's bridge, by the piñata, on the walkway, over the spikes and the fireball's bridge, in the rest after it, over the Tlacuache's block, over the aisle's bridge and cordeles, on the last wall, in the column to the veladora, over the high bridge and on the rooftop. Each is in an empty tile within reach of something to stand on, none at the start or an ofrenda (measured). The main route collects 49; the expert route the 50th (measured).
- **Played through:** from a fresh start with seven lives, a bot reaches the exit through every ofrenda losing no life in 2519 steps (42.0 s, inside the 45 s of GDD-WLD-03), unrolling every rollo, the seventh by a fireball, and clawing the Chapulines on the stall's awning and on the bank; after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start it still does (measured). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- Drawn with World 2's art (GDD-ART-06), with 22 decor items of all seven kinds (measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`).

### GDD-WLD-14 Level 2-3

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: World 2's levels in "One World 2 PR"; eight screens, user, 2026-11-04: "8 screens"). The layout is
Proposed, and so are giving the optional fireball route to a second far-post rollo over the Fuego pit rather than to the alley's (Open
Question 32) and the mixed chain, the main session's beat for the longer level (below).

- **Place in the game:** World 2's third level, the last before the boss (GDD-BOS-03), "Azoteas", the rooftops and the toy stalls above the market (the name Proposed, Open Question 26): the twist and the test. "Next level" on 2-2's results menu starts it with 2-2's lives, "Retry" on its own starts it again with the lives at its exit, and its "Next level" starts La Estrella's arena 2-4 with them (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02; `src/levels/levels.test.ts`); `?level=2-3` opens it with seven lives. The frame and rules of 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12): open gaps of at most 4 tiles, with the covered-pit rule (GDD-WLD-03), no tips and no placeholder lines, the glide and the wall cling off (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level2-3.ts`.
- **What it brings:** the torn awning (GDD-WLD-10), the Trompo (GDD-ENM-03) and the rollo on the far post (GDD-WLD-11), each first in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test, and it ends with all of World 2 at once. It is meant to be the hardest of World 2's three levels (playtesting will tell). It has no spikes, crumbling tiles, springs, platforms, blocks, doors, keys or fragile walls: World 2's own toys and enemies carry it.
- **Size:** 240 x 17 tiles (8 screens, 3840 x 272 px). The base roof's top is row 14 (y = 224); a column whose bottom row is empty is the street far below, a pit. Poncho starts on the roof at column 3. The exit stands at column 227 on the exit roof (columns 220 to 239, top y = 128), like 1-3's on its last cliff; with the camera at the right edge it is on screen at 280 px, and the roof runs on past it to the level's end.
- **Teaching order (kishōtenketsu, GDD-WLD-03; every "measured" by `src/levels/level2-3.test.ts`).**
  - *Ki, columns 0 to 28: the torn awning over safe roof.* A whole awning W1 (columns 6 to 8, 32 px over the roof), 2-1's ladder recalled, with its column of hilos at the tier heights; then, two tiles on, a torn awning T1 of the same size and height (11 to 13), patched and faded, with its own column. The roof under both is safe and the main path walks under them. A plain landing on T1 rebounds once at 180 px/s, tears it, and he falls back on the roof; held, T1 gives one tier 1 rebound, which takes the lowest hilo of its column and not the next. The wordless puzzle: the chain carries from one awning to the next, so two held rebounds on W1 and then T1 give a tier 3 off T1 and its whole column (measured). Then a 3-tile gap (20 to 22) and an Alacrán (from 26) between it and the Trompo's pen, World 1's review.
  - *Shō, columns 29 to 131: the Trompo, the torn chain, then whole and torn mixed with a Trompo waiting.* The Trompo alone in a pen between two walls 2 tiles high (29 and 36): a claw topples it and a hop onto it then defeats it, no life lost (measured). The first piñata (38, row 10) is one empty column past the pen's right wall. Then two Trompos past a 3-tile gap (44 to 46), each penned: one between the gap's edge and a wall 2 tiles high (52), from 49 (x = 759.7 to 825), one between that wall and another (58), from 55 (x = 855 to 921); from either wall's top Poncho looks down into a pen before he steps in (measured). The first ofrenda (60) stands past the second pen, out of both Trompos' reach.
    - **The torn chain:** from the roof's edge (column 63) three pits of 4 tiles (64 to 67, 69 to 72, 74 to 77) between two chimneys 4 tiles above the roof (68 and 73, top y = 160), each pit with a torn awning 3 tiles wide at y = 208 against the chimney beyond it (T2 at 65, T3 at 70, T4 at 75). Holding jump and right from the roof's edge rebounds on T2, T3 and T4 at tiers 1, 2 and 3, tears each, and lands on the roof past them, taking the six hilos of the chain, which climb with the tiers; letting go after the first or the second rebound cuts the rise, he does not clear the next chimney and falls into the pit; without the torn awnings a running jump from the edge meets the first chimney and falls (measured). A chimney's 1-tile top is a perch for a precise player, a slower way than the chain (Open Question 32). No pit there is wider than 4 tiles, so the validator is met without the torn awnings, which cover nothing (GDD-WLD-03; made whole, they would cover part of the pits; measured).
    - **A breather roof** (78 to 91) with the second piñata (85, row 10, one column past a 2-tile wall at 83).
    - **The mixed chain (the beat the eight screens add):** stripes to stop on, patches to bounce on, and a Trompo on the landing roof. From the roof's edge (column 91) three pits, all their awnings at y = 208: the first, 9 tiles (92 to 100), holds the whole awning Wa (93 to 96) and the torn Tb (98 to 100) against a **tall chimney** 6 tiles above the roof (101, top y = 128); the second (102 to 105) holds the whole Wc (103 to 105) against a chimney 4 tiles high (106, top y = 160); the third (107 to 110) holds the torn Td (108 to 110) against the roof L. Read left to right it is stop, bounce, stop, bounce.
      - *A whole awning is a place to stop over the pit:* a jump from the edge onto Wa, let go, rebounds once at 180 px/s and settles (measured), and so does a landing on Wc let go (measured by the bot).
      - *A patch is not:* a plain landing on Tb rebounds once at 180 px/s, tears it, and he falls into the pit (measured from three aims).
      - *The tall chimney asks for the ladder, and only a whole awning can start it:* a jump from standing on Wa that comes down on Tb (from x = 1506 on, every spot tried to Wa's right end) is a tier 1 rebound that peaks at y = 130.9, 2.9 px under the tall chimney's top, as 2-1's ladder awning falls 2.9 px short, and a life is lost; from Wa's left end the same jump comes down on Wa again (measured). Hopping in place on Wa holding jump starts the chain: Wa gives tier 1, steered onto Tb that gives tier 2, which carries him over the tall chimney (his feet at least 4.5 px above its top as his centre crosses its middle), and letting go past it he settles on Wc, from every spot of Wa's right part tried (x = 1496 to 1556; measured). It is the Ki's puzzle (W1, then T1) over a pit. Wa's column of hilos (column 95, rows 11, 6 and 5) gives one per beat and never the next: the jump the lowest, tier 1 the middle, tier 2 the top (measured), 2-1's "this goes higher".
      - *The Trompo waits on L* (111 to 120), penned between the pit's edge and the step R (121 to 126, 2 tiles high), from 116 (x = 1783.2 to 1929). It never reaches Poncho waiting on Wc (measured over 1200 steps). From standing on Wc, holding jump and right reaches L, through Td or over it from the chimney's top (measured from x = 1656 to 1688). Taking off from Wc (jump and right held until he lands on L, then standing still) lands him in the Trompo's path in 112 of the 250 steps of its round, a hit within half a second of landing; taking off once it has turned at the pit's edge and heads for the step (heading right, short of x = 1840) never does (measured). Wait on the stripes, go when it turns; on L the claw and the hop of its first pen finish it.
      - *The expert's line:* holding jump and right from the roof's edge flows Wa (tier 1), Tb (tier 2) over the tall chimney and on to L with no stop, losing no life (measured with no enemies); with the Trompo there, where he comes down is luck.
      - No pit there is open wider than 4 tiles: Wa covers its pit's columns 93 to 96, so the first pit's open runs are 1 and 4; the torn awnings cover nothing (measured).
    - **Rest:** the step R and the roof past it, to the second ofrenda (132).
  - *Ten, columns 132 to 173: the rollo on the far post.* **The Fuego pit, the question** (140 to 143, right after the ofrenda): its rollo R2 is tied at its far post level with the roof (y = 224); its bundle hangs below the roof's top, out of the claw from either edge, and the pit is a 4-tile jump on the main route: the first bundle on the far post, seen where failing costs nothing. In Fuego (the second piñata gives the chile to a Poncho in Taco), a fireball spat 2 to 3 tiles back from the edge (x = 2188 to 2208) or at the very edge (x = 2245) bounces into the bundle and unrolls it (22 of the positions tried from 5 tiles back to the edge); walking the line takes two hilos that a running jump across passes over (measured). **The alley, the answer** (156 to 163, 8 tiles): the high roof F1 beyond it (164 to 169) is 6 tiles above the roof (top y = 128). The rollo R1 is tied at F1's wall, its line 48 px over the roof (y = 176), so it unrolls towards Poncho. Deep in the alley a whole awning A2 (160 to 162, y = 256) lies under the bundle, with a column of hilos up to it. Bounce on A2, claw while rising past the line's height, and the bridge unrolls towards him; the next rebound goes up through it and he lands on it; from it a full jump reaches F1 (measured by the bot). Without R1 no rebound off A2 reaches F1, wherever it is taken: the tier 3 peak is at y = 135.8 at the highest, 8 px under F1's top; from the near roof neither the claw nor a fireball reaches the bundle (measured). The alley's left four columns are covered by R1, its right four are open, so its open run is 4. A2 is whole: a missed claw costs nothing but another bounce; stepping off it into the alley is the one danger. **A Tlacuache on a rooftop between awnings:** it walks F1 (from 167, x = 2632 to 2712, measured) between A2's alley and A3, a whole awning against F1's far wall (170 and 171, y = 208) that catches a Poncho who steps off; from F1 a jump to the right arcs over A3 to the roof M.
  - *Ketsu, columns 174 to 239: everything together, then the exam.* The roof M (174 to 184) with a Trompo (from 179, x = 2791.5 to 2952.5); a pit of 4 (185 to 188) filled by a whole awning A4 (y = 208), where the Chapulín, which starts on the roof K1 (189 to 197), is thrown high: it hops over M, A4 and K1 (x = 2791 to 3171, its feet as high as y = 130.9), never falls and never tears an awning (measured). Then a 4-tile pit (198 to 201) with a cordel 16 px over the roof, the Paloma walking on it (x = 3175 to 3225, measured), and the third ofrenda (204) on the roof K2 (202 to 213). **The exam:** past K2 a pit of 6 (214 to 219) before the exit roof. The rollo R4 is tied at the exit roof's wall, its line at y = 176, and under it the torn awning T5 (217 and 218, y = 240), 16 px from the wall: the Ten's trick with the Shō's tarp, one bounce, so the claw must come on that rise. Without R4, T5's one rebound never reaches the exit roof wherever it is steered, and a life is lost; with the claw on the way up the line unrolls, he lands on it, and a full jump puts him on the exit roof (measured). The claw unrolls R4 pressed against the exit roof's wall (his body over the bundle) and from 18 px back (the swipe over it) (measured; GDD-WLD-11). The pit's left three columns are covered by R4, the right three open. The respawn is the third ofrenda, ten columns back: a failed exam is a short retry, and T5 and R4 come back with the life.
  - *The exit* at column 227. A giant seven-pointed star piñata is to hang in the far background over the exit roof, from a rope between two poles, in view from the exam on: La Estrella foreshadowed (GDD-BOS-03). Scenery only, paler and behind the rooftops, never read as a piñata block (GDD-PWR-05). Not drawn yet (Open Question 32).
- **Rhythm:** tension and rest alternate: the Ki's safe puzzle; the pen, the gap and the two pens (ofrenda); the torn chain, a breather roof with a piñata, the mixed chain with its two whole awnings as rests inside it and the Trompo at its end, the step and the roof (ofrenda); the Fuego pit as a question that costs nothing, the alley, F1 and the drop to M; M, A4, K1, the cordel (ofrenda); the exam. The respawns sit just before each hard beat: the first ofrenda before the torn chain, the second before the far-post beats, the third before the exam.
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora (GDD-LIFE-03) floats high over A2's left end (column 160, row 6), in plain view (Open Question 15): only a tier 3 rebound off A2 reaches it, a tier 2 does not (measured). The tier 3 chain is taken over the alley's pit, so it is a risk.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 60, 132 and 204, on the roof, about 25%, 55% and 85% of the width (25.2%, 55.2% and 85.2%). Each rollo lies after the ofrenda Poncho respawns at before its pit, and no ofrenda lies inside a rollo's span (measured), so he never respawns on the wrong side of a rolled-up bridge (GDD-WLD-11).
- **Gaps (GDD-WLD-03):** 3, 3, 4, 4, 4, 9 (the mixed chain's first pit), 4, 4, 4 (the Fuego pit), 8 (the alley), 4, 4, 4 and 6 (the exam) tiles; the open runs 3, 3, 4, 4, 4, 1, 4, 4, 4, 4 (the alley's), 4 and 3 (the exam's) (measured). The validator passes it with World 2's rules.
- **Awnings and papel picado (GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-11):** thirteen awnings, six of them whole (W1, Wa, Wc, A2, A3, A4) and seven torn (T1 to T4, Tb, Td, T5), each 2 to 6 tiles wide, its top a multiple of 8 and no higher than y = 160, at least a tile from any level tile whose top is at its height; one cordel; three rollos, all tied at their far, right post (R2, R1, R4). Every rollo line over an awning has 2 tiles of clear air or more (measured).
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-01 to GDD-ENM-03):** an Alacrán (from 26), five Trompos (the pen's from 32, the penned pair from 49 and 55, L's from 116, M's from 179), a Tlacuache (on F1 from 167), a Chapulín (on K1 from 193) and a Paloma (on the cordel from 199). None starts within 3 tiles of a respawn (the level validator). While Poncho waits at the start each walker keeps to its roof (the Alacrán x = 375 to 457, the pen's Trompo 487 to 569, the others above) and the Paloma to its cordel, and none walks through or under an awning; the Chapulín is the one that plays on an awning, A4 (measured).
- **Piñatas (GDD-PWR-05, L-047):** two, each 48 px over the roof, one empty column past a wall 2 tiles high, as in 1-3: in Base a jump from under it opens it and holding right once the taco has dropped catches it; in Taco a claw from the wall's right end opens it, the chile stays on its top, and a hop takes it (measured for both). The first gives the taco, the second the chile for the Fuego route, which comes after the second ofrenda.
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), each in an empty tile outside every awning, none touched standing at the start or an ofrenda (measured): a floor trail, W1's and T1's columns at the tier heights (the Ki puzzle), over the gaps and the pen's wall, over and in the two pens, six along the torn chain's arcs climbing with its tiers, the breather roof and the second piñata's wall, the mixed chain (the edge, Wa's column, over the chimneys, Wc), L and the step, the Fuego pair on R2's line, the roof before the alley, the alley's column to the bundle, F1, the top of the jump arc from F1 to M, M, over A4, K1, the cordel, K2, the exam's rise and the exit roof.
- **Played through:** from a fresh start with seven lives, a bot (holding right; jumping at walls, drops off level tiles and piñatas, waiting while a walker is where it would come down; clawing enemies in reach; letting a spinning Trompo heading away go and hopping onto a stunned one; holding jump through the torn chain; in the mixed chain hopping in place on Wa, steering onto Tb, letting go past the tall chimney to settle on Wc and waiting there until the Trompo on L has turned at the pit's edge; at each far-post rollo steering its rebounds under the bundle and clawing on the rise; waiting out of the Paloma's sight) reaches the exit through all three ofrendas losing no life, in 2574 steps (42.9 s; asserted under 2700), tearing T2, T3, T4, Tb, Td and T5, stringing R1 and R4, standing on Wc but never on a torn awning, and defeating the Trompo on L, with 40 of the 50 hilos: all but W1's and T1's columns (the ladder and the chain take them), the top two of Wa's column (the hop in place takes them) and the Fuego pair (R2's line takes them); after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start it still clears it losing none (measured). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- Drawn with World 2's art (GDD-ART-06), with 20 decor items of all seven kinds (measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`). Its rooftops use World 2's ground tiles; chimneys and high roofs of their own are not drawn (Open Question 32). The decor rule stands a maceta on the tall chimney's 1-tile top and a farol on the chimney beside Wc (visible in the shots), as it would on any roof.

## 9. Enemies and bosses

### GDD-ENM-01 Enemy design rules

Status: Proposed

- One idea per enemy, readable at a glance, with a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack. (Built for the Zopilote's dive, the Tlacuache's charge and Don Zopilote's dive and sweep, GDD-BOS-02, and for the Paloma's startle, the Chapulín's hop (the last 0.5 s of its crouch) and La Estrella's drop, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03, while the Trompo, which has no attack, needs none. His cactus fruit has none of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps, 0.7 to 0.9 s, before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below it, Open Question 20.)
- Every enemy can be beaten at least one way: stomp, claw or avoidance.
- Enemies never spawn on screen within 3 tiles of Poncho's respawn. (Checked for every enemy in every level, the sandbox included, by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04.) Don Zopilote starts at least 3 tiles from the spawn of his arena, its only respawn, and starts there again whenever Poncho respawns (checked by the level validator's boss-level rule, GDD-TEC-04; GDD-BOS-01), and so does La Estrella in hers, at the start of each of her three phases (GDD-BOS-03).
- Enemy contact is resolved by GDD-CMB-03 and never costs more than 1 life. (Built for the Alacrán: it is a hit like a spike, one life in Base.) Avoidance is a built way to beat an enemy: the cactus buddy cannot be defeated at all (GDD-ENM-02).

### GDD-ENM-02 World 1 bestiary

Status: Proposed (the enemies' look is Decided, user, 2026-11-04: "Approved"; see The look below)

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alacrán (scorpion) | Patrols a platform, turns at edges | Stomp or claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03) |
| Zopilote (vulture) | Flies a sine path, swoops after a 0.5 s telegraph | Claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03; not stompable) |
| Cactus buddy (el Cuate) | Static, spiky, harmless from below | Avoid only (not stompable; the claw passes through it and a fireball is used up by it) |
| Tlacuache (opossum) | Fast charger, plays dead after hit | Claw (even while it plays dead), stomp after wake (also a fireball while awake, GDD-PWR-03) |

- **Alacrán (built, details Proposed):** a 14 x 10 px scorpion standing on the ground that walks at 30 px/s (`ENEMY` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right. It turns round when its box meets a wall, flush against it, and when the ground under its leading edge ends, so it never walks off a ledge and never falls. It walks up and down sand slopes with the middle of its feet on them, a slope's high side turns it like a wall, and the ground under its leading edge is a level tile or a slope in the row of its feet or the row below (GDD-MOV-09). A stomp (GDD-CMB-01), a claw swipe whose box overlaps it (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball that overlaps it (GDD-PWR-03) defeats it; it is then gone. Every fireball that overlaps a live enemy in a step is used up, whatever defeats the enemy that step. Touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like spikes: boxes that only share an edge do not touch. A defeated one stays defeated until a life is lost, when every enemy is alive again and back at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is drawn as the Durango scorpion (the look below). The sandbox has one, on the platform at row 10, columns 4 to 7, which Poncho reaches from the top of the 2-tile wall; the piñata at column 8 is the platform's right end, at the same height, so the Alacrán walks across its top too and turns at its far edge (x = 71 to 137); the platform is 64 px above the first ofrenda, clear of its respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 0-1 has three, level 1-1 six and level 1-3 one, penned behind a fragile wall, its walk clear of the moving block just past its pen (GDD-WLD-06, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-WLD-09), and the slopes level one, which walks over its hill, its dune and its first bump (GDD-MOV-09). In World 2, 2-1 has one, penned by two crates, and 2-3 one, on the roof before the Trompo's pen (GDD-WLD-12, GDD-WLD-14). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`, its walk in `src/sim/walker.ts`.
- **Zopilote (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px vulture that flies (`ZOPILOTE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The level gives the middle of its start and its cruise line, and it ignores tiles. Cruising, it flies at 40 px/s up to 48 px either side of its start, turning round there (it starts heading right), while bobbing up and down 12 px on a sine with a period of 2.5 s (150 steps). When Poncho's hurtbox is within 96 px to either side of it and between 0 and 80 px below it, it spots him: it freezes and flashes for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01; the body flashes red and the wings are raised). It then dives in a straight line at 150 px/s to the spot Poncho's hurtbox centre was at when the telegraph ended (it aims once and does not follow him on), and from there climbs straight back up to its cruise line at 60 px/s, then cruises again from where it is, and may spot him again at once. It cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01): landing on it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), and so is touching it any other way. A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03, which is used up) defeats it. It is defeated for good until a life is lost, when it is alive again on its cruise line at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is drawn as a black vulture of Don Zopilote's court (the look below). The sandbox has one, cruising at y = 64 from x = 216 (168 to 264), above the middle of the level: from the floor Poncho's hurtbox centre is 144 px below its cruise line and it never spots him; on the Alacrán's platform he is 80 px below it, the edge of its reach, so it spots him there whenever it is at or below its cruise line: half of every bob, and on its first step (user, 2026-10-25: kept as the taller body makes it, a harder platform) (a Poncho who lingers there is dived on); and the spring ledge and the moving platform, 32 px and 48 px below it, are well within reach. The Tlacuache's ledge, from x = 400, is out of its sideways reach, so it never spots Poncho there. It is far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 1-1 has two, each cruising at y = 136, 72 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho on the floor, so it spots him there, and neither reaches a respawn point (GDD-WLD-07). Level 1-3 has one, cruising at y = 112 over the shuttle's spikes, 64 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho waiting on the step before the shuttle, so it spots him there, and it never spots him at the start or at an ofrenda (GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds, a swoop that leaves the screen. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Cactus buddy (built, details Proposed):** a static 12 x 16 px cactus standing on the ground at the place the level gives it (`CACTUS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It never moves, and it is "avoid only" (user decision, 2026-10-22): a stomp does not defeat it (it cannot be stomped, GDD-CMB-01, and landing on it is a hit), a claw swipe passes through it, and a fireball that overlaps it is used up without harming it, so it can cover Poncho from fireballs. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), except that it is harmless from below: a contact is no hit while Poncho is moving up and his hurtbox top was at or below its underside on the step before, once his head is above that line he is inside it and the usual hit applies. That can only happen for a cactus with no solid tile directly beneath it: a cactus standing on the ground or a ledge has the top of that tile as its underside, which Poncho's head cannot get below while he overlaps it (tile collision, GDD-MOV-06). So no cactus buddy that stands on something can be passed from below, and no level has one with nothing beneath it yet; the rule is only covered by unit tests on a cactus in mid-air. It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like the other enemies. It is drawn as el Cuate (the look below). It is alive again, simply where it was, after a lost life (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one on the right half of the top of the 2-tile wall (columns 10 and 11, top y = 192), at x = 184, leaving the left 18 px of the wall top free to stand on and to jump from to the Alacrán's platform; it is more than 3 tiles from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). No spot in the sandbox shows the from-below case (see above), so that is covered by unit tests. Level 1-1 has three, and levels 1-2 and 1-3 one each, each on the floor (GDD-WLD-07 to GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Tlacuache (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px opossum standing on the ground (`TLACUACHE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right, and walks at 24 px/s, turning at walls (flush against them) and at ledge ends, and walking and charging up and down sand slopes, like the Alacrán (GDD-MOV-09). It spots Poncho when his feet are within 16 px of its own, up or down (its feet where they are now, on a slope too), and his hurtbox centre is in front of it (the way it faces) by more than 0 and up to 80 px (user decision, 2026-10-22: sight, then dash).
