It then freezes and flashes red for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01) and charges at 150 px/s the way it faces, without turning to follow him, until a wall or the end of its ground stops it (as in its walk, it never runs off a ledge); there it turns round and walks again, and may spot him again at once. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), while it walks, telegraphs or charges. Its first stomp (GDD-CMB-01) does not defeat it: Poncho bounces as from any stomp and it plays dead for 2.0 s (120 steps), lying still on its back (user decision, 2026-10-22: stomp twice). Playing dead it is out of play except for the claw: touching it is no hit, landing on it is no stomp and a fireball passes over it without being used up, but a claw swipe that overlaps it defeats it (user decision, 2026-10-22). Then it wakes facing the way it faced, walking, and touching it is a hit again (so a Poncho standing in it when it wakes is hit; its feet twitch in its last 0.5 s as a warning). After it has played dead once, a stomp defeats it. The claw (GDD-CMB-02) defeats it at any time, and a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) at any time it is awake, whether or not it has been stomped; either wins over a first stomp in the same step. It is drawn as the opossum who stole fire (the look below). A lost life brings it back at its start, walking, not yet stomped (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one in the middle of a ledge 5 tiles long in the top right corner (row 6, columns 25 to 29, x 400 to 480, top y = 96), which it walks end to end; Poncho reaches the ledge with a jump from the moving platform at its right end, 32 px lower. The ledge is out of the Zopilote's sideways reach and 128 px above the third ofrenda, far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). The slopes level has one, which walks its second bump (GDD-MOV-09). Level 1-2 has three, each in a pen: one on the floor, which does not spot Poncho on the pen's walls, one on a 45° hill and one on a gentle dune (GDD-WLD-08). Level 1-3 has three: on a sand dune penned by a block and a door, behind a fragile wall, and on the last cliff's top, where it walks past the exit; the one behind the fragile wall, let out and left alive, walks through moving blocks (GDD-WLD-09). In World 2, 2-2 has one, penned by a block against a pit's edge, and 2-3 one, on a high roof between two awnings (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **The look (built; the designs Decided, user, 2026-11-04: "Approved", from a design page that played every animation at game speed, and the cactus buddy's name, "El Cuate"; the frame rules Proposed):** the four are real animals and plants of the Chihuahuan desert, each with a true Mexican story, none of them dressed (GDD-VIS-04), drawn in code like Poncho and Don Zopilote (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/enemies1/` (one module each and a shared kit; `scripts/art/make-enemies.mjs` writes `assets/sprites/enemies/`), at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined (GDD-ART-05) and only in World 1's 24 colours (GDD-ART-06; checked by `scripts/art/enemies1.test.mjs`), facing right and mirrored when they face left. Each body fills its box, only a tail, a claw, a flower, a wing or a spine tip standing outside it (fair hits, GDD-VIS-03), as far as `ENEMY_ART.reach` says (pinned by `scripts/art/enemies1.test.mjs`, L-059). A walker never reaches below its feet. A tail, a claw or a wing beside the box may lie over a wall's face, as the Tlacuache's tail does when it turns flush against a pen wall in 1-2 and 1-3 (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow it, pin it"), or over a slope's sand, as the Tlacuache's and the Alacrán's tails do walking down a sand hill, trailing uphill behind them (up to 9.5 px on 1-2's 45° hill: only the middle of a body's feet meets a slope, GDD-MOV-09, so Poncho's drawing dips into it the same way) and a diving Zopilote's wing tip at a cliff in 1-3 (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow beside the body"). Nothing of any frame reaches into a tile or a slope's sand over or below its box in the real levels: checked at every step over every level with a World 1 enemy by `scripts/art/enemies1.levels.test.mjs`, with Poncho at his spawn and dropped beside each enemy so that it shows every look it has in play. The design and its notes: `assets/characters/world1-enemies/`.
  - **Alacrán: the Durango scorpion** (*Centruroides suffusus*), the emblem of Durango, whose people call themselves alacranes: a busy little patroller on duty. Red-brown with the species' two dark stripes down its back, honey pincers, a tail of five beads arched over its back in a C, a cream stinger (the hazard mark), and big stern eyes. Frame 24 x 16 px, feet on its bottom row: a four-frame walk, one frame for every 2 px it walks (its x), the legs in alternating pairs, the body bobbing, the tail a frame behind and the pincers snipping.
  - **Zopilote: a black vulture of Don Zopilote's court** (*Coragyps atratus*, the zopilote over every Mexican road): navy-black plumage, a bare grey-lilac head with a pale hooked beak and a sly eye, the black vulture's pale patches at the base of its five finger feathers, and one cholla joint stuck on its back, green with three pale spines, the not-stompable mark it shares with its king (GDD-CMB-01). Frame 32 x 24 px centred on its box: cruising, four flap frames of 6 steps each; in its telegraph, wings flared high, beak open in a hiss and talons out, flashing red on alternate 4-step blocks as before; diving, wings swept back, a steep frame when the dive is at least 1.3 times as tall as it is long (aim point against where it is), else a shallow one, facing the way it dives; climbing back, tilted up, two frames of 4 steps.
  - **Cactus buddy: el Cuate** ("cuate" is Mexican for buddy, from Nahuatl *cōātl*, twin): a chubby ribbed column with two raised arms (the shape no scenery plant has, GDD-ART-06), pale spine clusters all round and three on top (the hazard and not-stompable marks), a red claret-cup flower on his head (2 px above his box, his spines 2.5 px when he is alarmed) and a sleepy face. Frame 16 x 24 px. He dozes, breathing in and out every 45 steps; he peeks with one eye at Poncho when Poncho's hurtbox centre is within 64 px of his middle sideways and 48 px up or down; and within 28 px sideways and 32 px up or down he throws his arms up, eyes wide and spines bristling, shivering every 4 steps. Peeking or alarmed he faces Poncho. Drawing only: he never moves and nothing of his rules changes.
  - **Tlacuache: the opossum who stole fire** (in Mazatec and Nahua tellings it carried fire to people in its tail, which is why the tail is bare; a faint scorch near its tip): grizzled grey-lilac fur, a white pointed face with a dark eye mask, round dark ears and a mischievous eye. Frame 28 x 16 px, feet on its bottom row: a four-frame waddle, one frame for every 2 px it walks; its telegraph crouched, rump up, ears flat and jaws open in a toothy hiss, flashing red as before; its charge a stretched gallop, one frame for every 5 px; playing dead on its back, legs up, X eyes and tongue out, its feet curling in the last 0.5 s as before.
  - **The defeat poof (built, Proposed):** a defeated Alacrán, Zopilote or Tlacuache no longer just vanishes: for 8 steps it shows a squashed frame (stomped; never the Zopilote, which cannot be) or a knocked-over frame rising 6 px (the claw, a fireball), then a Nintendo-style poof centred on its box, a white star burst and a dust cloud that swells and breaks into three puffs, 4 steps a frame, 24 steps (0.4 s) in all. It is drawing only: nothing touches it, and a lost life clears it. World 2's enemies still vanish (GDD-ENM-03).
  - `ENEMY_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: the frames, `walkPx` 2, `chargePx` 5, `flapSteps` 6, `climbSteps` 4, `flashSteps` 4, `steepRatio` 1.3, `dozeSteps` 45, `peek` 64 x 48, `alarm` 28 x 32, `shiverSteps` 4, `defeatSteps` 8, `hitRise` 6, `poofSteps` 4, and `reach` (game px behind, in front, above and below the box: the Alacrán 4.5, 1.5, 3, 0, the Zopilote 6, 0.5, 6.5, 3.5, the cactus buddy 1, 1.5, 2.5, 0, the Tlacuache 5.5, 3, 0.5, 0); the frame rules in `src/ui/enemyPose.ts` and `src/ui/defeatFx.ts`.

### GDD-ENM-03 World 2 bestiary

Status: Decided (three new enemies in this round, the Paloma, the Chapulín and the Trompo, and the Zanate later, user, 2026-11-04: "Three now, Zanate later"). Their rules, names and numbers are Proposed. Built with placeholder shapes (`src/sim/paloma.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.ts`, every rule that depends on the kind a table over every kind in `src/sim/enemy.ts`; tested in `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`, `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`, `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, `src/sim/world2run.test.ts`, the test level `lab-2e`, `src/levels/lab2e.test.ts`, and `e2e/enemies2.spec.ts`); their placeholder look approved from screenshots with World 2's (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`), their final art still to come. The details the main session settled during the build (2026-11-04: the Paloma startled from either side, only while it walks; its bob drawing only; the Chapulín's look-before-it-hops rule and its start crouched) are Proposed.

World 2's enemies follow GDD-ENM-01: one idea each, a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack, each beaten at least one way, none starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, never more than one life per contact. They are animals of a Mexican town and animated toys from its stalls: no people, and no rats, cockroaches or flies, which in a food market say "dirty" (GDD-VIS-04). Each new one is met alone on safe ground before it is combined with others: the Paloma in 2-1, the Chapulín in 2-2, the Trompo in 2-3 (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14).

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Paloma (pigeon) | Walks on the ground and on cordeles; come close and it flutters up, hovers, and comes back down | Stomp, claw or fireball; or walk under it while it hovers |
| Chapulín (grasshopper) | Crouches, then hops in a fixed arc; an awning throws it high | Stomp, claw or fireball |
| Trompo (spinning top) | The first heavy enemy: spins along the ground | Claw or fireball to stun it, then a stomp (not stompable while it spins) |
| Tlacuache, Alacrán | Return from World 1 with their rules (GDD-ENM-02) | As in World 1 |

- **Paloma (pigeon):** a 14 x 12 px pigeon, World 2's basic enemy from 2-1 on, which teaches that an enemy can react to how Poncho comes at it. It walks at 20 px/s on the ground, along a cordel or the strung part of a rollo (GDD-WLD-11), turning at walls and at the ends of its ground, like the Alacrán (GDD-ENM-02); off the end of a tile ledge it walks on onto a cordel that goes on at the height of its feet, not onto one lower down. **Startle:** while it walks, when Poncho's hurtbox centre is within 48 px of its middle across, on either side, and his feet within 24 px of its feet up or down, it stops and telegraphs for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, frozen on the ground: puffed up wider, wings raised, flashing red 4 steps on and 4 off), then flies straight up 40 px at 120 px/s (2 px a step, 20 steps, 0.33 s), hovers 1.0 s (60 steps; drawn bobbing 2 px, which moves only the drawing), comes back down at 60 px/s to where it was (40 steps, 0.67 s) and walks on; it is not startled while it flies, and once down it can be startled again at once. Hovering, its box's bottom is 40 px above its feet, 9 px over the hurtbox top of a Poncho standing where it stood (8 px over his body box), so he can pass under it for 73 steps (1.22 s), from 0.27 s into its rise to 0.15 s into its descent (measured by `src/sim/paloma.test.ts`). A Poncho whose feet are more than 24 px above it does not startle it, so a jump onto it from higher up is a stomp, and it can be stomped in every phase, walking, telegraphing, rising, hovering or coming down: a stepping stone over a gap (2-1's pit, GDD-WLD-12). Sprinting into one is the mistake it punishes, after a clear warning: 48 px at the 120 px/s run takes 0.4 s, inside its telegraph, so running on into it is a hit (measured). A stomp, a claw swipe or a fireball (used up) defeats it; touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). A lost life brings it back at its start, walking. Not built yet: a coo for its telegraph (no sound of the game's set fits; GDD-AUD-02). `PALOMA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 14, `height` 12, `speed` 20, `sightX` 48, `sightY` 24, `telegraphSteps` 30, `riseHeight` 40, `riseSpeed` 120, `hoverSteps` 60, `bobPx` 2, `descendSpeed` 60.
- **Chapulín (grasshopper):** a 12 x 10 px green field grasshopper, from 2-2, the first enemy whose danger is an arc to read, and the one that plays with the awnings. It starts crouched. It crouches still for 0.6 s (36 steps: legs folded, body low; in its last 0.5 s, 30 steps, it flashes red 4 steps on and 4 off, its telegraph), then hops the way it faces at 300 px/s up and 60 px/s across, falling as Poncho falls (GDD-MOV-04): its feet peak 30.2 px up, 25 steps (0.42 s) in the air, 25 px along (measured by `src/sim/chapulin.test.ts`), and it crouches again where it lands. Before it hops it plays the hop's arc out: if the hop would meet a wall, or end lower than it stands (a pit, the end of a ledge, a step down) and not on an awning, it turns round instead and crouches again, so it never falls; it climbs a step 1 tile high and turns at a wall 2 tiles high (measured). A crouch with no ground under it falls. **On an awning:** a fall whose feet cross the top of an awning its box is over throws it up at 480 px/s, the awning's tier 1 (GDD-WLD-10), at every landing, without crouching and keeping its 60 px/s across: each bounce peaks 77.1 px up and lasts 40 steps (0.67 s) and 40 px, so on a 4-tile awning it bounces twice, then falls off the end and crouches again on the ground (measured). A level tile at the awning's height wins in the same step. A torn awning tears under its bounce as under Poncho's (`src/sim/world2run.test.ts`). It passes up through an awning from below. Over an awning it is a tall, regular hazard that Poncho times his own rebound against, or stomps in mid-air for a bounce of his own. Its hop passes through cordeles. Jump over it while it crouches, or stomp, claw or fireball it; it is soft, so a stomp defeats it crouched or in the air. Running under its hop does not work: its feet at its peak reach 0.8 px into a standing Poncho's hurtbox (measured), which its first meeting shows on safe ground. Touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). A lost life brings it back at its start. No food framing, and nothing that recalls a famous TV character's costume. `CHAPULIN` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 12, `height` 10, `crouchSteps` 36, `flashSteps` 30, `hopUp` 300, `hopAcross` 60, `awningUp` 480 (`AWNING.tiers[0]`).
- **Trompo (spinning top):** a 14 x 14 px spinning top from the toy stall, 2-3's new enemy and the game's first **heavy** enemy, the one the claw's stun waited for (GDD-CMB-02); it teaches the first two-verb combo, claw, then stomp. It spins along the ground at 70 px/s, turning at walls and ledge ends like the Alacrán, faster than it but slower than Poncho's 120 px/s run, so he can always back off. It has no attack and so no telegraph: its threat is contact. **Not stompable while it spins:** landing on it is a hit, marked by the three pale points on its crown (GDD-CMB-01). **Stun:** a claw swipe that overlaps it, or a fireball (used up), stuns it for 1.0 s (60 steps), with the stomp's thud (GDD-AUD-02): it topples onto its side and lies still, harmless to touch, and wobbles 1 px every 4 steps in its last 0.5 s (30 steps, drawn only), as the Tlacuache twitches (GDD-ENM-02). A claw on a stunned top does nothing more and does not renew its stun, and a fireball passes over it without being used up; a **stomp on a stunned top defeats it**, and Poncho bounces as from any stomp. After the second it gets up spinning the way it faced, and a Poncho inside it then is hit. The claw's box reaches 20 px in front of him, and a short hop from there lands on the stunned top well within the second (measured by `src/sim/trompo.test.ts`, and in 2-3's first pen by `src/levels/level2-3.test.ts`). A lost life brings it back at its start, spinning. `TROMPO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 14, `height` 14, `speed` 70, `stunSteps` 60, `wobbleSteps` 30.
- **Returning from World 1:** the Tlacuache (opossums raiding a market are true to life) and a few Alacranes (in fruit crates; the review enemies of 2-1's opening), with the rules of GDD-ENM-02: an Alacrán in 2-1 and in 2-3, a Tlacuache in 2-2 and in 2-3. Walkers ignore awnings and cordeles, and a level never lets a walk cross an awning (GDD-WLD-10; each World 2 level's tests check it). The Zopilote and the cactus buddy stay in the desert.
- **Defeats (Proposed, Open Question 26):** a defeated bird flies off the screen in a puff of feathers and a defeated toy tumbles away: nothing is shown dying (GDD-VIS-04). Not built yet: like World 1's, a defeated enemy is simply gone.
- **Later, the Zanate (great-tailed grackle; Proposed, not built; user, 2026-11-04: "Zanate later"):** the bold black bird of every Mexican plaza and market, a thief whose one idea is that it wants Poncho's food, not Poncho. In Base it ignores him; in Taco or Fuego, after a 0.5 s telegraph, it swoops at him once from its perch (a tile, a post or a cordel), and touching it in the swoop takes his taco or chile as a hit in those states does, never a life (GDD-CMB-03); it flies slowly back to its perch with it, and defeating it on the way (a stomp, the claw or a fireball) drops what it carries for him to take back. Its rules and numbers are put to the user when it is built.
- **Look (GDD-ART-06, Proposed):** placeholder shapes first, as World 1's enemies were until 0.10.1, in World 2's palette with the near-black outline: one dominant shape each, told apart from each other, from the returning enemies and from Poncho by silhouette alone, the pale points only for danger and for the not-stompable mark; the final sprites drawn in code and approved from screenshots. **Built (placeholders, `src/ui/mercadoEnemies.ts`, checked by `src/ui/mercadoEnemies.test.ts`):** flat shapes drawn facing right and mirrored for left, each within 2 px of its box, outlined in #070509, the telegraph's red flash 4 steps on and 4 off as World 1's: the Paloma slate #4E4A78 with a teal neck, puffed up wider with its wings raised in its telegraph; the Chapulín leaf green #3E7A3C, low while it crouches and stretched in the air (darkened from #4F8F4C so it is 18 L* from World 2's pink plaster walls, at least the art guide's 15, checked by `src/ui/mercadoEnemies.test.ts`; user, 2026-11-04: "Darken it", Open Question 34); the Trompo wood with a rosa band, a lit patch running round its crown while it spins, its three pale points (2 x 3 px, glaze white) only while it spins, lying on its side when stunned. Their placeholder look was approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`).

### GDD-BOS-01 Boss structure

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: World 1's boss built "as written", the numbers Proposed for the user to adjust after playing; what a lost life does to the fight "As planned"; user, 2026-11-04: World 2's boss "La Estrella", GDD-BOS-03)

Each world ends with a boss in a single-screen arena: Don Zopilote in World 1 (GDD-BOS-02), La Estrella in World 2 (GDD-BOS-03). Three phases, each teaching or testing the world's signature element. Bosses do not drop extra lives; the fight must be doable with the lives the player has left. Checkpoint at the arena start.

- **A boss level (built, details Proposed):** the arena is a level of its own, the last of its world, one screen wide (30 x 17 tiles): `1-4` for World 1 (GDD-BOS-02), and `2-4` for World 2 (GDD-BOS-03). A level with a boss is a boss level, and which boss it holds is read in one place (`levelBoss` and `isBossLevel` in `src/sim/bossLevel.ts`), for the validator and the boss bar. It has no ofrendas, no exit, no hilos and no veladora: no extra lives and no secret. Poncho starts, and respawns after every lost life, at the arena start the level gives (GDD-LIFE-02). The boss takes 3 hits; 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit that defeats him the level clear screen comes up as at an exit (GDD-UI-02): the progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), and the results menu greys "Next level" while no level comes after it: 1-4's "Next level" starts World 2's 2-1 (GDD-UI-02), and 2-4's is greyed while there is no World 3 (`e2e/world2.spec.ts`, `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`).
- **A life lost mid-fight (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"; details Proposed):** the boss keeps the hits he has taken and starts the phase he is in again from its start (the next phase, if a hit's flashing had not ended), with all his fruit gone and every rock back on its spot (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`); La Estrella the same, every tejocote gone (GDD-BOS-03; checked by `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). Running out of lives in the arena restarts the world from its first level with seven lives, as anywhere in the world (GDD-LIFE-05; 1-1 in World 1, 2-1 in World 2): the fight starts afresh, with all three of the boss's hits, when Poncho next reaches the arena (checked for World 1 by `e2e/world1.spec.ts`).
- **The validator's boss-level rule (GDD-TEC-04):** a level with a boss has no ofrendas and no exit and is exactly one screen wide, and the boss's starting point is at least 3 tiles from the spawn (GDD-ENM-01): La Estrella's, the middle of her box, at the start of each of her three phases, since a lost life starts her phase again. It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit, no enemy starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).
- **The boss bar:** the HUD shows the boss's own name ("Don Zopilote", "La Estrella") and the hits the boss has left, a pip for each of the boss's three hits (GDD-UI-01).
- **Each boss's own rules:** a boss's rules may override a general one, and its section says so: Don Zopilote is hit by the claw and fireballs only while he is stuck in the sand (GDD-BOS-02); La Estrella is hit by a bump or the claw, and fireballs do nothing to her (GDD-BOS-03).
- `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/sim/estrella.ts`, `src/sim/bossLevel.ts`, `src/levels/level1-4.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.ts`, `src/levels/validate.ts`, the boss bar in `src/ui/bossBar.ts`.

### GDD-BOS-02 World 1 boss

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: "Don Zopilote as written"; "Placeholders for now", shapes "like the other enemies have"; the speeds, timings and arena filled in as Proposed "for you to adjust after playing"; his rules "As planned", marked below; the placeholder look approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-03: "Approved, merge"; his real art from the design canvas the user approved, user, 2026-11-03: "Ok merge it and implement it!", its details Proposed).

A giant vulture, Don Zopilote, circles the arena and drops cactus fruit. Phase 1: dodge dives. Phase 2: knock rocks onto it with claw swipes. Phase 3: faster dives plus ground hazards. Defeat in 3 hits.

- **The arena, level 1-4 (built, details Proposed):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 1's last level: "Next level" on 1-3's results menu starts it (GDD-UI-02, GDD-WLD-09), and `?level=1-4` opens it. Drawn with World 1's backdrop and ground tiles (GDD-ART-06). The floor is flat (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"): its top is row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit and no sand slope, so World 1's boss does not test the sand slopes, as GDD-BOS-01 asks of each boss. A pillar 4 tiles wide and 3 tall stands at each side, the arena's walls (columns 0 to 3 and 26 to 29, rows 11 to 13, top y = 176): a full jump from the floor beside either one lands on its top and a short hop does not (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A rock rests on the inner edge of each pillar top, its middle at x = 56 and x = 424. Poncho starts on the floor at column 14 (x = 232). No ofrendas, exit, hilos, veladora or other enemies (GDD-BOS-01). `src/levels/level1-4.ts`.
- **Don Zopilote:** a box of 48 x 30 px, three times the Zopilote's (GDD-ENM-02), that ignores tiles. **His look (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-03: "Ok merge it and implement it!", of the design canvas with its key art, lore, pose sheet, crown, tunas and rocks, and palette; the design saved in `assets/characters/don-zopilote/`):** a chubby turkey vulture, the zopilote of the Chihuahuan desert, who crowned himself king of the desert ("El Rey del Desierto"): brown plumage with a cool blue shadow, silvery lilac flight feathers, a fluffy dark ruff, a bare red head, a pale hooked beak and a pale eye under a grumpy brow. Story (Proposed): he has circled over Poncho since the curse took his lives, sure the cat is done for, and drops tunas from the nopales to hurry things along; he holds none of Poncho's lives (GDD-BOS-01). Funny because he is vain, never a caricature (GDD-VIS-04). Three cholla joints stuck in his back, green with pale spines (a jumping cholla he landed in), mark that he cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01, GDD-ART-05): landing on him is a touch like any other (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). His crown, of hammered gold tin with a tuna as its jewel, has a point for each hit he has left: three, then two and one, none once he is defeated, as the boss bar's pips (GDD-UI-01). Drawn in code (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/boss.mjs` into `assets/sprites/boss/don-zopilote.png`, at the scale of Poncho's drawing (2 x 2 art px a drawing pixel), outlined (GDD-ART-05) and only in World 1's colours (GDD-ART-06: 20 of its 24 and the outline, checked by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`); a frame is 75 x 62 game px with his box's middle at its middle, drawn facing right and mirrored when he faces left. His frames (`src/ui/bossPose.ts`): his wings flap, raised for 8 steps and lowered for 8, while he laps or drops, and while he climbs, tilted upwards; are raised through his telegraph, where his beak is open, his eye wide and his talons out; are tucked while he dives, head first, while he sweeps the floor, talons out and beak open, with no flap, while he is hurt (crossed eyes) and while he is defeated (tumbling, then on his back with the three dots, no crown: see his defeat below); and are held lowered while he is stuck (his beak in a sand mound on the floor, tail up, the three dots over him). Only his lowered wings and his two tumbling frames reach below his box's bottom: the lowered wings by 14 px (`BOSS_ART.wingsBelow`), the tumbling frames by 7 and 12 px, shown only while they fit (see his defeat below); every other frame stays above it (checked by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`). While he flaps, his wings stay raised whenever his box's bottom is less than 14 px above the floor, as at the end of a phase 2 drop or the start of a climb from the floor, so nothing of him ever reaches into the floor (the rule checked by `src/ui/bossPose.test.ts`, every frame of a bot's fight through 1-4 by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`, whose bot hits him with a rock during his only phase 2 drop, and the end of a drop in play by `e2e/boss.spec.ts`). The red and white flashes paint every pixel but his outline (`BOSS_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). He follows a fixed pattern with nothing left to chance; only where Poncho is changes it. `BOSS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- **Circling:** he laps an ellipse 152 px to either side of a middle the level gives and 16 px above and below it (x = 240, y = 72 in 1-4), once in 4.0 s (240 steps), starting and ending at its right or left end (x = 392 or 88 in 1-4): from the right end he goes leftwards along its lower half first, from the left end rightwards along its upper half first. The fight starts with him at the right end. He drops a cactus fruit on the 40th, 100th, 160th and 220th step of each lap, its top at his box's bottom: in 1-4, from the right end they fall at x = 316, 108, 164 and 372, from the left end at 164, 372, 316 and 108 (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`). He faces the way he moves across; when he does not move across he keeps the way he faced.
- **Telegraph (GDD-ENM-01):** at the end of each lap he freezes for 0.5 s (30 steps) before his attack, facing Poncho, wings raised and body flashing red, 4 steps on and 4 off. In phases 1 and 3 the attack is a dive, in phase 2 a sweep.
- **Dive (phases 1 and 3):** as the telegraph ends he takes the middle of Poncho's hurtbox, across only, and dives in a straight line to the floor below it (his box's bottom on the floor's top), that x kept between the ends of his lap (88 to 392 in 1-4), so he never clips a pillar. He dives at 180 px/s in phase 1 and at 300 px/s in phase 3. Then he is stuck in the sand, dazed (three pale dots over his head): 1.0 s (60 steps) in phase 1, 0.6 s (36 steps) in phase 3. Then he climbs in a straight line at 120 px/s to the nearer end of his lap (the right one when he is in the middle) and laps from there. In 1-4 a phase 1 dive aimed at the start (x = 232) sticks him in the sand 71 steps after it starts (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`).
- **Sweep (phase 2):** as the telegraph ends he drops straight down at 180 px/s until his box's bottom is on the floor, sweeps along it at 150 px/s to below the other end of his lap, and climbs straight up at 120 px/s to that end, then laps from there; so the sweeps go right to left and left to right in turn. In 1-4 his drop or his sweep reaches Poncho standing anywhere on the floor between the pillars, and neither reaches him on a pillar top away from its inner edge (see "Touching him") (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`): the pillars are where he dodges it.
- **Cactus fruit:** a red prickly pear (a tuna, with pale glochids and a green cut end, squashed flat when it lies; drawn in code into `assets/sprites/boss/fruit.png`, 9 x 9 px over its box) with a box of 8 x 8 px, that falls straight down at 120 px/s from where he dropped it. In phases 1 and 2 it bursts and is gone when it touches a tile. In phase 3 it lies on that tile's top for 3.0 s (180 steps), then it is gone: these lying fruit are phase 3's ground hazards (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"). Touching one, falling or lying, is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from the fruit. The claw and fireballs pass through fruit (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). A fruit has no telegraph of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps (0.7 to 0.9 s) before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below where it was dropped (the first of a lap from the right end, dropped on its 40th step at x = 316, reaches him on the 83rd), longer than the 0.5 s of GDD-ENM-01 (measured by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`; Open Question 20). In 1-4 no fruit falls on a pillar top (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). `FRUIT`.
- **Touching him** is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from his middle, while he laps, telegraphs, dives, drops or sweeps. Stuck in the sand, climbing back, hurt or defeated he does no harm. A step where several things touch Poncho is one hit, pushed away from the first of spike, enemy, Don Zopilote, fruit (GDD-CMB-03; checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4, on a pillar top nothing of his reaches a Poncho standing there in any phase, neither he nor his fruit, while Poncho's middle is at least 5 px in from the pillar's inner edge (x = 59 or less on the left pillar, 421 or more on the right); nearer that edge (he can stand with his middle up to just under 6 px past it), the dive or the drop beside that pillar, his box's side flush with the pillar's wall, reaches him (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`; Open Question 19).
- **Rocks:** a rock is a 12 x 12 px box resting on its spot, drawn as an outlined lump of red sandstone 13 x 13 px (`assets/sprites/boss/rock.png`). A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) whose box overlaps a resting rock knocks it the way the swipe faces, at 90 px/s across and 150 px/s up, from the next step; it then falls at 1500 px/s², and it breaks when it touches a tile, falls out of the level or touches Don Zopilote. A broken rock is back on its spot 2.0 s (120 steps) later. A rock never hurts Poncho (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4 a rock knocked inwards off the left pillar breaks on the floor on its 23rd step, about 35 px out from where it rested, and one knocked back over its pillar breaks on the pillar top (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts` and `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). `ROCK`.
- **Hitting him (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned": a claw swipe or a fireball only while he is stuck in the sand after a dive, a rock knocked onto him with a claw swipe, never a stomp; details Proposed):** a flying rock that touches him is a hit, in any phase, unless he is hurt or defeated. A claw swipe or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) that touches him is a hit only while he is stuck in the sand, so only in phases 1 and 3; a fireball that touches him is used up either way, unless he is defeated, and passes through him then. Stomps never hit him. A hit on him is settled before his touch, so his own touch on the step that hits him does not hurt Poncho. A step that costs Poncho a life does not hit him (as such a step never reaches an exit, GDD-WLD-03).
- **Phase 2 in 1-4 (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`):** standing on a pillar top facing the middle (x = 40 or 440), a claw press knocks its rock onto him when it comes in a window of steps: on the pillar he drops beside, from the 12th to the 35th step after his drop starts (24 steps); on the pillar he climbs beside, from 36 steps before his climb starts to 28 steps after it (65 steps).
- **After a hit:** he is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, his body flashing white, 4 steps on and 4 off, and can neither be hit nor hurt anyone; then he climbs to the nearer end of his lap and starts the next phase. The third hit defeats him: his fruit is gone at once, he falls at 120 px/s, flashing white, until his box's bottom is on the floor, and 1.0 s (60 steps) after that hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-UI-02).
- **His defeat (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Crown pops off, he flops"):** on the hit that defeats him his crown, by then just the gold band with its tuna jewel, pops off his head, its bottom middle starting on top of his head in the frame he shows on the hit's step: tumbling, when he has room for it above the floor, 15 px from his middle on the side he faces and 4.5 px below his middle; lying on the floor, 11 px to the other side (he lies on his back) and 5 px below (`BOSS_ART.headTumble`, `headLying`, measured from the art by `scripts/art/boss.test.mjs`). It moves 45 px/s across towards the arena's middle (rightwards from the middle itself) and starts 180 px/s up under 1800 px/s², rising 7.5 px; it bounces once on the floor at 0.3 of its speed and rests there at its second touch (a step that puts it exactly on the floor is a touch), its frame tilted on every other 4 steps while in the air (`BOSS_DEFEAT` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `bossCrown` in `src/sim/boss.ts`; drawn 12 x 8 px from `assets/sprites/boss/crown.png`, its bottom on the floor). In 1-4, hit stuck at the start (x 232) it first touches the floor on the 14th step after the hit and rests on the 18th, after a bounce of 0.9 px; from wherever he can be defeated (his middle from y 56, the top of his lap, down to the floor) it rests by the 47th, before the clear screen (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts` and `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). Falling, he tumbles, flashing white, turning between two tumbling frames every 6 steps; a tumbling frame shows only while it fits above the floor (the one 12 px deep, the other 7 px), else the next one that does; on the floor he lies on his back, the three dazed dots over his head (`src/ui/bossPose.ts`). No frame reaches into the floor.
- **Played through:** a bot that waits at the start, runs 64 px past where a dive aims, claws him while he is stuck, climbs the left pillar in phase 2 and claws its rock when he drops beside it, beats him with all seven lives in under 1500 steps (25 s) (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A player's first try takes longer, and with the lives carried through World 1 (GDD-LIFE-05) a player may reach him with fewer than seven.
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02; his own voice, user, 2026-11-04: "Telegraph + hit + defeat"):** real vultures have no song and can only hiss and grunt, so he does: a raspy hiss at the start of every telegraph, a warning before each attack; a grunt on a hit that leaves him hurt; a long deflating hiss on the hit that defeats him. His landing in the sand after a dive plays the land. Generated in code like the other sounds (GDD-AUD-02).

---

### GDD-BOS-03 World 2 boss

Status: Decided (La Estrella, a star piñata boss in her own arena, user, 2026-11-04: "La Estrella"; a piñata boss met only in her own arena, under a boss bar, is fine with the user, which settles GDD-PWR-05's "never hide a lethal surprise" for her). Her rules, numbers and arena are Proposed. Built with placeholder art, as Don Zopilote's was (`src/sim/estrella.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.ts`, `src/ui/estrellaPose.ts`; tested in `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`); her placeholder look approved from screenshots with World 2's (user, 2026-11-04, `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`), her final art still to come. Three choices the main session made during the build (2026-11-04) are Proposed, for the user to confirm (Open Question 33): when she can be hit, her phase 3 drop through the awnings, and her telegraph's shake (below).

La Estrella de Siete Picos, a giant seven-pointed posada piñata hanging from a trolley on a cable stretched between two rooftops over the plaza, in the one-screen arena 2-4. In the posada tradition her seven points stand for the seven deadly sins and breaking her is good's triumph over them; here the curse of the story seed (GDD-VIS-01) has got into her. Her seven paper cones echo Poncho's seven triangles: each hit tears some off (7, then 5, then 3, then none), so the fight's progress is drawn on her as well as on the boss bar. **Her one idea:** she is hit the way any piñata is, by a head bump from below or the claw (GDD-PWR-05), but she hangs too high to reach from the floor, so every hit is earned with World 2's elements, a rebound off an awning (GDD-WLD-10) or a jump from a cordel (GDD-WLD-11); and like a real piñata on its rope she is yanked up and down (the *jalón*). Three phases, each teaching or testing World 2's elements (GDD-BOS-01), and three hits. She copies no other game's boss.

- **The arena, level 2-4, "La Plaza" (the name Proposed, Open Question 26):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 2's last level: "Next level" on 2-3's results menu starts it with 2-3's lives (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02), and `?level=2-4` opens it (`e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). The boss-level rule holds it (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-TEC-04): no ofrendas, exit, hilos or veladora, and the spawn is the only respawn. The floor is flat, its top at row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit. **Three whole awnings,** each 4 tiles wide with its top at y = 176 (48 px above the floor, so Poncho walks under them): x = 48 to 112 (columns 3 to 6), 208 to 272 (13 to 16) and 368 to 432 (23 to 26). **Two cordeles** between them, their tops at y = 136 (40 px above the awnings): x = 112 to 208 (columns 7 to 12) and 272 to 368 (17 to 22). The cable runs across the top at y = 8, from rooftop to rooftop (scenery), and her rope hangs from its trolley. Poncho starts on the floor at x = 240 (column 15, under the middle awning); she starts phase 1 high over x = 400, phase 2 over 320 and phase 3 over 400, her box's middle 256, 215 and 256 px from the spawn, more than 3 tiles (GDD-ENM-01; measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`, and checked by the validator, GDD-TEC-04). From the spawn a full jump rises through the middle awning and lands on it, and held through the landing it rebounds (measured). Drawn with World 2's art, the plaza with its kiosco in place of the rooftops (GDD-ART-06), with 1 decor item, a talavera pot (`src/ui/plants.test.ts`); its medal is gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, else silver, as in 1-4 (GDD-PRG-03).
- **Her body:** a box of 32 x 32 px, the clay pot at her core, drawn as a star about 56 px across; her cones and streamers outside the box are paper and harmless, so a near miss feels fair. She cannot be stomped: her three top cones have pale tips (GDD-CMB-01), and landing on her is a touch like any other. Touching her (Poncho's hurtbox on her box; boxes that only share an edge do not touch) is a hit (GDD-CMB-03) while she glides, telegraphs, drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests, rises, hangs or is yanked; hurt or defeated she does no harm. Her box's bottom hangs at one of three heights: high, y = 40, her box's top then at the cable (y = 8); low, y = 112 in phase 1 and y = 72 in phases 2 and 3. Wherever she aims and however she sways, her box stays on the screen and under the cable and never ends a drop inside a tile (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`).
- **Hitting her** (in any phase, only while she glides, telegraphs, hangs or is yanked: never while she drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests on the floor or rises from it, when her box is low enough for a jump or a claw from the floor; the main session's reading, 2026-11-04, of the user's "only reachable from awning rebounds or cordel jumps", Proposed, Open Question 33): a **bump**, Poncho rising with his head reaching her underside, his hurtbox's top at or below her box's bottom on the step before and above it after, and his body box overlapping her box's width (the piñata's rule, GDD-PWR-05, and the cactus buddy's from-below test, GDD-ENM-02), which stops his rise there as a piñata does, his head flush under her, so that, still holding jump, he falls back to the awning and the chain goes on: after a bump off a tier 1 rebound the next landing is tier 2 (GDD-WLD-10; `src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`); or a **claw** swipe whose box overlaps her (GDD-CMB-02), in mid-air beside her. In no phase does a jump, a claw or a jump with a claw from the floor hit her, though such moves reach her box low in her drops, bounces, rests and rises (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). A hit on her is settled before her touch, so the step that hits her does not hurt him, and a step that costs Poncho a life does not hit her (as in GDD-BOS-02). **Fireballs** are used up on her and do nothing, since a piñata is not burnt open: her rule overrides GDD-PWR-03's "1 damage per fireball" (GDD-BOS-01); once she is defeated they pass through her.
- **Reach (measured over the real arena by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`, and against each height by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`, L-003):** with jump held all the way, his hurtbox's top peaks at y = 136.56 from a floor jump, 88.56 from a jump off an awning, 67.92, 47.73 and 24.79 from held rebounds of tiers 1, 2 and 3 off an awning, and 48.56 from a jump off a cordel. So from the floor no jump reaches her at any height; a jump from an awning reaches her phase 1 low height (112) only; a tier 1 or tier 2 rebound reaches both her low heights (112 and 72), tier 1 barely; a jump from a cordel reaches her phases 2 and 3 low height (72) but not her phase 1 low, since standing on the cordel his head is already above 112, beside her, not under her; and only a tier 3 rebound reaches her at her high height: the expert's shortcut, never needed. (The plan had a tier 2 rebound or a cordel jump for the low height of phases 2 and 3; tier 1 reaches it too.)
- **Her cycle, in every phase:** she **glides** along the cable at her high height to her next drop spot; **telegraphs** for 0.5 s (30 steps, GDD-ENM-01): she stops, her body flashes red 4 steps on and 4 off, and her body and her trolley shake 2 px to either side on the cable, flipping every 4 steps (`ESTRELLA.shakePx`: the main session's choice, 2026-11-04, Proposed, in place of the planned zigzag of her rope, which has no length up there; her streamers are not drawn yet); then the **jalón**, a drop straight down until her box's bottom meets the surface under her middle, a whole awning's top or the floor (she passes through cordeles: they hold a cat, not a clay pot); in phase 3 she tears through the awnings instead (below). Every drop follows its 30 steps of telegraph high on the cable and falls for at least 23 steps, so it is seen to fall (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). Dropped onto an **awning**, she is thrown straight back up to her low height at 240 px/s (the tarp's dip under her is not drawn yet). Dropped onto the **floor**, she thuds, **rests** 0.3 s (18 steps) while fruit rolls out of her (below), and is pulled up to her low height at 120 px/s. She **hangs** low for her phase's window, the "dale, dale, dale" moment, then is **yanked** back up to her high height at 180 px/s and glides on.
- **Tejocotes:** her filling, the small orange fruit of the posada piñata: 8 x 8 px, rolling out of her middle on the floor at each thud, a pair at a time, one each way (`TEJOCOTE.pairs`, a pair in phases 1 and 2, two in phase 3, the second 24 steps after the first), at 90 px/s along the floor, gone at a wall (from x = 160, on the 105th step leftwards, the 211th rightwards; measured by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`). Touching one is a hit (GDD-CMB-03); a claw swipe or a fireball (used up) bursts one. A short hop (about 15 px, GDD-MOV-02) clears one (worked out by hand), and on an awning or a cordel Poncho is clear of them. Her defeat clears them at once; a hurt does not. `TEJOCOTE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `size` 8, `rollSpeed` 90, `secondPairSteps` 24, `pairs` [1, 1, 2].
- **Phase 1, "Arriba" (teaches the awning):** her drop spots are x = 80 and x = 400, the middles of the side awnings, in turn: she starts high over 400 and glides at 80 px/s. She drops at 300 px/s onto the awning, which throws her up to y = 112; she hangs there 2.0 s (120 steps), still, then is yanked up and glides to the other side. Measured (`src/sim/estrella.test.ts`): from the fight's start she telegraphs over x = 80 on step 240, lands on the left awning on step 298, hangs on step 314 and is back at the cable on step 458. She never reaches the floor, so a Poncho on the floor is never touched in phase 1; on the awning under her drop her box spans x = 64 to 96 (384 to 416 over the right one), and with his 10 px hurtbox a Poncho standing at x = 60 or 420 is reached and one at 58 or 422 is not, nor one on a side awning's outer end in phases 1 and 2 (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). The intended play: wait beside the awning while she drops, jump onto it holding jump, and the tier 1 rebound bumps her; or jump from the settled awning. The awning she lands on is the awning that reaches her.
- **Phase 2, "Vaivén" (teaches the cordeles):** her drop spots become x = 160 and x = 320, over the cordeles' middles, where there is no awning: she drops at 300 px/s through the cordel to the floor, rests, and one tejocote rolls out each way; she is pulled up to y = 72, hangs there 1.5 s (90 steps) swaying 8 px each way on a 1.0 s sine, and is yanked up. She glides at 100 px/s; the phase starts with her high over 320, so her first drop is at 160. Measured (`src/sim/estrella.test.ts`): from the phase's start she telegraphs over x = 160 on step 96, is on the floor on step 163, rests 18 steps, hangs on step 257, and her yank reaches the cable in 11 steps. Her drop through a cordel hits a Poncho standing on it under her. The intended play: jump or bounce up onto a cordel, wait beside the drop spot, then step under her as she hangs and jump; or chain held rebounds on an awning and drift under her.
- **Phase 3, "Remolino" (tests both):** her drop spot is where Poncho is: as the telegraph ends she takes the middle of his hurtbox, across only, kept between x = 56 and 424 (Don Zopilote's aim, GDD-BOS-02), and drops at 360 px/s in a straight line to the floor under that spot. **She tears straight through the awnings** (they stay whole) to the floor (`ESTRELLA.tearsAwnings` [false, false, true]; the main session's choice, 2026-11-04, Proposed, in place of the plan's throw up from an awning), so standing under an awning is no shelter: standing still under the one she aims at, at x = 80, 240 or 400, her drop reaches him (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). On the floor she thuds and two tejocotes roll each way, the second pair 0.4 s (24 steps) after the first, and she rests and rises to y = 72. She hangs 1.0 s (60 steps) swaying 16 px each way, glides at 120 px/s, and her glide always heads for the spot above Poncho. The intended play: keep moving between awnings and cordeles, make her drop where he is not, then get under her within the second, from a cordel or with a tier 2 chain; or the expert's tier 3 bump while she glides.
- **After a hit:** she is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, flashing white 4 steps on and 4 off, harmless and unhittable; two cones tear off (three in phase 3: `ESTRELLA.cones` [7, 5, 3, 0], the bottom ones first, the pale top three last). Then she is yanked straight up from where she is to her high height and starts the next phase from its drop spot nearest her (hurt over x = 400, her phase 2 starts at 320; measured by `src/sim/estrella.test.ts`). Not built yet: the torn cones drifting down as paper confetti (scenery).
- **A life lost mid-fight (as GDD-BOS-01 says for every boss):** she keeps her hits and starts her phase again from its start, high over that phase's first drop spot (a life lost while she is hurt starts the next phase); every tejocote is gone; Poncho respawns at the spawn (`src/sim/estrellafight.test.ts`, `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). Running out of lives sends the run back to 2-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05), and the fight starts afresh, with all three hits, when Poncho next reaches the arena.
- **Defeat, the third hit:** her rope snaps and the empty clay pot falls at 120 px/s to the floor and lies there, her tejocotes gone at once; 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-UI-02). 2-4 is World 2's last level, so its results menu greys "Next level" while there is no World 3 (GDD-UI-02; `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`). Not built yet: her burst in a shower of paper and candy (scenery only: a boss drops no lives, GDD-BOS-01).
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02):** a hit on her plays the stomp, the end of her drop, on the floor or an awning, the land, and her defeat Poncho's meow; nothing else is new.
- **Boss bar (GDD-UI-01):** "La Estrella", the same in every language (her name's key `estrella` in `TEXT`, `src/ui/text.ts`), and three pips, one emptied by each hit (`src/ui/bossBar.ts`). Gliding high she can pass behind it, and the HUD (the lives, the boss bar) is drawn on top of her; her top cone may reach past the screen's top (Open Question 33).
- **Played through (measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`):** a bot beats her from the spawn with all seven lives in 1009 steps (under 1800, about 17 s), hitting her on steps 344, 663 and 949. In phase 1 it waits on the floor under the left awning and, when she hangs over it, jumps onto the awning holding jump through the landing: the tier 1 rebound bumps her. In phase 2 it jumps from the left awning onto the left cordel and waits at x = 127, clear of her drop at 160; when she hangs it steps under her and a cordel jump bumps her. In phase 3 it waits on the middle awning so that she aims there, runs right as her telegraph ends and stops 26 px from her line while her drop tears through the awning beside it, jumps up onto the end of the right cordel as she rests, above the tejocotes, and a cordel jump bumps her when her sway brings her within 12 px across. A player's first try takes longer, and with the lives carried through World 2 (GDD-LIFE-05) a player may reach her with fewer than seven.
- `ESTRELLA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`: `width` 32, `height` 32, `highBottom` 40, `lowBottom` [112, 72, 72], `glideSpeed` [80, 100, 120], `telegraphSteps` 30, `dropSpeed` [300, 300, 360], `awningThrow` 240, `tearsAwnings` [false, false, true], `restSteps` 18, `riseSpeed` 120, `hangSteps` [120, 90, 60], `swayPx` [0, 8, 16], `swayPeriodSteps` 60, `yankSpeed` 180, `hurtSteps` 60, `defeatSteps` 60, `fallSpeed` 120, `hits` 3, `aimMinX` 56, `aimMaxX` 424, `cones` [7, 5, 3, 0], `coneTip` 28 (a star about 56 px across), `cableY` 8, `shakePx` 2, `flashSteps` 4.

## 10. Progression and meta

### GDD-PRG-01 Unlocks

Status: Proposed

- Clearing worlds unlocks permanent **abilities** in order (these are not the temporary power-ups of section 6): Glide (GDD-MOV-07, after World 2), Wall cling (GDD-MOV-08, after World 3). Further powers are open (GDD-MVP-02). Until the unlocks are built, a level turns an ability on itself: the sandbox has the glide and the wall cling on.
- **The glide's unlock is deferred (Proposed):** it is not part of World 2's first round (GDD-WLD-10 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-BOS-03): clearing 2-4 unlocks nothing yet. The glide and the wall cling are off in every World 2 level, as in World 1's (GDD-MVP-02). When the unlock is built, whether the glide then works in every level, replays of World 1 and World 2 included, is Open Question 27.
- Hilos buy cosmetics for the poncho (patterns and colours). Cosmetics never change gameplay.

### GDD-PRG-02 Save system

Status: Proposed

- Autosave to browser `localStorage` after each level and each checkpoint. Versioned schema with migration. Built: at the level clear (see "Progress", "World 1's run" and "World 2's run"), at each checkpoint (see "Saved run") and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (see "Progress", "World 1's run" and "World 2's run").
- Saves: world progress, hilos total, unlocked powers, cosmetics, settings, assist flags.
- Save never blocks the main thread and survives storage being unavailable (play without saving).
- **Built (details Proposed):** the settings and whether the tutorial has been started, as `{ "version": 1, "settings": { "master": 0-10, "effects": 0-10, "language": "en", "es" or "de", "buttonOpacity": "low", "medium" or "high", "layout": "standard" or "mirrored" }, "progress": { "tutorialPlayed": true or false, "worldLives": { "1": 0 to 7, "2": 0 to 7 }, "worldAt": { "1": "1-2", "2": "2-1" } } }` (`worldLives` and `worldAt`: see "World 1's run" and "World 2's run") under the key `poncho-siete-vidas` in local storage, written at each change of either and read when the game opens (GDD-UI-02). A save without `progress`, as the settings-only saves before it, or with anything but `true` there, reads as not played, and one without `language` takes the browser's (GDD-UI-02), so the version stays 1. Saving one part keeps the other. Where the browser refuses storage, the tutorial counts as played for the rest of the visit only. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **Progress (built; user, 2026-10-31: each hilo once, the best medal, the best time and "cleared" per level, saved at the level clear; its hilos also when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up, user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them"; details Proposed):** the save gets `"levels": { "0-1": { "hilos": "0110…", "medal": "bronze", "silver" or "gold", "bestSteps": 8040, "cleared": true }, … }`, one entry per real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and the boss arena 1-4, whose `hilos` text is empty since it has no hilos, and World 2's 2-1, 2-2, 2-3 and its arena 2-4, empty too; the test levels, the sandbox, the slopes level, `lab-2m` and `lab-2e`, are never saved; `src/levels/levels.test.ts`). `hilos` is one character per hilo of the level, in the level's order, `1` for collected. `bestSteps` is the fastest clear time in simulation steps (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). It is written on the step Poncho reaches the exit (GDD-WLD-03), or in a boss level on the step its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), and its hilos also on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (below); a run that quits or retries keeps nothing of its own. At the clear every hilo collected in the run is added to the saved ones (never removed), the medal is kept if it is better than the saved one (gold over silver over bronze), the time if it is shorter, and `cleared` becomes true; nothing else of the level's entry changes. On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in every real level (user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them": "Saved for good when the Siete vidas gastadas screen comes up, in every level; that's what GDD-LIFE-05 says."; this replaces the rule of 2026-10-31 that only a clear saved a run's hilos), the hilos collected in the run are added to the saved ones in the same way, and nothing else of the level changes: the medal, the time and `cleared` stay as saved, and a level with nothing saved gets an entry with no medal, no time and `cleared` false. So the hilos stay when the restart plays another level (GDD-LIFE-05, Decided: "Hilos collected stay"). `recordHilos` in `src/sim/save.ts`. A save without `levels`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not readable, reads as nothing saved for that level: a `hilos` text whose length is not the level's hilo count, or with another character than `0` and `1`, reads as no hilo; a medal other than the three names as none; a `bestSteps` that is not a whole number above 0 as none; and `cleared` only as true when it is exactly true. So the version stays 1. `cleared` decides what "Start" plays only for a save that keeps no run for the world played (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"), and whether 1-4 is cleared decides whether "Start" plays World 2 (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"); the world map will read it too. Where the browser refuses storage, the progress counts for the rest of the visit only, like the tutorial flag. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 1's run (built; user, 2026-11-02: "Save the lives": "The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there."; "Follow the run": "The save remembers where your World 1 run is: Start plays the next level in order of the current run (1-1 after a game over, then 1-2, ...). Clears still count for medals.", which replaced "first level not cleared" for "Start"; details Proposed):** `progress` gets `"worldLives": { "1": 4 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-2" }`, each per world by its number: `worldLives` the lives the save keeps for the world's run (GDD-LIFE-05), a whole number from 0 to 7, and `worldAt` the level its run has reached, the id of one of the world's real levels. The worlds with a real level have their runs kept, World 1's as below and World 2's the same way (see "World 2's run"); a test level adds no world (`SAVED_WORLDS` in `src/levels/index.ts`). Both change on the same two steps, and on no other. On the step Poncho reaches the exit of a World 1 level (GDD-WLD-03), or the level clear screen of the boss arena 1-4 comes up (GDD-BOS-01), `worldLives` becomes the lives he has there, the lives "Next level" carries (GDD-UI-02), and `worldAt` the next level of the world in the game's order (1-2 after 1-1, 1-3 after 1-2, 1-4 after 1-3); after the world's last level, the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), it is the world's first level, 1-1: the run is over and a new one starts (Proposed), though "Start" now plays World 2's run instead (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"). On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in a World 1 level, `worldLives` becomes 0 and `worldAt` 1-1: the world restarts at 1-1 with seven however that is reached (GDD-LIFE-05). A life lost or won, a checkpoint, quitting, "Retry" and closing the game leave both as they were, so quitting a level and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear (Open Question 18). The tutorial, outside the seven worlds, and the test levels write neither. "Start" reads them (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"). A clear still counts for the medal, the best time and the hilos, and `cleared` is still saved (see "Progress"), but it no longer decides what "Start" plays while the run is kept. A save without `worldLives`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not a whole number from 0 to 7, keeps no lives for that world, which reads as seven; a save without `worldAt`, or with an entry that names no real level of that world (a test level, the tutorial, another world's level, anything that is no level id), keeps no run for it, and "Start" goes by the levels cleared (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"); the version stays 1. Where the browser refuses storage both last for the rest of the visit, like the progress. `worldLivesAfter` and `worldAtAfter` in `src/levels/index.ts`, `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 2's run (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world"; checked by `src/levels/levels.test.ts` and `e2e/world2.spec.ts`):** World 2's run is kept as World 1's is, under the world's number: `"worldLives": { "1": 4, "2": 6 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-1", "2": "2-3" }`, `worldAt["2"]` the id of one of World 2's real levels (2-1, 2-2, 2-3 or the arena 2-4). They change on the same two steps as World 1's: at the exit of a World 2 level, or as 2-4's level clear screen comes up, the lives there and the next level of World 2 (2-2 after 2-1, 2-3 after 2-2, 2-4 after 2-3, and 2-1 after 2-4, a new run); as the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in a World 2 level, 0 lives and 2-1. 1-4's clear writes World 1's run as before (its lives, and 1-1) and nothing of World 2's: "Start" goes to World 2 because 1-4 is held as cleared (see "Progress"), and without a World 2 run kept it goes by World 2's levels cleared, as for World 1 (2-1 always with seven), so a save from before World 2 that cleared 1-4 goes to 2-1 with seven (GDD-UI-02, "Which level, with two worlds"). World 2's levels get entries in `levels` and can hold the saved run like any real level. A save without World 2's keys, or with a value that names no real World 2 level, keeps no World 2 run, so the version stays 1.
- **Saved run (built; user, 2026-10-31: resumed by a "Continue" row; it keeps the lives, the run's hilos, the clock and the used ofrendas; dropped at the level clear and by the Siete vidas gastadas screen, not by quitting or by starting another level; details Proposed):** on the step the checkpoint moves to a later ofrenda (GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-03) in a real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2 or 1-3, and World 2's 2-1, 2-2 or 2-3, never a test level; the boss arenas 1-4 and 2-4 have no ofrenda, so no run is ever saved there, GDD-BOS-01), the save gets `"run": { "level": "0-1", "checkpoint": 1, "lives": 5, "livesIn": 7, "used": ["ofrenda:…", …], "hilos": "0110…", "clock": 4210 }`: the level, the position of that ofrenda in the level's order, the lives after its touch, the lives the run started with (GDD-PRG-03 counts the lives lost from them), every ofrenda and veladora used so far in the run (each restores a life once, so a resumed run gives none again), the hilos collected in the run (one character per hilo, as in "Progress", the saved ones included) and the clock (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). There is one saved run at most: a checkpoint reached in any run, of any level, replaces it. It is dropped on the step Poncho reaches the exit or a boss level's clear screen comes up (where "Progress" is written) and on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in a real level only: a test level never drops it; quitting to the title, "Retry", starting another level and closing the game keep it. Picking "Continue" (GDD-UI-02) resumes it: the level starts (at once, as the next level does, when it is not the one behind the title), with the lives, the used ofrendas and veladora, the clock and the run's hilos as saved (added to the hilos saved for good, which stay faded, GDD-WLD-04), and Poncho comes in at that ofrenda by the respawn flip of a restart (GDD-LIFE-05: it cannot be skipped, the meow plays), in Base with no keys, and everything else at its start, as after a lost life. In 0-1 the tutorial tips show again for the stretch Poncho is in, as on a fresh start (user, 2026-11-01: "show it again"; GDD-UI-05). A saved run that does not read (an unknown level, a checkpoint that is not one of its ofrendas, lives that are not a whole number from 1 to 7, a `hilos` text of the wrong length, a clock that is not a whole number of 0 or more) reads as none; `used` keeps only texts. A `livesIn` that is not a whole number from 1 to 7, as in a run saved before it (which started with seven), reads as 7. Where the browser refuses storage it lasts for the rest of the visit, like the rest of the progress. `src/sim/save.ts`.

### GDD-PRG-03 Score and medals

Status: Proposed. Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30); the score is optional and can be cut.

Score from hilos, enemies and time. Level medals: Bronze (clear), Silver (all hilos), Gold (all hilos and at most 3 lives lost in the level; user, 2026-11-02: "Lives lost in the level": "Gold if you lose at most 3 lives in that level, whatever you came in with."). Última Vida doubles hilo score (GDD-LIFE-04).

- **Medals (built; kept, user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the medal is shown on the level clear screen (GDD-UI-02). "All hilos" counts every hilo of the level collected, those taken before a restart after the last life included, since they are kept (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-LIFE-05); a level without hilos has them all. "Lives lost in the level" is the lives the run started with (those carried into the level, GDD-LIFE-05, or seven) less the triangles still whole as Poncho reaches the exit, or in a boss level as its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), so a life restored by an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (GDD-LIFE-03; Open Question 18), and a level started with seven gives gold for 4 or more lives at the exit, as before. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from its seven, as the clock does (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"), and a run resumed by "Continue" counts from the lives it started with (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"). The boss arena 1-4 has no hilos (GDD-BOS-01), so it has them all: its medal is Gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, else Silver, never Bronze (entered with seven, Gold with 4 lives or more left at its clear; entered with five, with 2 or more; measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`), and so is La Estrella's arena 2-4's (`src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`). 3 is `MEDAL.goldLivesLost` in `src/config/tuning.ts` (measured by `src/sim/run.test.ts` and `src/sim/results.test.ts`). Since progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), every hilo saved from an earlier run counts too, so Silver and Gold can be built up over runs; the best medal is kept per level. `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Not built yet:** the score, and Última Vida's double hilo score (Open Question 10).

---

## 11. UI and accessibility

### GDD-UI-01 HUD

Status: Proposed

- Top-left: seven triangles (GDD-LIFE-01), filled = life, outline = lost. Lives fill from the left, so a lost life empties the rightmost filled triangle. Filled and outline differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). Next to them, a small icon of the current power-up state (GDD-PWR-01). After it, one small key per key carried (GDD-WLD-05).
- Top-right: hilo counter. On touch the pause button takes the corner (GDD-UI-02), so the counter goes left of it.
- **Hilo counter (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: collected/total):** a small skein (the hilo pickup's look, in yellow) and then the hilos collected and the level's total, as "12/20", in 8 px pale monospace text. Its right edge is 8 px from the screen's right edge, as the lives are 8 px from the left, and its top is level with the lives. On a touch device it ends 6 px left of the pause button instead, wherever that button has grown or moved to (GDD-CTL-03, safe-area insets). In the boss arenas 1-4 and 2-4, which have no hilos, it reads "0/0" (Open Question 21). `src/ui/hiloCounter.ts`.
- **Boss bar (built in a boss level, details Proposed):** at the top middle of the screen, the boss's name ("Don Zopilote" in 1-4, "La Estrella" in 2-4, GDD-BOS-03; the same in every language, GDD-UI-02) in 8 px pale text (`#fff3dc`) with a 2 px dark outline (`#2a1f2e`, `BOSS_BAR.nameStroke` and `nameStrokePx`), so it reads on any sky, World 2's pale one and La Estrella's cable included (user, 2026-11-04: "Dark outline", Open Question 34; checked by `e2e/boss.spec.ts` and `e2e/estrella.spec.ts`), its middle 10 px from the top, and under it three pips 6 px square and 4 px apart, their top 18 px from the top, centred together on the screen's middle (x = 227 to 253): one filled (red `#b94842` with a 1 px pale edge) for each hit the boss has left, from the left, and a grey outline (`#9ba3c4`, 1 px) for each hit taken, so they differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). It is there for the whole level, the three outlines of a defeated boss included, and drawn with the rest of the HUD: above the pause, level clear and *Siete vidas gastadas* screens, under the title screen's veil. Nothing else is drawn there: the lives are top left, the hilo counter and the pause button top right. A level without a boss has none. Each boss has its name and its pips in one place (`bossBar` in `src/ui/bossBar.ts`, checked by `src/ui/bossBar.test.ts`); La Estrella can glide behind the bar, which is drawn on top of her (GDD-BOS-03). `BOSS_BAR` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/bossBar.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- Minimal, semi-transparent, hidden during cutscenes. On touch, the touch controls keep clear of the safe-area insets (built, GDD-CTL-03). The HUD should keep clear of them too (user, 2026-11-02: "Later, own PR"); until then the lives, the hilo counter and the boss bar keep their places, the counter moving only with the pause button, so on a screen of about 16:9 with a cutout on the left the first triangles lie under it. On a landscape iPhone nothing of the HUD is covered: the side notches lie in the letterbox bars and there is no top inset.

### GDD-UI-02 Menus and flow

Status: Proposed

Boot, title, world map, level, pause, results, settings. Fully operable by touch, keyboard and gamepad. Pause happens automatically if the tab loses focus or the device rotates to portrait.

- **Pause in a level (built; the button top-right, auto-pause on focus and portrait and resume with the pause key or a tap decided by the user 2026-10-22, at first resume-only, a menu since 2026-10-23, details Proposed):** Escape or P (GDD-CTL-01), or the touch pause button, pauses. The pause button sits in the top-right corner, 6 px from both edges (of the safe area, where an inset reaches into the picture, GDD-CTL-03), 24 px, growing like the other buttons so it is never under 48 CSS px (`TOUCH.pauseSize` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); it shows only on touch devices, drawn as two upright bars. The level stands still under a dim layer with "Paused" and the pause menu below it. The HUD and the touch buttons stay on top.
- **Resume:** Escape or P again (each press counts once, on any keyboard layout), the pause button, a tap or click anywhere but on a menu row, or picking "Resume" from the pause menu. The game keys (jump, claw, Fuego, moving) do not resume by themselves, though Space and Z pick the chosen menu item and Up, W, Down and S choose one; on touch any tap off the menu rows does, on a game button too, but that tap is no game press. A button pressed while paused (the Jump button under the tap that resumed, a key pressed while the game stood still, a Left or Right key or button held through the resume, a finger on the slide stick, GDD-CTL-03) does nothing until it is released and pressed again, also on the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05), wherever the finger is (the Resume row reaches into the stick's lower quarter, left or, mirrored, right); a key held at the pause and pressed again while paused is such a press too, and a quick tap of Left or Right while paused counts for nothing after the resume; a button held since before the pause stays held, so a run or a jump carries on.
- **Pause menu (built; a two-item menu decided by the user 2026-10-23, a third item, "Settings", 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** under "Paused", three items, "Resume", "Settings" and "Quit to title", in 12 px text, centred, one under the other. Each item is a row 160 px wide and 28 px tall, the first one's top 136 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below; on a small screen the rows grow so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`PAUSE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The chosen item is pale with a ">" in front of it, the others grey. The menu opens with "Resume" chosen, every time the game pauses. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the item above and Down or S the item below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item. Each press counts once, on any keyboard layout, and the presses of one frame count in order, so Down, Down and then Enter in one frame quits (they are read from the browser's own key events, like Escape and P). A tap or click on an item's row picks that item at once, whichever is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the pause menu with "Settings" chosen. There is no confirmation for quitting (user, 2026-10-23). While the phone is upright the menu is hidden and nothing on it can be chosen or picked; turned sideways again, the item chosen before is still chosen (the menu did not open again).
- **Quit to title:** picking "Quit to title" brings back the title screen, waiting for a press as when the game opens. The level behind it is a fresh run of the level "Start" plays (see "Which level"), as when the game opens (user, 2026-10-23): the lives "Start" gives it (seven, or the lives World 1 keeps, or the lives World 2 keeps, see "Which level" and "Which level, with two worlds"; a triangle the run quit had filled and the fresh one has not shows no crack, and a last life pulses from the level's start, GDD-LIFE-04), every ofrenda unused and the respawn point at the level start, every hilo not saved yet back (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-PRG-02), Poncho in Base with no keys, and every piñata, door, fragile wall, taco, enemy, crumbling tile, moving platform and moving block back at its start. Poncho stands at the level start with no respawn flip and no invulnerability, and no sound plays. The press that quit does not start the level: the button that picked counts as already held on the title screen, and an Enter press, tap or click in the same frame does not count there.
- **Automatic pause:** the tab or window losing focus or being hidden pauses; it never resumes by itself. A touch device whose screen is upright pauses (the screen's orientation decides, or the window's shape where the browser has none), and the pause menu gives way to "Turn your device sideways", in 12 px grey text, larger than the 8 px prompts since the whole level is drawn small; no key, button or tap resumes until it is sideways again, and then it stays paused until the player resumes. A desktop window made taller than wide does not pause, nor does a touchscreen laptop's, whose screen stays landscape. `src/sim/pause.ts`.
- **Title screen (built; the level behind a veil with the placeholder Poncho decided by the user 2026-10-23, the prompt replaced by the title menu 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** the game opens on it. The level "Start" plays (see "Which level" below) is drawn standing still (no simulation step runs) under a dark veil like the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen's (GDD-LIFE-05), which covers the HUD too; the touch buttons are hidden. Over the veil, centred: "PONCHO" in large pale text (32 px), "Siete Vidas" under it (16 px), Poncho's standing frame (GDD-ART-04) at his size in the level, standing left of the two lines with his feet level with the bottom of "Siete Vidas" (104 px from the screen's top) and 8 px between him and the wider line's left edge (user, 2026-10-31: beside the title, so four rows fit; it stood above the menu before), and under them the title menu (user, 2026-10-25; the third row, 2026-10-28; "Continue", 2026-10-31): "Continue" while a saved run exists (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"), then "Start", "Tutorial" and "Settings", drawn like the pause menu (12 px, 160 x 28 px rows growing to 48 CSS px, the first one's top 112 px from the screen's top, so four fit on a landscape phone, where they grow to 34 px; `TITLE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`), with "Continue" chosen whenever the title screen appears with it, else "Start". "PONCHO" and "Siete Vidas" have their middles 68 and 96 px from the screen's top (Proposed).
- **Starting (the title menu, with the other buttons still starting, decided by the user 2026-10-25):** Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item, and a tap or click on a row picks that row. Picking "Start" starts the level, picking "Tutorial" starts 0-1 (see "Which level" below) and picking "Continue" resumes the saved run (GDD-PRG-02); none of them drops the saved run. A tap or click anywhere off the rows (on the hidden buttons' places and the pause button's too) and a new press of claw, Fuego, Left or Right (GDD-CTL-01) start the level as "Start" does, whichever row is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the title with "Settings" chosen. The jump keys start the level only by picking: Space and Z pick, Up and W choose. The touch buttons are hidden and press nothing on the title screen, nor on the settings screen opened from it: a finger on their places, the slide stick's lower quarter included (left, or right when mirrored), is only a tap (a finger on the hidden mode switch does not change the mode, GDD-CTL-03). Only a game button that was up on the frame before counts, so pressing a second button while holding one does; a tap shorter than one frame counts (the game buttons are latched, L-006; Enter, Space, Z and the menu keys are read from the browser's own key events like Escape and P). Escape and P do nothing on the title screen, and losing focus there does not pause the level behind it. The button that started the level counts as already held, as after a resume: a jump held from the title screen does not make Poncho jump until it is released and pressed again, and a Left or Right key, button or slide-stick finger down when the level starts does not move him until it is released. `src/sim/title.ts`.
- **Upright on the title screen:** a touch device whose screen is upright (the same test as the automatic pause) hides the title menu and shows "Turn your device sideways" in its place, in grey 12 px text, also when the game opens that way; nothing on the menu can be chosen or picked until it is sideways again (the presses of a frame count in order, like the pause menu's, so a pick of "Settings" just before a turn upright opens the settings screen, hidden until the phone is sideways), and the level starts only in a frame that ends sideways (so a tap and a turn upright together do not start it); sideways again, the title menu is back with the item chosen before still chosen. So the level never starts upright.
