- **Settings screen (built; volume rows, Left and Right to change, "<" and ">" on touch, saved in the browser, decided by the user 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** opened from the pause menu or the title menu, it replaces that menu: the same dark layer, "Settings" in 16 px pale text, centred, its middle 44 px from the screen's top, and under it six rows, "Master volume", "Effects volume", "Language", "Button opacity", "Layout" and "Back", each 260 px wide and 28 px tall, centred, the first one's top 62 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below, growing like the pause menu's rows so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`SETTINGS_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`; on a landscape phone they grow to 34 px, so the six end 266 px from the top). A volume row shows its name in 12 px text from 20 px inside the row's left edge, a "<" 8 px inside its left edge, a ">" 8 px inside its right edge, and its value as 10 small upright bars, 5 px wide and 10 px tall with 2 px between them, centred in the row's height, the last one ending 20 px inside the row's right edge: as many filled as the value, the rest outlined. The chosen row is pale and the others grey; "Back", when chosen, has a ">" in front of it like the pause menu. The screen opens with "Master volume" chosen, every time. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Left or A lowers the chosen volume by 1 and Right or D raises it by 1, from 0 to 10 (no wrap-around; the default is 10, `VOLUME` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); Enter, Space or Z on "Back", Escape, P or the touch pause button leave the screen, back to the menu it was opened from; Enter, Space or Z on a volume row does nothing. Each press counts once, read like the pause menu's keys, in order within a frame. On touch, a tap or click on a volume row chooses it and, on the row's left half, lowers it by 1, on its right half raises it by 1; a tap on "Back" leaves; a tap anywhere else does nothing (it does not resume). A change that moves a volume plays the jump sound at the new volume, so the player hears it; at 0, or at 10 when raised, nothing changes and nothing plays. Every sound effect plays at Master times Effects, each as a share of 10 (GDD-AUD-01, GDD-AUD-02). Opened from the pause menu, it lies under the HUD and the touch buttons like the pause menu, and a button or finger pressed on it is held back after the resume like any press made while paused; opened from the title, it covers everything like the title screen. A phone turned upright hides it like the pause menu, showing "Turn your device sideways"; turned sideways again, the same row is still chosen. Losing focus changes nothing on it. `src/sim/settings.ts`, `src/ui/settingsMenu.ts`.
- **Language (built; a Language row with English, Español and Deutsch, user, 2026-10-25; every text on screen switches and a first visit takes the browser's language, user, 2026-10-29; details and the Spanish and German texts Proposed):** the "Language" row is drawn like a volume row (its name from 20 px inside its left edge, "<" and ">"), with the chosen language's own name ("English", "Español" or "Deutsch", 12 px, in the row's colour) centred where a volume row's bars are, so it reads the same in every language. The languages go in that order. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the language before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes (no wrap-around), and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing, like the volume rows. A change plays no sound, is saved at once (see "Saving the settings") and from that frame every text below is drawn in the new language, the settings screen's own included, so its title and rows change under the player's eyes while the same row stays chosen. The names "PONCHO", "Siete Vidas", "Siete vidas gastadas", "Hilos", "Don Zopilote" (GDD-BOS-02) and the language names are the same in every language, and so is "La Estrella" (GDD-BOS-03), in the table below, and so are the placeholder and dev lines of the test levels (GDD-UI-05).   The tutorial's tips are listed in each language in GDD-UI-05.
- **Button opacity (built; user, 2026-11-01: three steps, "Low / Medium / High"; details Proposed):** the "Button opacity" row is drawn like the Language row, with the step's name ("Low", "Medium" or "High", in the language chosen, 12 px, in the row's colour) centred where a volume row's bars are. The steps go in that order. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the step before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes (no wrap-around), and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing. A change plays no sound and is saved at once (see "Saving the settings"). The default is Medium. The row is there on every device; it changes the touch buttons (GDD-CTL-03), which a device without touch does not show. Opened from the pause menu, the buttons drawn above the settings screen change as the step is picked.
- **Layout (built; user, 2026-11-01: a row "Layout: Standard / Mirrored", the pause button staying top-right; details Proposed):** the "Layout" row is drawn like the Button opacity row, with "Standard" or "Mirrored" (in the language chosen) where a volume row's bars are. Standard comes first. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the step before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes, and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing. A change plays no sound and is saved at once (see "Saving the settings"). The default is Standard. The row is there on every device; it moves the touch buttons (GDD-CTL-03, "Mirrored layout"), which a device without touch does not show. Opened from the pause menu, the buttons drawn above the settings screen move as the step is picked.
- **The first language:** a first visit, or a save with no language or one that is not `en`, `es` or `de`, uses the browser's languages (`navigator.languages`, else `navigator.language`), in the browser's order: the first one whose main part (before any `-`, in any case) is `en`, `es` or `de` decides, so `fr-FR, de-AT` gives Deutsch; none gives English. Until something is saved the browser's language is followed, so a new one counts at the next visit; once the game saves (a setting changed, or the tutorial started), the save holds the language in use, and that one is used from then on.
- **Saving the settings (built, details Proposed):** every change, of a volume, the language, the Button opacity or the Layout, is saved in the browser's local storage at once, and the game opens with the saved settings (GDD-PRG-02). Nothing saved, or something unreadable, opens with the defaults, and a saved value that is not a whole number from 0 to 10 falls back to its default alone (the language to the browser's, see "The first language"; a Button opacity that is not `low`, `medium` or `high` to Medium, a Layout that is not `standard` or `mirrored` to Standard). Where the browser refuses storage the game plays with the settings of this visit and saves nothing, without an error; a level started as a scene of its own (the next level, or a title or pick over another level) keeps them too.
- **Gamepad (built):** the D-pad or left stick works like the arrow keys, south like Enter and start like Escape on every menu, and start on the title screen starts the level (GDD-CTL-02).
- **Which level (built; user, 2026-10-25, 1-1 added 2026-10-27, the tutorial played once 2026-10-28, World 1's run followed and its lives saved 2026-11-02, details Proposed):** "Start" plays the tutorial 0-1 (GDD-WLD-06), with seven lives, until 0-1 has been started once; 0-1 counts as played the moment it starts, by "Start" or by "Tutorial", even if the player quits at once (user, 2026-10-28), and this is saved in the browser (GDD-PRG-02). From then on "Start" follows the World 1 run the save keeps (user, 2026-11-02: "Follow the run": "The save remembers where your World 1 run is: Start plays the next level in order of the current run (1-1 after a game over, then 1-2, ...). Clears still count for medals.", which replaced their first answer, "First level not cleared"; "Save the lives": "The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there."): it plays the level the run has reached (GDD-PRG-02, "World 1's run") from its start, with the lives the save keeps for World 1, or seven where it keeps none; 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven, and 1-1 with seven where the run is kept with 0 lives (only a damaged save holds that: a game over moves the run back to 1-1). So it plays 1-1 until 1-1 is cleared, then 1-2 with the lives at 1-1's clear until 1-2 is cleared, then 1-3 with the lives at 1-2's clear until 1-3 is cleared, then the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02) with the lives at 1-3's clear until 1-4 is cleared; then World 1's run is over (the save moves it to 1-1 with seven, a new run, since 1-4 is World 1's last level; Proposed) and "Start" plays World 2's run, the latest world reached (see "Which level, with two worlds"); after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in a World 1 level, 1-4 included, it plays 1-1 with seven, whatever else is cleared, until 1-4 is cleared (GDD-LIFE-05). A save that keeps no World 1 run (one from before the run was kept, or whose run names no real World 1 level) keeps the older rule: the first World 1 level, in World 1's order (1-1, 1-2, 1-3, then the arena 1-4), that the saved progress does not hold as cleared (GDD-PRG-02, "Progress"), with the lives World 1 keeps, or seven where it keeps none, but 1-1 always with seven, and 1-1 where World 1 keeps 0 lives; once every World 1 level is cleared such a save holds 1-4 as cleared, so "Start" plays World 2 (see "Which level, with two worlds"). The test levels are not among a world's levels here. Where the browser refuses storage, the run, the lives and the levels cleared in this visit count. "Tutorial" always plays 0-1 with seven, and "Continue" resumes a run saved at an ofrenda inside a level, with its lives (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"). An address picks what "Start" plays instead, whatever was played, cleared or kept, with seven lives: `?level=1-1`, `?level=1-2`, `?level=1-3`, `?level=1-4` (the boss arena, GDD-BOS-02), `?level=0-1`, `?level=2-1` to `?level=2-4` (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-BOS-03), or a test level: the sandbox with `?level=sandbox`, the slopes level with `?level=slopes` (GDD-MOV-09), World 2's labs with `?level=lab-2m` and `?level=lab-2e` (GDD-TEC-04); every level's id is its address; any other `level`, or none, gives the rule above. The title screen shows the start of the level "Start" plays behind the veil, with the lives it gives: when the game opens, after quitting to the title, from the pause menu or the results menu. So after the tutorial has started once, quitting it brings back the title over 1-1, or over 1-2, 1-3 or the arena 1-4 while the run is there, or over the level World 2's run has reached once 1-4 is cleared. When the level picked is not the one behind the veil ("Tutorial" over a World 1 level, or "Start" over a level "Start" no longer plays), it starts at once from its own start with seven lives, as the next level starts (below), with the press held. The playtests use the sandbox, except those for 0-1, 1-1, 1-2, 1-3, the boss arena 1-4 (`e2e/boss.spec.ts`), the slopes level, World 1's run and lives (`e2e/world1.spec.ts`), World 2's levels, run and lives (`e2e/world2.spec.ts`), La Estrella's arena 2-4 (`e2e/estrella.spec.ts`), the awnings and the rollo in `lab-2m` (`e2e/awning.spec.ts`) and World 2's enemies in `lab-2e` (`e2e/enemies2.spec.ts`). `startLevel` in `src/levels/index.ts`.
- **Which level, with two worlds (user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world": once 1-4 is cleared "Start" continues World 2's run, and a save from before that cleared 1-4 goes to 2-1; built, details Proposed; `frontierWorld` and `startLevel` in `src/levels/index.ts`, checked by `src/levels/levels.test.ts` and `e2e/world2.spec.ts`):** once the tutorial has started, "Start" plays the run of the latest world reached: World 2 once 1-4 has been cleared, by a clear the save holds (in its `levels`, GDD-PRG-02), else World 1 as above. In World 2 it follows World 2's run as it follows World 1's: the level `worldAt` keeps for World 2, with the lives `worldLives` keeps for it, or seven where it keeps none, and 2-1, World 2's start, always with seven; with no World 2 run kept it goes by World 2's levels cleared as the older rule does for World 1: the first World 2 level not cleared, with the lives World 2 keeps, but 2-1 always with seven, so a save from before World 2, which cleared 1-4 and nothing of World 2, plays 2-1 with seven. So, after 1-4's clear, it plays 2-1 with seven until 2-1 is cleared, then 2-2 with the lives at 2-1's clear until 2-2 is cleared, then 2-3, then La Estrella's arena 2-4, then 2-1 with seven again, a new run, since 2-4 is World 2's last level; after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in a World 2 level it plays 2-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05). World 1 stays cleared; until a world map lets the player pick a world, its levels are played again by an address (and "Next level" from them). Where the browser refuses storage, the clears and runs of this visit count.
- **The next level (built; user, 2026-10-27: a press on 0-1's level clear screen starts 1-1; since 2026-10-31 a pick of "Next level" on the results menu; details Proposed):** a level can name the level after it; 0-1 names 1-1, 1-1 names 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08), 1-2 names 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09) and 1-3 names the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02); 1-4, World 1's last level, names World 2's first, 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12), which names 2-2, 2-2 names 2-3 and 2-3 names La Estrella's arena 2-4 (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14, GDD-BOS-03); 2-4, World 2's last level while World 3 does not exist, and the test levels (the sandbox, the slopes level, `lab-2m`, `lab-2e`) name none. In a level that names one, picking "Next level" on the results menu (see "Results menu") starts that level at once, with no title screen: a fresh run of it, with the lives Poncho has at the clear when both levels are in the same one of the seven worlds, as 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and 1-4 are, and 2-1, 2-2, 2-3 and 2-4, and with seven after the tutorial, which is outside them, and from 1-4 into 2-1, a new world (GDD-LIFE-05), drawn from its start with the camera there (GDD-UI-04). The button that pressed counts as already held there, on any device, as after a resume: a key, finger or gamepad button still down does nothing until it is released and pressed again, and so does a key that the browser's auto-repeat presses again (a key held through the change shows there only as a repeat). The settings carry over (GDD-PRG-02). Quitting to the title from 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4, or from a World 2 level, by the pause menu or by the results menu, brings back the title screen over a fresh run of the level "Start" plays, with the lives it gives, as for any level: the level the World 1 run has reached (see "Which level"), so 1-1 with seven until 1-1 is cleared, 1-2 with the lives at 1-1's clear until 1-2 is cleared, 1-3 with the lives at 1-2's clear until 1-3 is cleared, 1-4 with the lives at 1-3's clear until 1-4 is cleared, and 1-1 with seven after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in any of them, and World 2's run once 1-4 is cleared (see "Which level, with two worlds"), whose levels go likewise; a new visit to the game opens on the same level with the same lives. "Continue" resumes a run saved at one of their ofrendas (1-4 and 2-4 have none, GDD-BOS-01), with its lives (GDD-PRG-02).
- **Level clear screen (built; a placeholder for the results screen, user, 2026-10-25; the time, the medal and the reveal, user, 2026-10-30; the menu, user, 2026-10-31; details Proposed):** when Poncho reaches the exit (GDD-WLD-03), or in a boss level 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit that defeats the boss (GDD-BOS-01), the level stays in view, standing still, under the dark veil of the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05), with, centred, one under the other: "Level clear!" in 16 px pale text (its middle 92 px from the screen's top), the hilos as "Hilos 31/50", collected and the level's total ("Hilos 0/0" in a boss arena, 1-4 and 2-4, which have none, Open Question 21; 12 px, pale, its middle at 116 px), the clear time as "Time 2:14" (12 px, pale, at 132 px; see "Best time"), the medal (at 150 px) and, from its first 1.0 s (60 steps) on, the results menu (below). Poncho stays where he reached it, or in a boss level where he is as the screen comes up, drawn above the veil and under the screen's lines and menu, playing his victory pose (user, 2026-11-08: "Above veil, in place"; GDD-ART-04). The HUD and the touch buttons stay on top. A new press of jump, claw, Fuego, left or right, on any device, counts only for the reveal (below); a tap shorter than a frame counts, and a button held as Poncho reaches the exit, or as a boss level's clear screen comes up, is no new press. No button leaves the screen: only a pick on the results menu does. Not built yet: the score (GDD-PRG-03), the world map and a sound for the screen itself.
- **Clear time (user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the time from the level start to the exit (in a boss level, to the step its clear screen comes up), counted in simulation steps (60 a second), so the pause, the settings screen and the title screen do not count, and the waits after a lost life do. The restart after the last life (GDD-LIFE-05) starts it again from 0, as it gives back the seven lives, also when it plays 1-1 after the last life was lost in 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4 (or 2-1 after one lost in a later World 2 level). It is shown as minutes and whole seconds, rounded down: "Time 0:07", "Time 2:14", "Time 12:05", and "Time 99:59" for anything longer. `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Medal line (user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the medal of GDD-PRG-03 as "Bronze medal", "Silver medal" or "Gold medal" (in the chosen language, see "Language") in 12 px text in the medal's colour, with a filled disc 10 px across in the same colour 6 px left of the text, the pair centred together. Bronze is `#c7834a`, silver `#c9cfdc`, gold `#f2c14e` (placeholders until medal art exists). `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Best time and new bests (user, 2026-10-31: the best time and the best medal are kept, and the screen says when a new best is reached; details Proposed):** the time line adds, after two spaces, the level's saved best time before this run, as "Best 1:58" in the format of the clear time, or "New best!" when this run's time is shorter than it. The medal line adds "New best!", after two spaces and in the medal's colour, when this run's medal is better than the saved one; the disc and text stay centred together. A level cleared for the first time has no best yet: its time line and medal line show nothing more. Both words are in the language chosen (see "Language"). The saved bests read are those from before this run; the save is written on the same step (GDD-PRG-02). `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Reveal (user, 2026-10-30: lines one by one with a tick; details Proposed):** "Level clear!" shows as the screen comes up, then the hilo line at 0.25 s (15 steps), the time line at 0.5 s (30 steps) and the medal line at 0.75 s (45 steps), each playing the tick (GDD-AUD-02) on the step it appears. A new press of jump, claw, Fuego, left or right before the medal line shows shows every line at once and plays one tick; the results menu still appears at 1.0 s. A press after the medal line does nothing. `RESULTS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
- **Results menu (user, 2026-10-31: "Next level", "Retry" and "Quit to title", Retry a fresh run, "Next level" greyed out where there is none; details Proposed):** from the level clear screen's first 1.0 s on, three rows, "Next level", "Retry" and "Quit to title", drawn like the pause menu (12 px, centred, 160 x 28 px rows growing so none is under 48 CSS px, the first one's top 162 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below; `RESULTS_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The chosen row is pale with a ">" in front of it, the others grey. It opens with "Next level" chosen, or "Retry" in a level that names no next level (see "The next level"; La Estrella's arena 2-4), where "Next level" is drawn darker (`#5c6178`) and can be neither chosen nor picked: Up or W from "Retry" stays there and a tap on it does nothing. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen row, and a tap or click on a row picks that row at once; a tap anywhere else does nothing. Each press counts once and the presses of one frame count in order, read from the browser's own key events like the pause menu's. The gamepad's D-pad or left stick and south work like the arrow keys and Enter (GDD-CTL-02). Claw, Fuego, Left, Right and the touch buttons pick nothing; the jump keys Space and Z pick only by their key events. Escape, P, the pause button, the gamepad's start, losing focus and a phone turned upright pause the game as anywhere in a level: the pause menu comes over the screen, the results menu hidden under it, and resuming brings the results menu back with the same row chosen. Picking "Next level" starts the next level (see "The next level"); picking "Retry" starts a fresh run of the same level at once, as the next level starts: from its start, with the lives Poncho has at the clear in a level of one of the seven worlds (World 1 and World 2; user, 2026-11-02: "Lives at the clear": "The lives you finished the level with; its ofrendas and veladora can win some back before Next level.") and with seven in the tutorial (GDD-LIFE-05), every ofrenda and the veladora unused (GDD-LIFE-03), every hilo not saved yet back (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-PRG-02), the clock at 0:00, the button that picked held; picking "Quit to title" brings back the title screen over a fresh run of the level "Start" plays (see "Which level"), as the pause menu's "Quit to title" does, the button that picked held there. `src/sim/results.ts`, `src/ui/pauseMenu.ts`.
- **Not built yet:** boot and world map screens.

### GDD-UI-03 Accessibility

Status: Proposed

- Remappable keys and adjustable touch layout (size, mirror, opacity; the mirror and the opacity are built, GDD-CTL-03).
- Assist mode: infinite lives, slower game speed (0.75x), hold-to-jump toggle.
- Reduced motion and screen-shake off. High-contrast outlines for hazards.
- Never rely on colour alone to signal danger. Subtitles for any spoken line.
- English, Spanish and German text, with a language switch (German added by the user, 2026-10-25): the Language row of the settings screen, GDD-UI-02.

### GDD-UI-04 Camera

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-25: it follows Poncho sideways only, with a small dead zone, and stops at the level's edges).

- The camera shows 480 x 270 game px of the level. It follows Poncho left and right: while the middle of his drawn body is inside the dead zone, 32 px wide in the middle of the screen (from 224 to 256 px from the screen's left edge), it stands still; when he goes past either side of it, it moves with him so that he stays on that side's edge. It never shows anything past the level's left or right edge, so a level one screen wide (the sandbox, the slopes level, the boss arenas 1-4 and 2-4) never scrolls. It does not move up or down: levels are one screen tall for now.
- It follows the body where it is drawn, between steps, so it moves smoothly, and it stands at whole art pixels (a quarter of a game px, GDD-ART-01). While Poncho is hidden waiting to respawn it stands still; when he appears at the respawn point it moves there at once. On the title screen and in a fresh run he is at the level start, so it shows the level start.
- The HUD, the screens (title, pause, settings, *Siete vidas gastadas*, level clear) and the touch buttons do not move with it. The placeholder title, controls and dev lines at the top of a test level's start move with the level.
- `CAMERA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/camera.ts`.


### GDD-UI-05 Tutorial tips

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-26: texts and highlights pop up while Poncho is in a stretch of the level and play goes on; they name the buttons of the device used last; the highlight is a pulsing outline). The wording, sizes and timings are Proposed.

- **When:** a level can have tips (only the tutorial 0-1 does, GDD-WLD-06). Each tip has a stretch of tile columns. While Poncho is playing (not waiting to respawn, not on a screen) and the middle of his body is in a tip's stretch, that tip is shown; the stretches do not overlap. Play never stops for a tip. It fades in over 0.2 s (12 steps) and out over 0.2 s when he leaves the stretch, counted in steps so it stands still with the game; when he goes from one tip's stretch into the next, the old one goes at once and the new one fades in. A tip shows again whenever he comes back to its stretch. A tip about a thing that is done goes for good once it is done: the Alacrán tip once that Alacrán is defeated, the piñata tip once the piñata is open, the veladora tip once it is used, until the run starts afresh (a restart after the last life or quitting to the title).
- **Text:** in a box at the top middle of the screen, under the HUD: 8 px pale text (`#fff3dc`), at most two lines, on a dark box (`#0d1020` at 0.75) with 4 px of room around the text, wrapped at 280 px, its top 26 px from the screen's top. It does not move with the camera (GDD-UI-04). The placeholder title, controls and dev lines at the top of the level start are drawn in the test levels only (the sandbox, the slopes level, GDD-MOV-09, and World 2's labs `lab-2m` and `lab-2e`, GDD-TEC-04): in the tutorial the tips take their place, and 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and 1-4 have neither, nor do World 2's levels 2-1 to 2-4 (the dev keys still work everywhere).
- **Device:** the text names the buttons of the device used last: the keyboard (any key press), touch (a finger on the screen) or a gamepad (any button or stick push past its dead zone). Before any is used it is touch on a device that reports touch, otherwise the keyboard. A mouse click does not change it.
- **Highlight:** with each tip, its thing in the level gets a dashed pale outline (`#fff3dc`, 1 px, dashes of 4 px with 3 px between them) 2 px outside it, which grows by up to 2 px on each side and back, smoothly, every 0.8 s (48 steps), and a small pale triangle 6 px wide pointing down, its tip 6 px above it, bobbing 2 px with the same beat. The outline is a shape, not a colour (GDD-UI-03). It moves with the level and fades with its tip. While touch is the device used last, the tip about running, jumping or the claw also draws the same dashed outline round that touch button (the left and right buttons, or the slide stick's track, for running).
- **The tips of 0-1** (stretch in tile columns; highlighted thing; keyboard / touch / gamepad text):
  1. Columns 0 to 5; none, but on touch the move buttons: "Arrows or A/D to run" / "Hold left or right to run" / "Stick or D-pad to run".
  2. Columns 6 to 13; the 2-tile wall (columns 11 and 12, rows 12 and 13): "Space to jump. Hold it to jump higher" / "Tap the jump button. Hold it to jump higher" / "Bottom button to jump. Hold it to jump higher".
  3. Columns 14 to 20; the two gaps (columns 16 to 20, row 14): "Jump over the gaps" (every device).
  4. Columns 21 to 25; the first ofrenda: "Ofrendas are checkpoints. The first touch gives back a life" (every device).
  5. Columns 26 to 38; the first Alacrán's pen (columns 28 to 36, rows 12 and 13): "Jump on the Alacrán, or X to claw it" / "Jump on the Alacrán, or tap the claw button" / "Jump on the Alacrán, or left button to claw it". Gone once that Alacrán is defeated.
  6. Columns 39 to 41; the piñata: "Jump into the piñata from below" (every device). Gone once it is open.
  7. Columns 42 to 47; the spikes (columns 43 and 44, row 13): "Spikes hurt. With a taco, a hit costs only the taco" (every device).
  8. Columns 48 to 52; the second ofrenda: "Hilos are threads to collect. Find all 50!" (every device).
  9. Columns 53 to 57; the 3-tile gap (columns 53 to 55, row 14): "A running jump crosses 3 tiles" (every device).
  10. Columns 58 to 61; the 3-tile wall (columns 58 and 59, rows 11 to 13): "A full jump climbs a wall 3 tiles high" (every device).
  11. Columns 62 to 75; the veladora: "A veladora up there gives back a life" (every device). Gone once it is used.
  12. Columns 82 to 89; the exit: "Reach the arch to finish the level" (every device).
- **The tips of 0-1 in Spanish and German** (Proposed, 2026-10-29; the same stretches and highlights, in the language chosen, GDD-UI-02; keyboard / touch / gamepad where they differ):
  1. "Flechas o A/D para correr" / "Mantén izquierda o derecha para correr" / "Stick o cruceta para correr"; "Pfeiltasten oder A/D zum Laufen" / "Links oder rechts halten zum Laufen" / "Stick oder Steuerkreuz zum Laufen".
  2. "Espacio para saltar. Mantenlo para saltar más alto" / "Toca el botón de salto. Mantenlo para saltar más alto" / "Botón inferior para saltar. Mantenlo para saltar más alto"; "Leertaste zum Springen. Halten für höhere Sprünge" / "Sprungknopf antippen. Halten für höhere Sprünge" / "Untere Taste zum Springen. Halten für höhere Sprünge".
  3. "Salta los huecos"; "Spring über die Lücken".
  4. "Las ofrendas son puntos de control. El primer toque devuelve una vida"; "Ofrendas sind Kontrollpunkte. Die erste Berührung gibt ein Leben zurück".
  5. "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o presiona X para arañarlo" / "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o toca el botón de zarpazo" / "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o botón izquierdo para arañarlo"; "Spring auf den Alacrán oder kratz ihn mit X" / "Spring auf den Alacrán oder tipp auf den Krallenknopf" / "Spring auf den Alacrán oder kratz ihn mit der linken Taste".
  6. "Salta contra la piñata desde abajo"; "Spring von unten gegen die Piñata".
  7. "Los picos hacen daño. Con un taco, un golpe solo cuesta el taco"; "Stacheln tun weh. Mit einem Taco kostet ein Treffer nur den Taco".
  8. "Los hilos son para coleccionar. ¡Encuentra los 50!"; "Hilos sind Fäden zum Sammeln. Finde alle 50!".

  10. "Un salto completo sube un muro de 3 casillas"; "Ein voller Sprung erklimmt eine 3 Felder hohe Wand".

  12. "Llega al arco para terminar el nivel"; "Erreiche den Bogen, um das Level zu beenden".
  Every tip in every language fits the two lines of the box.
- `TIPS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, the tips in `src/levels/level0-1.ts`, `src/ui/tips.ts`.

## 12. Audio

### GDD-AUD-01 Music

Status: Proposed

Upbeat chiptune-and-acoustic mix: marimba, guitarrón, guitar, trumpets, played in the spirit of regional styles of each world. Each world has its own theme and a tense variation for Última Vida. All music is original or properly licensed. Audio is unlocked on first user gesture (browser requirement). Master, music and effects volumes are adjustable. Built: Master and Effects volume on the settings screen (GDD-UI-02); a Music volume row comes with the music.

### GDD-AUD-02 Sound effects

Status: Proposed (the ofrenda sound being a marimba is Decided, user, 2026-10-20)

Short, punchy and readable: jump, land, claw, stomp, hilo pickup (rising pitch combo), life lost (unravelling thread sound), ofrenda activate (marimba), the results tick, Don Zopilote's hiss and grunt (GDD-BOS-02), Poncho meows as personality. Each SFX is under 1 s and compressed for the web.

- **Ofrenda activate:** two rosewood marimba bars struck in quick succession, E5 then B5, 0.95 s. Generated from code (`scripts/audio/ofrenda.mjs`, encoded by `scripts/audio/make-sfx.mjs`) as a placeholder until real audio exists (Open Question 9). Shipped as Ogg and MP3. It plays when an ofrenda is used for the first time, not on a repeat touch and not when a restart resets the ofrendas.
- **When the other sounds play (user, 2026-10-23: the meow plays on respawn; the rest Proposed).** Each is generated from code (`scripts/audio/effects.mjs`, encoded by `scripts/audio/make-sfx.mjs`) as a placeholder until real audio exists (Open Question 9), shipped as Ogg and MP3, and plays on the step it happens. All but the meow and the tick play only while Poncho is in play; the meow plays as he appears and the tick on the level clear screen, below. Numbers are in `SFX` in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
  - **Jump:** a short rising chirp, 0.15 s, on every jump takeoff (from the ground, in coyote time or from the jump buffer, GDD-MOV-03) and every wall kick (GDD-MOV-08). Not on a spring launch (GDD-WLD-05) or a stomp bounce (GDD-CMB-01), which are not jumps. An awning's held rebound plays it raised 0, 2 or 4 semitones for tiers 1, 2 and 3 (GDD-WLD-10).
  - **Land:** a soft thump, 0.12 s, when Poncho touches down from the air while falling at 60 px/s or faster, so stepping onto a platform or a tiny drop stays quiet. Not when the landing is a stomp or a spring launch, which have their own sound or none. It also plays when Don Zopilote lands in the sand after a dive (GDD-BOS-02), when La Estrella's drop ends on the floor or an awning (GDD-BOS-03) and on an awning's plain rebound (GDD-WLD-10); a step in which several of these happen plays it once.
  - **Claw:** a swish, 0.18 s, when a claw swing starts (GDD-CMB-02). A press the claw ignores (during a swing or its cooldown) is silent.
  - **Stomp:** a springy thud, 0.2 s, on every stomp (GDD-CMB-01), a Tlacuache's first stomp that makes it play dead included (GDD-ENM-02), on every hit on La Estrella (GDD-BOS-03), and when the claw or a fireball topples a Trompo (GDD-ENM-03). One sound for a step, even if two enemies are stomped at once. A hit on Don Zopilote plays his own grunt or defeat hiss instead (below).
  - **Hilo pickup:** a plucked thread, 0.3 s, for each step in which hilos are collected (GDD-WLD-04). Its note climbs a major scale: the first hilo plays the root (G5), and each hilo collected within 0.75 s of the previous one plays the next note up, to the octave (G6, the eighth note), where it stays. A hilo after a longer pause starts again at the root. Two hilos in one step move the scale on by two and play the higher note once.
  - **Life lost:** an unravelling thread, a fast run of plucks falling in pitch, 0.85 s, when a life is lost (GDD-LIFE-02): a pit, a crush, or a hit in Base. A hit that Taco or Fuego absorbs (GDD-CMB-03) is not a lost life and does not play it.
  - **Tick (user, 2026-10-30, the sound itself Proposed):** a short wooden tick, 0.06 s, as each line of the level clear screen appears, and once when a press shows them all at once (GDD-UI-02). It plays on that screen, where Poncho is not in play.
  - **Meow (Decided, user, 2026-10-23):** a short meow, 0.5 s, when Poncho appears again after a lost life: on the first step of the respawn flip, or at the respawn itself when a skip cut the flip short, and after the restart from the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05). It also plays when La Estrella is defeated (GDD-BOS-03, Proposed), on the step of the hit that defeats her, with that hit's stomp. The sound itself is Proposed.
  - **Don Zopilote's voice (GDD-BOS-02; user, 2026-11-04: "Telegraph + hit + defeat"):** a vulture can only hiss and grunt. **Hiss:** a raspy hiss, high band-passed noise with a rasp at about 35 Hz, 0.4 s, on the step his telegraph starts. **Grunt:** a low guttural grunt gliding from 95 to 70 Hz, 0.25 s, on a hit on him that leaves him hurt. **Defeat:** a long deflating hiss, noise whose brightness falls from about 4 kHz to 300 Hz as it fades, 0.9 s, on the hit that defeats him (in place of the meow it played before). Each once a step, while Poncho is in play (`scripts/audio/effects.mjs`: `HISS`, `GRUNT`, `BOSS_DEFEAT_SFX`).
- Sounds start playing only after the first key press or touch, because browsers keep audio locked until then (GDD-AUD-01).

---

## 13. Art

### GDD-ART-01 Style and resolution

Status: Decided (pixel art, user, 2026-10-19; drawn in code, the 1920 x 1080 art resolution and a mood per world, user, 2026-10-25). The game units, outlines and palette rules are Proposed.

- Pixel art with a hand-made feel. **Drawn in code (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** every sprite and tile is a pixel grid in a script that writes the image files, as the placeholder sounds are made (GDD-AUD-02), and the user approves each one from screenshots before it goes in.
- **Art resolution (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** 1920 x 1080 (16:9), four times 480 x 270 on each side, so the sprites can hold the detail of the sprite references (GDD-ART-02). A tile is 64 x 64 art pixels. Poncho's frame is 192 x 192 art pixels, where each pixel of the user's drawing is 2 x 2 art pixels (Proposed, GDD-MOV-06, GDD-ART-04).
- **Fitting the screen (Proposed, as before):** the picture is scaled to fit the screen by integer factors where possible and letterboxed on other aspect ratios.
- **Game units (Proposed):** the rules keep their numbers. Every number in this document is in game pixels unless it says art pixels, and a game pixel is 4 x 4 art pixels, so the screen stays 480 x 270 game pixels, a tile 16 and Poncho's body box 12 x 32 (GDD-MOV-06). No speed, jump, gap or level changes with the art; only the drawing works in art pixels, and things are drawn at positions rounded to whole art pixels.
- The illustration (`reference.jpg`) is smooth; the sprites follow the sprite references' shapes, proportions and colours (GDD-ART-02, GDD-ART-03) and the pixel rules (GDD-ART-05).
- A limited palette per world, drawn from real Mexican craft colours (GDD-ART-03), and a mood per world (GDD-ART-06).
- **Built (details Proposed):** the canvas is 1920 x 1080 and the camera draws the 480 x 270 game pixels 4 times larger, so the placeholder shapes and the text look as before and Poncho's sprite shows every art pixel. A tap is read in game pixels as before. Built so far: Poncho's sprite, World 1's backdrop, ground tiles and plants, and World 2's backdrop, ground, decor, awnings, papel picado and arena plaza, each world's art loaded only in its own levels (GDD-ART-06), Don Zopilote with his cactus fruit and the arena's rocks (GDD-BOS-02), World 1's four enemies with their defeat poof (GDD-ENM-02), and Poncho's animation set with the taco, the chile and the claw swipe (GDD-ART-04); everything else, World 2's enemies and La Estrella included, is placeholder.

### GDD-ART-02 Poncho (character)

Status: Decided (appearance from the user's reference image, 2026-10-19; the sprite references and the main pixel reference, user, 2026-10-25). Sprite-level details are Proposed.

References, all in `assets/characters/poncho/`:

- `reference.jpg`: the illustration (512 x 512, orange background), the source for Poncho's colours, face, taco and PB badge.
- **Main reference (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** `sprite-pixel.jpg`, the user's pixel drawing of Poncho standing side-on, facing right (a 100 x 100 pixel grid, the figure 56 x 71 pixels). The sprite is this drawing, pixel for pixel, with a tail added behind him (user, 2026-10-25: "add a tail"); it wins over every other reference. It has a fringe along the poncho's hem, the furrowed brows of the illustration, a near-black outline, and the front paw held under the poncho.
- **Sprite references (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** `sprite-side.jpg` (two side views), `sprite-back-front.jpg` (back and front), `sprite-walk-a.jpg` and `sprite-walk-b.jpg` (walk cycles of four frames), and `sprite-run.jpg` (the user's run cycle, four frames of legs, the source of the run frames, GDD-ART-04). Poncho is drawn side-on, as in these. Where they and the illustration differ, the sprite references win for the sprite (user, 2026-10-25: "side view as drawn"); the main reference wins over both, and these stay the guide for poses it does not show (the walk, the back, the front; but see the front-view references below).
- **Front-view references (Decided, user, 2026-11-08: "I'll upload a pic as reference I want poncho looking like that from the front", then "I'll give you several front views of poncho"):** `assets/characters/poncho/front/`, the user's front views, with a README of what they agree on (the sombrero from slightly below, big dark eyes under furrowed brows, the poncho's chevron bands of triangles, the PB badge on his right chest). They are the guide for how he looks from the front; where they and `sprite-back-front.jpg` differ, they win (Proposed reading of the user's words). The victory frames are front views, the user's victory strip (`assets/characters/poncho/victory/`, GDD-ART-04).

The look:

- **Build:** chubby chibi cat standing upright on two legs, large round head, big pointed ears with pink insides. Below the poncho the ginger legs show, with cream paws.
- **Fur:** warm tan and ginger, cream muzzle, chest and paws, darker ginger tabby marks on the forehead (illustration).
- **Face:** large dark brown eyes with white highlights, pink cheek blush, small pink nose, small smirk, slightly furrowed brows (confident, cheeky; illustration). Side-on (sprite references): one big dark eye with a white highlight, a pink blush below it and a small smile on the cream muzzle. The front view shows the eyes shut in a happy smile.
- **Sombrero:** wide brim, blue on top with a red rim and a zigzag trim; a tall, rounded red crown with a blue band. In the sprite references the ear nearer the viewer pokes up above the brim in front of the crown, and the brim tilts with the way Poncho faces.
- **Poncho:** blue, with a red V-shaped chevron at the neck and a red border at the hem, both dotted white, and small white triangles in the blue field. The illustration also has a cream fringe and a darker navy layer behind (cape or back panel); the sprite references show neither, so the sprite leaves them out (Proposed reading of "side view as drawn").
- **Tail (from the sprite references):** long and ginger, curving up behind the body, with a white tip.
- **Back (from the sprite references):** the crown and brim cover the head and the ears poke up behind the crown; the back of the poncho has the same chevron and triangles.
- **Held item:** a taco with lettuce, tomato and meat in one hand (illustration). This becomes the Taco state (GDD-PWR-01); the sprite references, in Base, hold nothing.
- **Glow:** the illustration has a golden rim light. It is reserved as the invulnerability effect (GDD-CMB-03, GDD-PWR-04).
- **PB mark (Decided, user, 2026-10-19):** the small gold "PB" badge on the chest and the "PB" mark in the bottom-right corner of the reference stand for **Poncho on Base** (GDD-VIS-05). Keep the badge on the chest, as the front view shows it, and on the side view where the frame allows. The corner mark is a brand watermark and is not part of the character.
- **Personality (Decided):** loves tacos and spicy food.
- **Pronouns (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** he.
