The held and pickup taco's shell is his fur colours, its tomato and the chile his red and crimson. The lettuce has two shades, not three.
- Each world adds its own palette of at most 24 colours for tiles, scenery and enemies (GDD-ART-06), starting from these and adding talavera, serape and marigold tones. Poncho must stay readable against every world background: the outline and the poncho's blue and red do that against the warm desert.

### GDD-ART-04 Poncho animation set

Status: Proposed (the look of the animations, the taco, the chile and the claw swipe Decided, user: designed from his references and approved on an animated design page, 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-08, "Make it punchier" for the swipe, "More expressive", "Fire spit bigger", "Eat with crumbs", the victory pose left out at first, "Let's skip the victory pose for now. And just do the others.", then approved in the game, "Approved"; the victory pose Decided, user, 2026-11-08: the user's strip only recoloured, "Just take thie sprite for the victory and adapt it minimal to fit our colour palett", "Ok I approve!", shown above the clear screen's veil, "Above veil, in place", always with the taco, "Always the taco"; when each frame shows is Proposed)

The set planned: idle (blink, glances at the taco in the Taco and Fuego states), run, skid, jump up, apex, fall, land, claw swipe, fireball spit (Fuego), hurt (the sombrero tips off; a hit in Taco or Fuego costs the power, GDD-PWR-01), respawn flip, eating a taco or chile, victory, wall cling (post-MVP), glide (post-MVP). What is built is below.

The run cycle is the user's run drawing (`sprite-run.jpg`, GDD-ART-02), side-on, the tail swinging behind; the four-frame walks of the sprite references (`sprite-walk-a.jpg`, `sprite-walk-b.jpg`) stay the guide for a walk.

- **Built: the standing frame and the run cycle** (user, 2026-10-25), made by `scripts/art/make-sprites.mjs` from the user's drawing and run cycle (`sprite-pixel.jpg`, `sprite-run.jpg`; GDD-ART-02): frames 0 to 4 of `assets/sprites/poncho.png` (160 x 160 art pixels until 0.10.2, now placed unchanged in the 192 x 192 frame). The four run frames are the user's run legs under the standing drawing's body, with the tail's tip swaying.
- **Built: the animation set** (user, 2026-11-05: "Create animations for ponch, the taco and, Chile and the claw swipe aswell, Nintendo art and style."), in `assets/sprites/poncho.png` (rows of 8 frames of 192 x 192 art px, 48 x 48 game px, so the sheet stays under a phone's 4096 px texture limit), made by `scripts/art/make-sprites.mjs` with Poncho's pose rig (`scripts/art/poncho/rig.mjs`): the user's drawing cut into its parts (sombrero, face, ears, poncho, legs, tail) and put back together in new poses, moved, squashed and stretched by whole rows and columns, never repainted; only his expressions, his front paw (as the walk references show it) and new leg poses are drawn new, in the drawing's colours (GDD-ART-02). The standing frame and the four run frames are unchanged, pixel for pixel (checked by `scripts/art/poncho/rig.test.mjs`). The frames: idle, blink, glance (at the taco), jump0 (the take-off stretch), jump1 (rising, legs tucked), apex, fall0, fall1 (falling fast, the sombrero lifting off his head), land (the squash), skid, flip0 to flip7 (tucked into a ball), clawWind, clawSwipe, clawFollow, hurt0 and hurt1 (knocked back, the sombrero tipping off his head), eat0 (the reach for the bite) and eat1 (the chomp), spit0 (cheeks puffed) and spit1 (the blast); then the victory frames (below).
- **Built: the victory pose** (user, 2026-11-08: "Victory pose take the image as reference and the sprite.", then "Just take thie sprite for the victory and adapt it minimal to fit our colour palett"; approved, "Ok I approve!"): the four frames of the user's strip (`assets/characters/poncho/victory/strip.jpg`), read cell by cell on its 4 px grid (`scripts/art/poncho/victory-pixels.mjs`), one of its pixels a drawing px (2 x 2 art px), only mapped to his 16 colours (GDD-ART-03), nothing re-posed: victory0 (ready, both fists up, no taco), victory1, victory2 and victory3 (the taco raised higher in each, in his right paw). Front view, facing the player, the PB badge on the viewer's right as in the front references; he holds this taco in every power state, Base included ("Always the taco"), and no held item is drawn with these frames. They follow the strip's outline, 2 drawing px thick in places (GDD-ART-05 asks for 1; left as drawn, as the user asked only for the colours to change). `scripts/art/poncho/victory.mjs`, last in `assets/sprites/poncho.png`.
- **The taco, the chile and the claw swipe (built, `scripts/art/poncho/items.mjs`, `assets/sprites/poncho/`):** the taco pickup, a golden hard shell with lettuce, tomato and meat as the illustration's (`reference.jpg`), floats in a four-frame bob, one frame every 8 steps of the run's clock, and glints for the last 8 steps of every 120; the chile pickup, glossy red with a green stem and a ring of warm glow and heat wisps, does the same. A taco or chile that walks or falls (from a piñata) is lifted by its frames' reach below their 12 px square, so no bob frame dips below its feet: past that square each taco frame reaches at most 0.5 px on every side and each chile frame 0 behind, 0.5 in front, 1 above and 1 below (`PONCHO_ART.pickup.reach`, pinned by `scripts/art/poncho/items.test.mjs` and fitted over the real levels by `scripts/art/poncho/items.levels.test.mjs`, L-059). He holds the taco in his paw (Taco) and the taco with the chile beside it (Fuego) at each frame's hand point; eating shows the food at his mouth, then bitten, with crumbs and lettuce flying; the spit shows a flame burst at his mouth; the claw swipe is four smear frames of three pale crescent streaks with crimson edges, its frame laid on the claw's box (GDD-CMB-02). The HUD's power icon uses the same pictures (GDD-UI-01).
- **When each frame shows (Proposed; `PONCHO_ART` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/ponchoPose.ts`), first match wins:** on the level clear screen (GDD-UI-02), the victory, counted from the step the screen comes up as its first: victory0 for 12 steps, victory1 for 5, victory2 for 5, then victory3 and victory2 in turn for 24 steps each for as long as the screen is up, never mirrored and drawn whole, not blinking even when he reached the exit while blinking after a hit (GDD-LIFE-02) (`PONCHO_ART.victorySteps`, `victoryPumpSteps`); the respawn flip (the flip frame nearest the turn's angle, GDD-LIFE-02); hurt, on a hit that costs his power and not a life (hurt0 for 6 steps, then hurt1 for 18); the claw (clawWind in the wind-up, clawSwipe in the swing with swipe0 on its first step and swipe1 on the next two, then clawFollow for 10 steps with swipe2 for 3 and swipe3 for 3); the spit, when a fireball leaves him (spit0 for 2 steps, then spit1 for 8 with flame0 for 4 and flame1 for 4); eating, when his power goes up (eat0 for 10 steps with the food whole, then eat1 for 14 with the taco bitten and crumbs0 for 6 and crumbs1 for 6; a chile stays whole); in the air (jump0 for the 4 steps after a take-off, then by his vertical speed, down positive: jump1 under -120 px/s, apex under 60, fall0 under 250, else fall1); landing from a fall of 150 px/s or faster (land for 6 steps); a skid on the ground at 40 px/s or more against the way he faces; running (the run frames as before, `ANIM`); standing (blink for the last 6 of every 180 steps, and in Taco or Fuego glance at the taco for 40 of every 360). Facing left mirrors him and what he holds, but for the victory frames, front views, which never mirror; the claw and its swipe face the way the swing does.
- **Where (Proposed):** the frame's bottom row is level with his feet and its middle column on his body box's middle (the frame is 48 game px, `SPRITE_FRAME`). Each frame reaches past his body box at most 13 px behind, 14.5 in front and 11 above, and never below his feet but in the flip, drawn in the air; the victory frames, never mirrored, at most 10 px to the left (behind), 10 to the right (in front) and 10 above (`PONCHO_ART.reach`, per frame, pinned by `scripts/art/poncho.test.mjs`, L-059). Over the real levels (`scripts/art/poncho.levels.test.mjs`) the brim or the tail beside his body may lie over a wall's face or a slope's sand, as the enemies' tails may (user, 2026-11-05: "Allow beside the body"), and the sombrero above his body may lie over a ceiling tile, as in 1-3's two-tile passage or a ceiling bumped in a jump (user, 2026-11-08: "Allow it, pin it"); nothing ever lies below his feet. The glide bar and the wall-cling marks keep their placeholders (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08).

### GDD-ART-05 Pixel rules

Status: Proposed

- **Outline:** every character, enemy, item and pickup has a near-black outline (#070509, as in the main reference, GDD-ART-02, which replaces the dark plum first proposed), 2 art pixels thick on the outside (one pixel of the user's drawing). Tiles and scenery have no outline, so what can be touched stands out. World 2's touchable world objects, the awnings, the cordeles' flags and the rollos, carry it too (Proposed, GDD-ART-06), while their posts, poles and lines do not.
- **Light** comes from the upper left. Each material has three shades: base, a shadow on the lower right and a highlight on the upper left. No soft gradients, no blurred or blended edges, no anti-aliasing.
- **Dither** (a checker of two colours) only in skies and far scenery, never on characters or pickups.
- **Glow:** the chile pickup's ring of warm glow and its heat wisps sit outside its outline, as a glow does; nothing else draws outside its outline (Proposed, GDD-ART-04).
- **Three layers:** far scenery (sky, mountains, clouds; low contrast, scrolls slower than the level), the play area (tiles, mid contrast) and characters and pickups (highest contrast, outlined).
- **Hazards** have a pale edge or tip (cream on a spike's points, a scorpion's sting): this is how the high-contrast hazard outlines of GDD-UI-03 are drawn, so danger never rests on colour alone. Hazard tiles get this edge although other tiles have no outline.
- **Silhouette first:** Poncho must be told apart by shape alone, the wide brim and tall crown above a poncho and a tail.
- Sprites are drawn facing right and flipped for left, but for Poncho's victory frames, front views that never flip, whose outline is the user's strip's, 4 art pixels (2 of the strip's pixels) thick in places (GDD-ART-04).

### GDD-ART-06 World moods

Status: Decided (a mood per world, darker towards Mictlán, user, 2026-10-25; World 1's blend, user, 2026-10-25; World 2's art designed without reference images and approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me", then "Approved", `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`). The other worlds' moods are Proposed, and so are World 2's details.

| # | World | Mood |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Desierto | **Decided (user, 2026-10-25): a blend.** The scenery of the user's desert scene (`assets/worlds/desierto/reference-scene.jpg`): blue and purple mountains far away, clouds, the cacti and plants of the Chihuahuan desert in place of the scene's saguaros (user, 2026-10-25: keep the Chihuahuan desert and swap in cacti that grow there): prickly pears (nopal), cholla, barrel cactus (biznaga), with palm yucca and ocotillo as the tall shapes (the species are Proposed), agave (lechuguilla) and red and orange flowers in front, a strip of grass along the sandy path, red earth below. Under a warmer golden sky than in that scene, with a bright sun. |
| 2 | Mercado | Early afternoon, still bright: pale blue sky, pink and terracotta walls in the first long shadows, papel picado strings in pink, gold, teal and blue. |
| 3 | Sierra | Cool afternoon: grey-blue sky, green-grey peaks, ochre rock. |
| 4 | Selva | Golden hour in the jungle: amber sky, deep greens, mossy ruins. |
| 5 | Cenote | Underground: dark teal, shafts of light from above, turquoise water. |
| 6 | Volcán | Dusk: red and black sky, a glowing orange crater. |
| 7 | Mictlán | Night: deep purple, a pale moon, marigold paths and candle light. |

Each world is a little darker than the one before, so the trip from World 1 to 7 goes from midday through the afternoon and dusk to night (the Cenote, underground, sits between golden hour and dusk). Proposed: the HUD and Poncho's palette stay the same in every world.

- **World 1's backdrop and ground (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: the backdrop and the ground first, from the desert scene alone; the look approved from screenshots the same day).** The levels of World 0 and World 1 (0-1, GDD-WLD-06, 1-1, GDD-WLD-07, 1-2, GDD-WLD-08, 1-3, GDD-WLD-09, the boss arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02, and the test levels, the sandbox and the slopes level, which count as World 1) are drawn in the desert look; the art of hazards, pickups and moving things comes later and keeps its placeholder look until then (but Don Zopilote, his cactus fruit and the arena's rocks, drawn in World 1's colours since 0.9.5, GDD-BOS-02, and World 1's four enemies, since 0.10.1, GDD-ENM-02; the plants are below). Drawn in code like Poncho's sprite (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` into `assets/sprites/desierto/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, with no outline on any of it (GDD-ART-05).
  - **Four far layers behind the level**, back to front (the fourth, the far desert strip in front of the mountains, is described under the plants below), each filling the screen's width and repeating sideways without a seam, never moving up or down:
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen. Four horizontal bands of gold, from a deep amber at the top to a pale gold at the horizon, with a two-colour checker dither, 2 art pixels tall, between each pair of bands (GDD-ART-05: dither only in skies and far scenery). The **sun** is a round pale disc 24 game px across with a gold ring and short pixel rays, its centre at x = 104, y = 36 on the screen, fixed with the sky.
    - **Clouds:** a few flat-bottomed clouds, cream on top and a warm shade underneath, between y = 16 and y = 88, scrolling at 0.1 of the camera's speed.
    - **Mountains:** two ranges of peaks between y = 92 and the screen's bottom, the far one purple, the near one blue with rust-red light on the slopes facing the upper left (GDD-ART-05) and a darker blue in shadow; below the peaks the blue fills down to the screen's bottom, so a pit shows mountains and not a hole. They scroll at 0.3 of the camera's speed.
    - A layer that scrolls at a share of the camera's speed is drawn at that share of the camera's scroll, rounded to a whole art pixel (GDD-UI-04).
  - **Ground tiles:** every solid tile of the level is a ground tile 64 x 64 art pixels. A tile with no solid tile and no sand slope directly above it (or at the level's top row) is a **top tile**: a strip of green grass along its top with a ragged lower edge and a few tufts, then pale sand, then red earth. Any other solid tile, but one under a sand slope (see Slope tiles), is an **earth tile**: red earth with darker specks. Each kind has two versions, so the ground does not look stamped: the version is (7 x column + 3 x row) mod 2, which alternates like a checkerboard. Light from the upper left: each grass tuft has its light green on the upper left and its shade on the right and below, and each sand speck has a pale cell on its left. The crumbling tiles, doors, fragile walls, piñatas, springs and the rest keep their own placeholder look.
  - **Slope tiles (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-02, "Extend the desert tiles": "The same approved sand and rock, cut along the slope by the same script, shown to you in screenshots before it lands."; the look approved from screenshots the same day: "Approved"):** a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09) and the tile under it are drawn as one 16 x 32 game px strip: the top tile over the earth tile of the same version, each one game px column moved down until its top cell is the cell the slope's surface passes through at the column's middle, then cut back into the two tiles. Above the surface the slope tile is empty, so the backdrop shows. So the grass strip, the sand and the red earth run along the slope at the top tile's thickness, in whole game pixels; at a slope's high end they meet the flat top tile beside it without a seam (checked by `scripts/art/desert.test.mjs`), and at its foot the surface starts one game px above the flat ground's. The tile under a slope takes the slope's version, so its part of the strip lines up. The ground sheet has 28 frames: the four tiles as before (frames 0 to 3, unchanged), then the six slopes in the order `/ \ r R F f`, two versions each (frame 4 + 2 x the slope's place in that order + the version), then the six tiles under them in the same order (frame 16 + 2 x place + the slope's version). Drawn by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` like the rest; `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
  - **Palette (20 colours, within GDD-ART-03's 24 for a world):** sky #E39A48, #EDB15A, #F4C66E, #F9DA8E; sun #FFF6D0, #FFD25A; clouds #FFF4DC, #E9B98A; mountains #8E6A96 (far purple), #A65B5B (rust light), #4D5A9C (blue), #3A4580 (blue shadow); grass #9CC24E, #6FA03A, #4E7A2C; sand #F4D29C, #E8B87A, #D19A5E; earth #A0472F, #7A3326. The four left are the plants' (below). `BACKDROP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/backdrop.ts`, `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
- **World 1's plants (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: drawn from the desert scene, the species it lacks from how they look, with a far plant layer too; the look approved from screenshots, 2026-10-27).** Scenery only: nothing touches them, they never hide a thing Poncho can touch, and they keep the backdrop's rules (drawn in code by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs`, no outline, blocky at whole game pixels, light from the upper left).
  - **Seven plants** (sizes in game px, width x height): nopal (prickly pear, round pads with a few red fruit; 16 x 16), cholla (a short branching cactus with pale spines; 12 x 20), biznaga (a barrel cactus, ribbed, with a crown of orange flowers; 10 x 10), palm yucca (a brown trunk with a spiky ball of leaves; 14 x 36), ocotillo (thin brown stems fanning up from one point, red tips; 16 x 40), agave (a blue-green rosette of pointed leaves; 16 x 12) and a flower clump (red and orange flowers on green stems; 12 x 8). No saguaros (GDD-WLD-02), and none has the cactus buddy's look of a short column with two raised arms (GDD-ENM-02), so scenery is never mistaken for an enemy.
  - **Where they grow:** on top tiles of the ground (GDD-ART-06 above), rooted at the tile's top in the middle of the tile, behind the ground tiles' front edge and behind everything else in the level (in front of the backdrop). A top tile (a level tile with no level tile and no sand slope directly above it, GDD-MOV-09) at (column, row) gets a plant when (37 x column + 11 x row) mod 9 is 0 or 1 (two top tiles in nine); its kind is the seven in the order above, chosen by (3 x column + 5 x row) mod 7, so neighbouring plants differ. 0-1 gets 12 plants of all seven kinds, the sandbox 4, the slopes level 1, a nopal at column 0, 1-2 11 of five kinds (no biznaga and no flower clump), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one, 1-3 16 of six kinds (no palm yucca), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one and none in a moving block's column, and the boss arena 1-4 gets 4, on the floor at columns 8, 9, 17 and 18 (a palm yucca, a flower clump, a biznaga and an agave), none on a pillar top, where the rocks rest (checked by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`). A top tile gets none when the tiles above it, up to the plant's height, are not all empty: a level tile, a fragile wall, a crumbling tile, a piñata or a door there stops it (so a plant never pokes into a wall or a platform, even one that breaks later), or when any of these stands within 2 columns of it on the same row or the row above: the spawn, an ofrenda, the exit, a spike, a spring, a door, a key, a piñata, a taco, the veladora, or an enemy's start. That keeps the spots that teach or warn clear, and keeps cacti away from the spikes and the cactus buddy. `src/ui/plants.ts`.
  - **Far plants:** a strip of far desert between the mountains and the level, from y = 180 to y = 224 on the screen, scrolling at 0.5 of the camera's speed like the other far layers and repeating sideways without a seam. It is sand in its two darker shades with a ragged top, dotted with small plants 4 to 10 px tall in the plants' shapes and colours, drawn flat (two shades each). Below y = 224, the floor's top in every level so far, the mountains' blue foot still shows, so a pit still reads as a hole.
  - **The palette's last four colours:** #D8473A (red: flowers, fruit, ocotillo tips), #F08A3C (orange: flowers), #6F9C8E (agave and yucca blue-green), #2F5A3A (deep green: the cacti's shade). The plants and the far strip use only the 24 colours of this palette. `BACKDROP.desertFactor` (0.5), `BACKDROP.desertTop` (180) in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
- **World 2's backdrop, ground and decor (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me": no reference images, the art designed from World 2's mood line above, following the art study's recommended defaults, and approved from screenshots, which are pending).** World 2's levels (2-1 to 2-3, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, La Estrella's arena 2-4, GDD-BOS-03, and World 2's labs `lab-2m` and `lab-2e`) are drawn in the market look; World 0, World 1 and World 1's test levels keep the desert (`src/ui/scenery/looks.test.ts`). The idea: what Poncho cannot touch is cool, pale and soft (the sky, a lilac hill town far off, the rooftops in the middle distance); what he can touch is warm, saturated and crisp (the terracotta roofs he runs on, the market's things); and what he touches in play, the awnings and papel picado among it, carries the near-black outline. Drawn in code like World 1's (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/make-mercado.mjs` into `assets/sprites/mercado/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, light from the upper left, no outline on the scenery or the tiles (GDD-ART-05); `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs` checks that the committed images are the ones the script makes, their sizes, the palette and the rules below. The game puts each world's look together from its numbers (`src/ui/scenery/looks.ts`, `src/ui/scenery/scenery.ts`) and loads only the files of the level's world (`src/ui/scenery/files.ts`, GDD-TEC-03), and the awning, cordel and rollo sheets only in a level that has them (`src/ui/MarketThings.ts`); `e2e/world2.spec.ts` checks it in the browser.
  - **Palette (24 colours, GDD-ART-03's budget for a world, none of them World 1's):** sky #86BEE0 (top), #A9D3EA (middle), #CFE8F1 (horizon); far town #C3C6DE (lit), #9AA6CD (base), #7C86B5 (shade); mid rooftops #E6C2B8 (rose), #C99A9E (rose shade), #E4CC9E (ochre), #4E4A78 (the street, in deep shade); tejas (roof tiles) #E07A52 (lit), #C45E3E, #8A3B30 (dark); plaster #F2B0A6 (lit), #E07F8E, #B85E6E (shade); talavera #1C3F9E (cobalt), #F4EFE2 (glaze white), #E9B53F (yellow); the market #D9327E (rosa mexicano), #2AA198 (teal), #4F8F4C (leaf), #3B2B3C (wrought iron), #7E5A3E (wood). The mood line's papel picado is #D9327E, #E9B53F, #2AA198 and #1C3F9E. Only the awnings, the cordeles' flags and the rollos add an outline, Poncho's #070509 (below). No colour of the ground or the decor is within ΔE 12 of Poncho's (checked by `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs`).
  - **Layers behind the level**, back to front, each filling the screen's width, repeating sideways every 480 game px without a seam and never moving up or down; they scroll at World 1's shares of the camera's speed (Proposed; `e2e/world2.spec.ts`):
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen. Three bands, lighter towards the horizon: #86BEE0 from y = 0 to 60, #A9D3EA to 110 and #CFE8F1 below (the camera's background colour), with a two-colour checker dither 2 art pixels tall at each border (`sky-strips.png`; the flat bands are drawn by the game). No sun: early in the afternoon it is above the frame, and the light still comes from the upper left.
    - **Clouds:** three small flat-bottomed clouds, glaze white on top and horizon blue underneath and on their lower right, about 35 x 12, 47 x 16 and 28 x 10 game px, centred near x = 90, 250 and 410 between y = 20 and 58, none touching the strip's sides; one image each (`cloud-0.png` to `cloud-2.png`), scrolling at 0.1.
    - **Far town** (`far-town.png`, 480 x 116 game px, y = 84 to 200, scrolling at 0.3): a lilac hill whose ridge rises from about y = 170 at the strip's sides to a shoulder at y = 140 (x = 122 to 178) and a crest at y = 128 (x = 331 to 399), its slopes that face the upper left lit in streaks; small houses in terraces climbing it, fronts lit and right sides in shade, clear of the ridge above them; a parish church on the shoulder (x = 129 to 172: a nave, a bell tower in three tiers with bell openings and a cross whose tip, at y = 84, is the layer's highest pixel, and a ribbed talavera dome with a glint) and a small chapel with its bell gable on the crest (x = 358 to 372). The far colours only, with the horizon blue for the dome's glint, and a checker into shade at the foot.
    - **Mid rooftops** (`mid-roofs.png`, 480 x 76 game px, y = 148 to 224, scrolling at 0.5; its bottom is the floor's top, row 14, as in every level): eleven flat-roofed blocks (*azoteas*) with roof lines between y = 164 and 190, fronts in rose and ochre with broken 1 px parapets and small dark windows with sills; a block taller than its right-hand neighbour shows its right side in shade and casts a 45° stair-step shadow across that neighbour's front, "the first long shadows" of the mood line; four *tinacos* (plain water tanks, no logo), two washing lines, two rooftop huts with arched doorways, the market hall (the *mercado municipal*, x = 300 to 400: its barrel vault's top at y = 170, a row of clerestory windows and one big arched entrance) and two muted papel picado strings, half the size of the playfield's and without outline. Its last six rows go into the street's dark through a checker.
    - **The foot:** below the far band the flat foot is the street's #4E4A78, drawn flat by the game; the mid layer covers it down to y = 224, so a pit between two roofs shows a dark street far below and reads as a drop.
    - **The arena's plaza** (`plaza.png`, the size and place of the rooftops): in La Estrella's arena 2-4 the mid layer is a plaza instead: a kiosco (the bandstand: a raised platform with railings, thin columns, a scalloped eave and a ribbed dome with a lantern) in the middle, x = 202 to 278, arcaded *portales* round it, and papel picado strung from the kiosco to them, in the mid layer's colours.
  - **Ground tiles** (`ground.png`, 28 frames of 64 x 64 art pixels in World 1's layout, so a tile's frame is chosen as there): a **top tile** is a roof's eave: the walk line (one unbroken row of #E07A52), the tejas' channels and round ends, a scalloped shadow under them, the eave's shadow on the wall, a talavera band (cobalt borders round cobalt diamonds with yellow centres on glaze white) and pink plaster. Every pattern repeats every 4 columns, so tiles meet without a seam, and the walk line and the talavera band are the same in both versions (a glint on one teja in version 0, a chipped tile end in version 1). A **wall tile** is pink plaster with specks, in version 0 with a chip that shows two courses of adobe bricks, in version 1 with a hairline crack. The six slope frames and the tiles under them are the top tile over the wall tile cut along the slope, as World 1's (Slope tiles, above): the tejas step down a pitched roof.
  - **Decor** (`decor.png`, seven frames of 16 x 40 game px, the frame of World 1's plants; scenery under the plants' rules): the market's things, in this order of kinds: a talavera pot with geraniums (10 x 12), a bougainvillea in a terracotta planter (16 x 20), a crate heaped with oranges and limes (14 x 10, never a loose round fruit, which would read as a hilo), three clay pots (14 x 14), an unlit wrought-iron lamp post (8 x 40), a wooden rack with three folded rebozos (16 x 24) and a lotería card of our own, a sun, on an easel (12 x 16, no title and no number). None looks like something that matters in play: no piñatas, cacti, striped canvas, papel picado, cats or birds. **Where they stand** is World 1's plant rule (above), with the decor's own heights: the same formula for the spots and the kinds, two top tiles in nine, the same clearances, with two more that change nothing in World 1, which has none of these things (built, the main session's reading, 2026-11-04, Proposed): an awning, a cordel or a rollo (its whole line, rolled up or not) blocks the decor in every tile its top crosses, like a level tile, so no item stands under one within its height; and no item stands below one within 2 columns of either of its end columns, where its poles stand. On the real levels the rule gives 2-1 20 items, 2-2 22 and 2-3 20, each of all seven kinds, and the arena 2-4 1, a talavera pot (`plantSpots` in `src/ui/plants.ts`, measured by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`).
  - **The touchable world objects** carry the outline (Proposed, GDD-ART-05). The **awning** (`awning.png`, GDD-WLD-10): frames of 16 x 16 game px in three columns, the left end, a middle piece that repeats and the right end, so any awning from 2 to 6 tiles wide is put together from them; its rows are the whole awning at rest and sagging 2, 4 and 6 px, the torn one at rest and sagging, the torn halves, and the posts. Rosa mexicano and white stripes 4 px wide, starting with rosa at the left end, under a white top row, 6 px of canvas over a valance of scallops, 8 px in all, the outline drawn inside its two ends so that the drawing is exactly as wide as what Poncho lands on; the torn one faded to #E07F8E with a teal and a yellow patch and gaps in its valance; the halves and the 1 px iron posts are scenery, without outline. The **cordel** (`cordel.png`, GDD-WLD-11): frames of 24 x 16 game px, a tile of line with 4 px on each side for the flags: a 2 px line of iron twisted with wood and two outlined flags, pink and gold or teal and blue, so a cordel alternates them by tile, at rest and swayed 1 px either way; then each post's top and its pole, which repeats downwards. The **rollo** (`rollo.png`, GDD-WLD-11): frames of 24 x 24 game px, a roll of the pink paper with its wound flags' spiral on its end, tied with the cord, outlined, at rest, swayed 1 px and flapping open, by a left post and by a right post.
  - **Files:** `sky-strips.png` (1920 x 4 art pixels), `cloud-0.png` to `cloud-2.png`, `far-town.png` (1920 x 464), `mid-roofs.png` and `plaza.png` (1920 x 304 each), `ground.png` (1792 x 64), `decor.png` (448 x 160), `awning.png` (192 x 640), `cordel.png` (960 x 64) and `rollo.png` (576 x 96), about 71 KB in all; only the files of the world being played are loaded (GDD-TEC-03).
  - **`MERCADO` in `src/config/tuning.ts`:** `cloudFactor` 0.1, `farFactor` 0.3, `midFactor` 0.5, `skyBands` #86BEE0, #A9D3EA and #CFE8F1, `skyEnds` 240 and 440 art px (y = 60 and 110), `stripArt` 2, `clouds` with their top left at (72, 20), (227, 20) and (396, 48), `farTop` 84, `farBandArt` 464, `midTop` 148, `midBandArt` 304, `footColour` #4E4A78, and `decor`, the seven kinds' sizes above (`maceta` 10 x 12, `bugambilia` 16 x 20, `huacal` 14 x 10, `ollas` 14 x 14, `farol` 8 x 40, `rebozos` 16 x 24, `loteria` 12 x 16); checked by `src/ui/scenery/looks.test.ts`.
  - **Not drawn yet:** the final sprites of World 2's enemies and La Estrella, which the game draws as placeholder shapes in World 2's palette (GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03); 2-3's chimneys and high roofs of their own and the star piñata in its background (GDD-WLD-14, Open Question 32); and World 2's other tiles and things, which keep their game-wide placeholders (the fragile wall's pink is close to the plaster's, Open Question 24; no World 2 level has a fragile wall).

### GDD-ART-07 UI look

Status: Proposed

- **Font:** a blocky pixel font drawn in code like the art (GDD-ART-01), cream text (#F1F3E3) with a dark plum outline, so it reads on any background. Gameplay text stays readable at the sizes GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 give.
- **Serape band:** menus, the title screen and the pause screen are framed by a woven band in Poncho's blue, red and cream triangles.
- **Lives triangles** (GDD-UI-01): filled in Poncho's red with a cream edge; a lost one is only the cream edge.
- **Title:** "PONCHO" in large cream letters with a red shadow, "Siete Vidas" in gold, Poncho beside it. The layout, sizes and menus stay those of GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02; only the look changes.
- **Not built yet:** the HUD, menus and title still use the placeholder look described in GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 (plain text, outlined triangles), until the art switch (GDD-ART-01).

---

## 14. Technical

### GDD-TEC-01 Stack

Status: Decided (user, 2026-10-19: state of the art, latest releases). See `docs/decisions/0002-state-of-the-art-stack.md`.

Phaser 4, TypeScript 7 (native compiler, strict), Vite 8, Vitest 5 for unit tests, Playwright for end-to-end playtests, Oxlint with type-aware rules for linting, Node 24 LTS in CI. Exact versions are in the ADR and `package-lock.json`. The first decision (Phaser 3) is superseded.

### GDD-TEC-02 Deterministic simulation

Status: Proposed

- Fixed timestep of 60 Hz for gameplay logic; rendering interpolates.
- Simulation code (movement, lives, damage, collision) has no Phaser imports and no `Math.random()` without a seeded source, so it is unit-testable and replayable.
- Scene code only wires input, rendering and audio to the simulation.

### GDD-TEC-03 Performance and mobile targets

Status: Proposed

- 60 fps on a mid-range phone from the last 4 years, 30 fps floor on low end with automatic quality fallback.
- Initial download under 5 MB for the first level; later assets lazy-loaded. The 1920 x 1080 art (GDD-ART-01) is drawn in code and saved as images: the first world's images must fit this budget too.
- Works offline once loaded (service worker, PWA installable).
- Handles device pixel ratio, safe areas, orientation change and tab suspension.

### GDD-TEC-04 Level authoring

Status: Proposed

Levels are authored in Tiled and exported as JSON (tilemap and object layers: spawn, ofrendas, hilos, enemies, hazards, exit). A validator test loads every level and checks: exactly one spawn, 3 ofrendas, an exit (a boss level: neither, GDD-BOS-01), no gap wider than the world's limit (GDD-WLD-03).

- The validator is built (`src/levels/validate.ts`) and a test runs it over every level in `src/levels/index.ts`: 0-1 (GDD-WLD-06), 1-1 (GDD-WLD-07), 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08), 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09), the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12), 2-2 (GDD-WLD-13), 2-3 (GDD-WLD-14), La Estrella's arena 2-4 (GDD-BOS-03), the sandbox and the slopes level (GDD-MOV-09), and World 2's two test levels, the Mercado lab `lab-2m` (an awning ladder, a torn awning, a cordel staircase, a rollo bridge, an awning over a pit and a far-post rollo, `src/levels/lab2m.test.ts`) and the enemy lab `lab-2e` (a Paloma, a Chapulín and a Trompo in pens, `src/levels/lab2e.test.ts`), each two screens wide and opened by its address; they add no world to the save (GDD-PRG-02). The Tiled JSON loader is not built yet: levels are still written in code, and the loader will feed the same validator (user decision, 2026-10-23). One spawn is guaranteed by the level data, which has exactly one; the loader will count spawn objects.
- It checks 3 ofrendas, an exit, a length of 3 screens to the world's longest, 6 in Worlds 0 and 1 and 8 from World 2 on (GDD-WLD-03; a world with no length limit fails, like one with no gap limit), no gap wider than the world's limit, and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn or an ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01). It also checks that every sand slope tile has a level tile directly under it, and holds no spike, spring, crumbling tile, door, fragile wall or piñata (GDD-MOV-09). A world with no gap limit in GDD-WLD-03 is itself a problem; World 1 and the tutorial (World 0, with World 1's 3 tiles) have one, and World 2 has 4 tiles (Proposed, GDD-WLD-03, Open Question 13); Worlds 3 to 7 have none yet.
- **World 2 (built, Proposed):** the validator holds World 2's levels (2-1 to 2-3, and the arena 2-4 under the boss-level rule, GDD-BOS-03) to its gap limit of 4 tiles and the covered-pit rule (GDD-WLD-03, `openGaps`): a gap's width counts only its uncovered pit columns, those with no whole awning, cordel or rollo spanning the column with its top at or above the ground at the pit's nearer edge and at most 3 tiles above it; a torn awning covers nothing. It also checks that an awning's top is no higher than y = 160 (`AWNING.minTopY`, GDD-WLD-10), that no awning (8 px thick, `AWNING.height`) and no cordel or rollo line (2 px, `ROLLO.linePx`) overlaps a level tile, that the 3-tile rule holds for the Paloma, the Chapulín and the Trompo, and that La Estrella's start in each of her three phases is at least 3 tiles from the spawn (GDD-BOS-01). Its own tests (`src/levels/validate.test.ts`) have a level that breaks each part (L-028). That no enemy starts on an awning, and that no awning or cordel stands over a sand slope, are left to each level's tests (World 2's levels have no slopes).
- A gap is a run of adjacent pit columns, and a pit column is one whose bottom row is empty, where Poncho falls out of the level. A slope tile never sits in the bottom row (it needs a level tile under it), so a slope beside a gap does not change its width. A floating tile higher up does not fill a pit (user decision, 2026-10-23), and a column whose bottom row alone is empty counts, on the safe side.
- A level marked as a test level (the sandbox, the slopes level, `lab-2m`, `lab-2e`) skips the exit and length checks and gets the others (user decision, 2026-10-23), the slope checks included (GDD-MOV-09). The sandbox's pit, columns 14 to 16, is a gap of 3 tiles.
- A level with a boss (the arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02, and La Estrella's 2-4, GDD-BOS-03) is a boss level, held to a rule of its own (built, Proposed): instead of 3 ofrendas, an exit and 3 to 6 screens, it must have no ofrendas and no exit and be exactly one screen (30 tiles) wide, and the boss's starting point must be at least 3 tiles from the spawn, the arena's only respawn (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-ENM-01). It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn (checked by `src/levels/validate.test.ts` with a boss level that breaks each), and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).

### GDD-TEC-05 Tuning in one place

Status: Proposed

All gameplay numbers in this document live in a single typed file, `src/config/tuning.ts`, each constant commented with its GDD ID. A unit test simulates a full jump and asserts the derived values stated in section 4: peak 56 px within 1.5 px, and horizontal distance 66 px at full run within one step of travel (2 px).

### GDD-TEC-07 Dependency policy

Status: Decided (use the latest stable releases, user, 2026-10-19). The review cadence and the holdback rule are Proposed.

- Track the latest stable release of every dependency. No pre-release versions in `main`.
- At every GDD minor version bump, run `npm outdated`, upgrade, run the full gate and the playtests, and record the result in the changelog or an ADR.
- Anything that must stay behind (a blocker like a peer-dependency conflict) is written down in an ADR with the condition for upgrading.
- Phaser APIs marked deprecated (the Canvas renderer, custom pipelines) are not used.

### GDD-TEC-06 Testing strategy

Status: Proposed

- Unit tests (Vitest) for simulation: movement numbers, forgiveness windows, lives rules, save migration.
- Level validation tests (GDD-TEC-04): every level in `src/levels/index.ts` passes the validator.
- Playwright playtests per the `harness-playtest` skill: boot, jump, life loss, touch input, console free of errors.
- `scripts/check.mjs` runs all of these through npm scripts once they exist.

---

## 15. Scope

### GDD-MVP-01 Vertical slice

Status: Proposed

The first playable milestone:

- Poncho with run, jump (GDD-MOV-01 to GDD-MOV-06), stomp and claw (GDD-CMB-01 to GDD-CMB-03).
- Seven-lives system complete (GDD-LIFE-01 to GDD-LIFE-05) and HUD (GDD-UI-01).
- Power-ups: Taco, Chile and Fuego, piñatas (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-PWR-05, GDD-PWR-06).
- World 1 with 3 levels and the boss, bestiary of GDD-ENM-02.
- Keyboard and touch controls (GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03), including the Fuego button; the gamepad (GDD-CTL-02) is built ahead of it.
- Autosave (GDD-PRG-02), pause, settings with volume and language.
- Placeholder music and SFX are acceptable.

Done means: the `harness-playtest` skill passes on desktop and a phone viewport, and the user has played it.

### GDD-MVP-02 Later scope

Status: Proposed

Worlds 2 to 7 (World 2, Mercado, is built: GDD-WLD-10 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03; its playtests and the user's approval of its screenshots are pending), Chile Fantasma (GDD-PWR-04), glide, wall cling, further powers, score, cosmetics shop, full soundtrack, localisation beyond English, Spanish and German, native packaging (new ADR).

The glide and the wall cling are built early but stay off in real levels; only the sandbox turns them on (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08).

## Open Questions

Answer these to move items from Proposed to Decided. Until then the Proposed text stands as the working assumption.

1. **Taco as shield (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-CMB-03):** Taco and Fuego absorb hits like Mario's mushroom and flower. This makes lives less scarce. Right balance, or should power-ups be rarer or not absorb hits?
2. **Power-up set (section 6):** Taco, Chile (Fuego fireballs), Chile Fantasma (star), piñata blocks. Anything you want added, such as a different fireball style, a reserve slot, or other foods?
3. **Seven worlds (GDD-WLD-02):** are the world themes and names right? Anything you want, or want avoided? (World 1 is answered: the Chihuahuan desert, user, 2026-10-25.)
4. **Story (GDD-VIS-01):** is the curse-and-seven-lives story seed what you want, or a different premise?
5. **Pronouns (GDD-ART-02):** answered: he (user, 2026-10-25).
6. **Brand and chain scope (GDD-VIS-05):** the draft keeps the game free of wallets, tokens and on-chain features. Is that right? Should the game mention the Poncho on Base token or community anywhere (credits, links), and who owns the final brand rules? Note that app stores restrict crypto features, which affects distribution (question 11).
7. **Combat (GDD-CMB-02):** keep both claw swipe and stomp, with fireballs on top? Partly answered (user, 2026-10-21): the claw swipe is a standard mechanic that Poncho always has, not a power-up. Stomp and fireballs alongside it are still open.
8. **Difficulty (GDD-VIS-02):** is assist mode and a kid-friendly tone right, or do you want a harder game?
9. **Audio (GDD-AUD-01):** will you provide music, or should placeholders be generated and licensed assets chosen?
10. **Score (GDD-PRG-03):** keep or cut? Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30).
11. **Distribution:** where will it be hosted (itch.io, own site, stores)? This drives PWA versus native packaging.
12. **Taco pickups in Taco (GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-06):** a taco lying on the floor is left where it is when Poncho is already Taco, but a walking taco released from a piñata is used up when he touches it. Should both behave the same?
13. **Gap limits after World 1 (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04):** World 1 has one (3 tiles), and World 2's is built at 4 tiles (Proposed), which needs a run-up, with a covered-pit rule: pit columns under a whole awning, a cordel or a rollo, within 3 tiles above the pit's nearer edge, do not count, and no uncovered run may be wider than 4. The level validator fails a level in any other world until that world has a limit. Is 4 right for World 2 (another way: keep 3 everywhere and let only awnings and rollos make wider pits)? Should the limit grow by world, up to the 5 tiles a running jump can cross?
14. **Detail on phones (GDD-ART-01):** at 1920 x 1080, one art pixel is less than one screen pixel on most phones, so the finest detail blurs there. Fine as it is, or should the sprites keep their key details (eye, triangles) at least 2 art pixels wide?
15. **Secrets (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-06 to GDD-WLD-09, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14):** tiles cannot hide anything yet, so the veladoras of 0-1, 1-1, 1-2 and 1-3 are off the path but in plain view, and World 2's, in 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3, are in plain view too, but high, each reached by a tier 3 rebound (2-2's floats in the top row; the sky above it stays unused: the camera does not follow him up there). Fine for now, or should a secret be truly hidden (a false wall, a room off the screen's top)?
16. **Short phone windows (GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-03):** on a landscape phone whose browser window is under 360 CSS px tall (a browser bar showing), the menu rows grow so far that the last row of the title menu and of the results menu runs off the bottom of the screen (found in the review of PR #62). Since the title menu moved up to 112 px (0.8.7) its four rows fit down to about 340 CSS px; the results menu's do not. The settings screen's six rows (0.8.10, from 62 px) fit down to about 381 CSS px; five rows from 80 px fitted down to about 341, four from 96 px to about 302. Shrink the rows there, move the menus up (into the medal line on the results screen), or accept it?
17. **What 1-2 brings back (GDD-WLD-08):** besides its new things it has one cactus buddy and two piñatas from 1-1, and no Alacrán or Zopilote; its secret veladora is reached by the spring's timed launch, which no level teaches (only the sandbox has it), or by a full jump left from the top of the 4-tile wall the plain launch throws Poncho onto. Right, or should 1-2 bring back more of 1-1's enemies, or move the veladora so that it asks for the timed launch alone?
18. **The details of World 1's run and lives (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03):** built as decided (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided", "Save the lives", "Lives at the clear", "Keep them", "Lives lost in the level", "Follow the run"), with these Proposed details. (a) The save writes World 1's run only at a clear (the lives at the exit and the next level) and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (0 lives and 1-1), so quitting a level, or closing the game, and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear, the lives lost in it given back (another way: write the lives at every life lost or won, which keeps the losses but lets a player who quits after an ofrenda win its life again on every new start). (b) An address (`?level=…`) always starts with seven. (c) "Start" plays 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven, and once the run has cleared World 1's last level a new run starts there (another way: the last level cleared, the boss arena 1-4, again); partly answered (user, 2026-11-04: "Latest world"): "Start" goes on to World 2's run after 1-4's clear (built), and World 2 works the same way, 2-1 always with seven and a new run after 2-4. (d) A save from before the run was kept goes by the levels cleared: one with 1-1 cleared opens on 1-2 with seven. (e) Gold counts the lives lost as the lives the level started with less those at its exit, so a life won back at an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (that keeps "4 lives at the exit" for a level started with seven); another way: count every life lost, won-back lives not helping. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from seven. (f) "Retry" on the tutorial 0-1's results menu starts it with seven, since its lives carry nowhere (another way: the lives at its clear, as "Lives at the clear" gives a World 1 level). (g) Out of lives in 1-1 the restart stays in the same scene, where a Left or Right key or a finger held through the press moves Poncho as soon as the flip ends, as before; out of lives in 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4 the restart in 1-1 starts as the next level does, where a button held through the press does nothing until it is lifted (GDD-UI-02). Right?
19. **The pillar tops in Don Zopilote's arena (GDD-BOS-02):** in 1-4 nothing of his reaches Poncho on a pillar top in any phase, except on a lip of about 5 px at each pillar's inner edge, where the dive or the drop beside that pillar clips him. Fine for World 1's boss (the lip a warning not to crowd the edge), or should the whole top be safe (his lap's ends a few px further in), or should a dive or a fruit reach the tops?
20. **His fruit's telegraph (GDD-ENM-01, GDD-BOS-02):** a cactus fruit has no 0.5 s telegraph of its own; it falls in view for 0.7 to 0.9 s before it reaches Poncho on the floor. Is the fall its telegraph, or should Don Zopilote flash or call out before each drop?
21. **Hilos in the boss arena (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-UI-01, GDD-UI-02):** 1-4 has none, so the HUD counter reads "0/0" and the level clear screen "Hilos 0/0", and so does La Estrella's 2-4, which has none either (GDD-BOS-03). Hide both in a boss level, or give the arenas some hilos?
22. **Awnings (GDD-WLD-10):** the hold ladder is decided (user, 2026-11-04: "Hold ladder"). Its details are proposed: three tiers at 480, 540 and 600 px/s, reusing the spring's two launches with one between (another way: two tiers, 480 and 600); and a plain landing settles him after at most one small hop, so he can stand on an awning (another way: awnings always bounce, pure trampolines). Right? They are built that way; the build's own details (a late press up to 6 steps after a plain landing, a torn awning's plain rebound of at least 180 px/s, what counts as held) are in GDD-WLD-10.
23. **Papel picado (GDD-WLD-11):** cordeles are one-way floors that never move or bounce; a rollo is unrolled into a bridge by the claw or a fireball; the claw does not cut cordeles. Right, or should the claw cut cordeles (like the fragile walls, but a swipe at an enemy could cut the next bridge), or should cordeles be scenery only?
24. **The fragile wall in World 2 (GDD-WLD-05, GDD-ART-06):** the game-wide fragile wall is a pink papel picado tile, close in hue to World 2's pink plaster walls and in theme to its hanging strings. Recolour it game-wide to rosa mexicano with a white scalloped edge and the outline when its final art is drawn, and check it in World 2's screenshots, or something else?
25. **World 2's look (GDD-ART-06):** designed without reference images (user, 2026-11-04: "Design it, show me"), on the art study's defaults: a parish church with a tiled dome and a hilltop chapel on the skyline (no real town copied), no sun, roofs of terracotta tejas over a talavera band over pink plaster, awnings in rosa mexicano and white only (other colourways later, always with white stripes and scallops), an outline on the awnings and papel picado flags as on characters, World 1's parallax speeds, no decorative piñatas, and a plaza with a kiosco behind La Estrella's arena. They are in the game now, and the user approved them from screenshots (2026-11-04: "Approved", `docs/screenshots/world2-mercado/`); any of these defaults can still be changed.
26. **World 2's names and tone (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03, GDD-VIS-04):** the levels "El Tianguis" (2-1), "Mercado Municipal" (2-2), "Azoteas" (2-3) and "La Plaza" (2-4); the enemies' names (Paloma, Chapulín, Trompo, and the Zanate later); defeated birds fly off in a puff of feathers and toys tumble away, nothing shown dying; and the guardrails: animals of the town and animated craft objects only, no people or vendors, no rats, cockroaches or flies, no brand names, lotería pictures of our own without titles. Right?
27. **The glide after World 2 (GDD-PRG-01, GDD-MOV-07):** its unlock is deferred past World 2's first round. When it is built, does the glide work in every level once unlocked, replays of World 1 and World 2 included ("permanent means permanent", with no route or medal needing it), or only from World 3 on?
28. **Level 2-1's near misses and its Palomas (GDD-WLD-12, GDD-ENM-03):** (a) on the ladder awning a tier 1 rebound peaks 2.9 px under the first rooftop's top, so only the chain gets him up; tight, but deterministic. Fine, or a wider miss so it reads as "not this one"? (b) The "wait" answer to a Paloma means waiting for its hover and walking under it; waiting out of its sight does not get Poncho past one, since any approach startles it again (GDD-ENM-03's rule as written). Right? (c) One Paloma on a rooftop (the first rooftop's, an easier copy of the pit's twist over a floor) stands for the plan's "Palomas on the rooftops between awnings": enough, or a second? (d) The pit awning is 3 tiles wide in an 8-tile pit, 3 open tiles before it and 2 after (the main session's choice, 2026-11-04: a 2-tile awning let a plain landing carry him off its far end, and a 9-tile pit made nearly every held crossing fail). Keep?
29. **Level 2-2's beats (GDD-WLD-13):** (a) its first "pit" is a sunken aisle he cannot die in, with a step back up, so the first rollo cannot be failed, and the first lethal pit comes after the first ofrenda. Keep? (b) The twist that replaces the Zanate's beat: the first chile of World 2 from a piñata, and its fireball unrolling a bundle the claw cannot reach, with the spikes below costing only the power in Taco or Fuego, so it is never a dead end. Keep, or another twist (a bundle clawed in mid-air from an awning bounce, which its last act already does)? (c) The veladora floats in the top row with nothing to stand on beside it, at the top of a column of hilos. Fine, or should it sit on something?
30. **Every rollo needs a hop (GDD-WLD-11):** a bundle hangs under its line and the claw's box ends at his feet, so a line whose bundle the claw reaches from the ground is always at least 8 px above his feet, and he hops up onto it ("claw, then hop") instead of walking straight on. Fine as a rule, or should a line at most 8 px up be stepped onto?
31. **Claw and jump together (GDD-WLD-11, GDD-CMB-02):** a jump pressed during the claw's 2-step wind-up lifts the swing over a bundle at his feet, so the claw and the hop must be two beats. Is a forgiveness wanted (the bundle counted for a swing that started level with it)?
32. **Level 2-3's choices and art (GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ART-06):** (a) the plan's Ten gave the optional fireball route to the far rollo over the alley; the layout gives it to a second far-post rollo over the Fuego pit, before the alley (the alley's rollo can be unrolled by a fireball spat from an awning rebound too, not measured). Keep, or move the fireball route onto the alley's rollo? (b) The chimneys' 1-tile tops let a careful player perch between the torn awnings, a slower way than the chain. Accept, or make them unstandable? (c) Its rooftops use World 2's ground tiles; should the chimneys (1 tile wide, 4 tall) and the high roofs have rooftop pieces of their own, and the giant star piñata planned for the background over its exit, La Estrella foreshadowed, be drawn now?
33. **La Estrella's build choices (GDD-BOS-03):** made by the main session during the build (2026-11-04), each Proposed. (a) She can be hit only while she glides, telegraphs, hangs or is yanked, never while she drops, is thrown up by an awning, rests on the floor or rises from it, so that, as you said, she is reached only from awning rebounds and cordel jumps, never from the floor. (b) In phase 3 her drop tears straight through the awnings, which stay whole, to the floor, so standing under an awning is no shelter (the plan had the awning throw her up there too). (c) Her telegraph shakes her body and her trolley 2 px sideways with the red flash, since her rope has no length at her high position to zigzag. (d) Gliding high she can pass behind the boss bar, which is drawn on top of her, and her top cone can reach past the screen's top. (e) A cordel jump cannot bump her at her phase 1 low height, since standing on the cordel his head is already above her bottom (the plan's reach table had not looked at it; a jump from the awning does). Right?
34. **World 2's contrast on screen (GDD-ART-06, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-UI-01):** answered (user, 2026-11-04): the Chapulín's leaf green is darkened to #3E7A3C, 18 L* from the pink walls ("Darken it"), and the boss bar's name gets a 2 px dark outline in both worlds ("Dark outline").
